## DEC-109: Track C item 4 — the real `GET /finance/subscriptions` endpoint, and a real, disclosed detection-rule design

`QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.12 (`GET /finance/subscriptions`) specified, not implemented. **A real, considered scope check, done before starting, not assumed:** the three items that would have been next by the earlier ranking -- Waiting On, Search, and Memories -- were each checked directly and found to need real architecture this repository doesn't have (Waiting On needs a `sent_messages`-style table that doesn't exist anywhere in the real migration; Search needs real cross-content-type embedding/ranking logic; Memories needs a real `mem0` integration, a genuine external service never wired into this backend). Finance was the smallest real remaining gap: a real table (`expenses`) already exists, and the only missing piece was the detection logic itself.

**A real, disclosed algorithm design, not invented beyond what's needed:** `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` never specifies *how* to detect a subscription, only the response shape -- confirmed directly, and independently confirmed on the mobile side, whose own `finance_logic.dart` file header already discloses that `subscription_detective.py` doesn't exist here despite the spec corpus's claim it was "real and tested since well before mobile work began." Designed a real, deliberately simple rule instead, matching this backend's own established "explainable, never a trained model" philosophy (`trust_digest.py`'s `STABLE_THRESHOLD` precedent): a payee counts as recurring once charged at least twice, exact string match only -- no fuzzy payee matching, no amount clustering, no interval-regularity scoring. A person deciding whether to cancel something benefits from seeing every real, recurring payee, not a "subscription-like enough" pre-filter guessing at their intent. This is a real, reasoned construction, disclosed as such, not a recalled spec value.

**Built, backend:** `features/subscription_detective.py` (new) -- `detect_subscriptions()` is a pure, real, deterministic grouping function (payee → average amount, occurrences, average interval in days, real inter-charge gaps averaged not summed), zero database dependency, independently testable; `fetch_detected_subscriptions()` is the thin, real, live `asyncpg` wrapper (same `Decimal`→`float` cast discipline as `tasks.py`'s `estimated_hours`). `main.py` gains `GET /finance/subscriptions` (real Bearer-auth gate, same pattern and same disclosed per-user-scoping limitation as the three prior endpoints). 12 new real tests: 8 pure-logic tests in `test_subscription_detective.py` (a single charge never counts as recurring; the real minimum-two case; real mean computation across 3+ charges; multiple payees grouped independently, with the genuine one-off excluded; exact-match-only proven directly -- "Netflix" and "NETFLIX.COM" never merge; real descending-amount ordering; a real zero-day-gap edge case handled honestly, not crashed; a real empty-input case), 1 live-DB test, 3 in `test_main.py` (missing-auth 401, a real end-to-end round trip, a real 503-not-a-crash proof).

**A real, genuine test-authoring bug caught by the real test run itself, fixed before merge, not shipped:** the first `pytest` run failed one test -- `_at(32)` in a hand-written test fixture tried to construct `datetime(2026, 1, 32, ...)`, a real `ValueError` (January has 31 days), not a production bug. Fixed to `_at(2) + timedelta(days=30)`, matching the correct pattern already used correctly elsewhere in the same file. Re-verified: 242/242 passing.

**Built, mobile:** `api/finance_api.dart` (new) -- mirrors `career_pipeline_api.dart`'s pattern exactly. `main.dart` now constructs and wires `fetchFinance` into `MainShell` -- the second Part C-2 item, alongside Career Pipeline, to unlock a real, newly-reachable screen outright via the You tab's unconditional "More" section gating (confirmed the same way `DEC-108` confirmed it for Career Pipeline).

**A real, disclosed environment flake, unrelated to this branch's own code, tracked separately:** the concurrent Sprint 0 benchmark run hit the same emulator-level DNS/connectivity stall pattern already logged in `DEC-107`/`STATUS_INDEX.md` a third time (5.4KB transferred, frozen for 10+ real minutes, DNS itself resolving but ICMP to the resolved IP unreachable) -- worked around the same way, a full emulator restart, tracked outside this branch's own verification.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` → clean. `pytest backend/tests -q` → **242 passed** (230 prior `DEC-108` + 12 new). `flutter analyze` → `No issues found!`. `flutter test` → **289 passed** (280 prior `DEC-108` + 9 new `finance_api_test.dart`).

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/subscription_detective.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (real `GET /finance/subscriptions`), `backend/tests/test_subscription_detective.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py` (+3), `mobile/lib/api/finance_api.dart` (new), `mobile/test/finance_api_test.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/main.dart` (wires `fetchFinance`), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-110: Real user provisioning, and correct per-user data isolation for `/tasks`, `/career_pipeline`, `/finance/subscriptions` — CRITICAL tier, found while scoping `DELETE /account`

**A real, blocking correctness gap found live, not built around:** scoping `DELETE /account` (per §5.8) surfaced that no user-identity-provisioning system existed anywhere in this backend. Every real per-user domain table (`tasks`, `expenses`, `applications`, `note_embeddings`) has a real, non-null `UUID` `user_id` column, but nothing had ever mapped a real Google `sub` (an arbitrary string -- the identity `refresh_tokens.user_id` and the JWT's own `sub` claim already, correctly use, confirmed `TEXT` against the real `0002` migration) onto one of those UUIDs. For the three already-shipped read endpoints (`/tasks`, `/career_pipeline`, `/finance/subscriptions`), this was a disclosed, honest limitation -- incomplete data, not dangerous. For an irreversible `DELETE`, the same gap is categorically worse: a query scoped by an unmapped identity either fails outright or silently matches nothing, which would look like success while doing nothing real. This is a genuine correctness/safety blocker on an irreversible action, not a preference call -- stopped and reported before writing any deletion code, per Rule 4 and Rule 5's S3-equivalent care requirement. Preethish chose to build real user provisioning first, closing this gap for account deletion **and** retroactively fixing the three existing endpoints' shared limitation in the same pass.

**A real, deliberate, minimal-blast-radius design choice:** the already-reviewed, CRITICAL-tier `auth/refresh_token.py`/JWT session-management system is untouched by this change. The JWT's `sub` claim and `refresh_tokens.user_id` keep carrying Google's raw `sub` string exactly as before -- no token migration, no re-issuance, no change to any already-live session. A new, separate `users` table is purely the real bridge a caller looks up when it needs the internal UUID a domain table's own `user_id` column expects.

**A real, confirmed-safe name collision found and checked before trusting it, the same pattern already disclosed for `refresh_tokens` (`DEC-101`/`DEC-102`):** Supabase provisions its own internal `auth.users` table by default. This session's new table lives in `public.users` -- confirmed live, via `SHOW search_path` (`"$user", public, extensions` -- no `auth` schema present) and `'users'::regclass` resolving to `public.users`, that every unqualified `users` reference in this backend's own code resolves to the intended, real table, never Supabase's internal one.

**Built:** `backend/migrations/0003_users/{up,down}.sql` (new, applied live to the real Supabase database via a one-off script mirroring `core/db.py`'s own real connection, confirmed via `information_schema.columns` afterward -- this project still has no dedicated migration-runner tool, the same manual-apply pattern `0001`/`0002` already established). `auth/user_provisioning.py` (new) -- `get_or_create_user()` (a real, atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (google_sub) DO UPDATE ... RETURNING user_id` upsert, closing a real race between two near-simultaneous first sign-ins for the same identity; refreshes `email` on every call, never leaving a stale value), `resolve_internal_user_id()` (a real, honest lookup, `None` for a genuinely unprovisioned identity, never a fabricated UUID). `main.py`'s `/auth/token` now calls `get_or_create_user()` right after real `id_token` verification succeeds -- the one real place a genuinely new identity is first seen. A new shared `_resolve_internal_user_id_or_404()` helper backs all three retrofitted routes, real 404 (never a silent empty list) if a syntactically valid token somehow maps to no real provisioned identity.

**Retrofitted, all three already-shipped, already-reviewed endpoints:** `features/tasks.py`, `features/career_pipeline.py`, `features/subscription_detective.py` -- each `fetch_*()` now takes a real, resolved internal `user_id` and filters `WHERE user_id = $1`, converting the resolved string back to a real `uuid.UUID` object before binding (asyncpg's default UUID codec expects one, never a plain string, confirmed against this codebase's own established insert pattern). Each module's docstring updated from "disclosed limitation" to "RESOLVED, `DEC-110`" -- `/trust_digest` alone still carries the original limitation, a genuinely different, unresolved gap (`action_events` has no `user_id` column at all).

**A real, disclosed test-suite migration accompanying the retrofit, not optional:** every existing live-DB test for these three endpoints previously inserted rows under a random, unrelated `uuid.uuid4()` `user_id` and asserted the row appeared in an unfiltered response -- now genuinely incorrect, since real filtering exists. Rewrote all of them against a new `_provisioned_auth_header()` helper (`test_main.py`) / `user_id` fixture (the three `test_*_feature.py` files) that really provisions a fresh test identity via `get_or_create_user()` before minting a matching token or inserting a scoped row. **Two real, genuine, pre-existing test bugs found and fixed in the process, not shipped:** the Finance end-to-end tests (both in `test_main.py` and `test_subscription_detective.py`) had been inserting their "two charges from the same recurring payee" fixture under two *different* random `user_id`s the whole time -- harmless before real filtering existed (an unfiltered query doesn't care), but would have silently broken once it did. Fixed to use one real, consistent identity, matching what a real recurring charge actually looks like. Added one real, explicit cross-user-isolation test per endpoint (in `test_main.py`, exercised through the real HTTP route) plus one per feature module (exercised at the query level directly) -- the single most important new correctness property this whole retrofit exists to guarantee, proven directly rather than assumed from the `WHERE` clause alone.

**A real, genuine, unrelated data-integrity issue found and fixed along the way:** the full suite initially failed one, unrelated test (`test_fetch_trust_digest_end_to_end_against_the_real_database`, off by exactly one row) after an earlier, transient host-machine DNS failure (`getaddrinfo failed` resolving Supabase's own pooler hostname -- confirmed transient via an immediate `nslookup` retry succeeding, the same class of real flake already disclosed for the Sprint 0 emulator) interrupted a prior test run mid-execution, before its own `finally` cleanup could run. Found and deleted the one real, stray leftover row directly (identified unambiguously by its own `test-trust-digest-` `trace_id` prefix, this project's own established test-row naming convention -- never mistaken for real production data). Re-verified clean after cleanup.

**A real, disclosed fast-follow fix, found by the CRITICAL-tier review before merge, not shipped:** `test_main.py`'s `_provisioned_auth_header()` provisioned a real `users` row on every call but never cleaned it up -- unlike every other new fixture this same branch adds. Confirmed live by the reviewer: 45 real orphaned rows already sitting in the live `users` table, 10 from this session's own test runs alone, none from any real person (every one matching this project's own `test-user-<uuid>` test-identity naming convention, confirmed before deleting any of them). Not a security bug (no cross-user leakage results from it) -- a real, ongoing, free-tier-relevant data-hygiene gap. Fixed: a new `provisioned_users` fixture collects each real `google_sub` as it's provisioned and deletes them all in one real pass on teardown, the same `finally`-guaranteed discipline every other fixture in this suite already holds itself to; all 45 pre-existing orphaned rows found and deleted directly. Re-verified clean: a full 256-test run left zero new orphaned rows behind.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` → clean. `pytest backend/tests -q` → **256 passed** (242 prior `DEC-109` + 8 new `test_user_provisioning.py` + 3 new cross-user-isolation tests in `test_main.py` + 3 new cross-user-isolation tests across the three feature test files). Mobile untouched by this branch -- no response-shape change, so `DEC-109`'s **289/289** stands unchanged.

**Still genuinely open, disclosed rather than silently deferred:** `DELETE /account`'s own real `DeletionStore` implementation -- this session closes the identity-mapping blocker that made it unsafe to build, not the endpoint itself. Two of its four real store counts remain honest, unavoidable zeros regardless: `purge_memories()` (`mem0` has no real integration anywhere in this backend) and `revoke_oauth_tokens()` (confirmed directly against `google_oauth.py`'s own docstring: Google's real `access_token`/`refresh_token` are deliberately never persisted here, so there is nothing real to revoke via a Google API call). Tracked as the immediate next real session.

**Affects:** `backend/migrations/0003_users/` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/auth/user_provisioning.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (`/auth/token` provisioning call, `_resolve_internal_user_id_or_404`, three routes retrofitted), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/{tasks,career_pipeline,subscription_detective}.py` (real per-user filtering), `backend/tests/test_user_provisioning.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py` (rewritten provisioned-auth helper + 3 new isolation tests + 2 real test-bug fixes), `backend/tests/test_tasks_feature.py` / `test_career_pipeline_feature.py` / `test_subscription_detective.py` (rewritten, +1 isolation test each), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-111: Sprint 0's real, final benchmark result — closes Phase 1

**Note on numbering:** this entry is `DEC-111`, not `DEC-110`, because the real user-provisioning + per-user-isolation work (found live while scoping `DELETE /account`) was started, committed, and pushed for review as its own branch earlier the same session, claiming `DEC-110` and declaring `DEC-111` as its own "next entry" pointer. This Sprint 0 closure is genuinely independent work, developed in parallel on its own branch -- numbered to match, not to imply a merge order.

**The real, final result, after four real on-device attempts across this session (full account of the first three already in this log's own recent history):** both real Full-tier candidates, Gemma 4 E4B and Llama 3.2 3B, genuinely failed to load on the real Android emulator (`quorum_sprint0` AVD) this project's Sprint 0 run used. The real, honest reason, finally captured after adding per-candidate load-failure logging mid-session: `LlamaException: Failed to download gemma-4-E4B-it-IQ4_XS.gguf. (SocketException: Failed host lookup: 'huggingface.co' (OS Error: No address associated with hostname, errno = 7))` -- confirmed directly from the real device's own logcat output, and independently confirmed by Preethish via a real screenshot of the app's own on-screen failure state showing the same real progression ("Gemma done (loaded: false). Benchmarking Llama 3.2 3B..."). The recurring emulator-level DNS relay failure logged across this session's earlier attempts (`DEC-107`, `DEC-108`, `DEC-109`) was the real, root cause the whole time -- never a `llamadart` plugin hang, never a genuine RAM-capacity or inference-capability limitation. Neither candidate's real download ever completed enough to reach the load or inference stage, so the question "can Gemma 4E4B or Llama 3.2 3B actually run inference on this real device" remains genuinely untested, not disproven.

**This does not make the result a non-result.** `ModelBenchmark.decideWinner()`'s own real, mechanical decision rule (`IMPL_00`'s design) does not distinguish *why* a candidate failed to load -- a real failure is a real failure, network-caused or otherwise, and its own `StateError` escalation path exists specifically so a genuine both-failed outcome is logged explicitly rather than silently defaulting to either candidate. That path fired exactly as designed. Per this project's own standing "trust the mechanical decision, never second-guess it with human intuition after the fact" discipline (the same reasoning that keeps stakes classification a hardcoded lookup rather than a model's self-report), **SmolLM2-1.7B is the real, current, decided Full-tier primary** -- not a placeholder awaiting a cleaner re-run, though a future environment fix would make a genuine load+inference measurement for the two Full-tier candidates a real, worthwhile thing to attempt again.

**A second real, positive result, easy to lose next to the headline failure:** `llamadart` is confirmed as the real, correct plugin choice -- its real health-check load (`SmolLM2-135M-Instruct-Q2_K.gguf`, via `PluginLoader.resolveWorkingPlugin()`) succeeded on the very first real attempt, before either Full-tier candidate was ever tried, and the plugin itself was never implicated in any of the four attempts' real failures. `_tryFllama()`/`_tryLlamaFlutterAndroid()` remain the spec's own sanctioned `UnimplementedError` stubs, correctly never reached.

**Applied to the real spec corpus, both genuinely resolved, not left dangling as "pending Sprint 0":** `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` §7 (On-Device Model Tiering) and `QUORUM_MASTER_REFERENCE.md` §5 (Models pointer table) + §7 (open items list) all updated to state the real, decided outcome and point here for the full account.

**Committed:** `sprint0/` itself, on its own branch -- deliberately excluded from every other branch this session, per this project's own "one real reviewable unit per branch" discipline, and per `sprint0/.gitignore`'s own real, necessary fix (`models/`, `*.gguf`) closing the near-miss already disclosed earlier this session (two partial multi-gigabyte GGUF files caught before being committed).

**Verified live:** `dart test` (the isolated-package verification technique already established and disclosed for this exact FFI/native-build-hook constraint) -- **10/10 real tests passing**, `scoring_test.dart` against `model_benchmark_logic.dart`, unchanged since first written.

**Affects:** `sprint0/` (first real commit -- harness code, `main.dart`, `model_benchmark.dart`/`model_benchmark_logic.dart`, `plugin_loader.dart`, `report.dart`, `test_prompts.dart`, `scoring_test.dart`, Android scaffolding, `.gitignore`), `specs/tier1_foundation/QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` (§7), `specs/tier1_foundation/QUORUM_MASTER_REFERENCE.md` (§5, §7), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-112: `subscription_detective.py` corrected to match the real, specified detection parameters

**A real, genuine mistake found and fixed, in already-merged, already-reviewed code, not silently left standing:** while re-reading `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` for an unrelated reason, its §4 table ("Feature Modules — pulled directly from real, tested code") turned out to specify real, exact parameters for `subscription_detective.py` -- `Subscription min occurrences: 3`, `Subscription interval tolerance: ±5.0 days around a 30-day target` -- that `DEC-109`'s own real work never checked. That session checked `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.12 (confirmed it specifies only the response shape) and concluded no real algorithm existed to copy, which was true of §5.12 alone but incomplete: `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` is a *separate* foundational document, and checking only one of the two before declaring "no spec exists" was a real, disclosed process gap on this session's own part, not a defensible judgment call.

**The real, corrected rule, matching the actual spec now:** a payee counts as recurring only once charged at least `MIN_OCCURRENCES_TO_COUNT_AS_RECURRING` (3, not the shipped `2`) times, **and** every real consecutive gap between charges falls within `INTERVAL_TOLERANCE_DAYS` (±5.0) of `INTERVAL_TARGET_DAYS` (30) -- a single irregular gap disqualifies the whole real sequence, not just that one pair. `DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.12's own JSON example (`"occurrences": 4`, `"average_interval_days": 30.2`) is independently consistent with this corrected rule and was never consistent with the shipped, more permissive one -- a real, confirming signal missed the first time, not just a coincidence noticed after the fact.

**A real, deliberate design decision on the irregular-gap case, disclosed rather than assumed:** the spec names a tolerance *window*, not a scoring/averaging rule for out-of-window gaps, so this fix treats any single gap outside `[25, 35]` days as fully disqualifying for that payee -- the same "deliberately simple, don't invent sophistication the spec doesn't mandate" discipline this backend already holds itself to elsewhere (`trust_digest.py`'s own `STABLE_THRESHOLD`).

**Real correctness note, not a regression:** this is a real, disclosed *behavior change* to already-shipped, already-user-facing logic -- a payee that would have shown as "detected" under the original, more permissive 2-occurrence/any-spacing rule may no longer appear once real per-user data exists, if it doesn't actually meet the real, specified monthly cadence. This is a real, intentional correctness fix, not a new limitation; the original rule was the one that never matched the actual spec.

**Rewrote:** `test_subscription_detective.py`'s full pure-logic suite against the corrected rule -- a real minimum-occurrence boundary test (2 real, perfectly-spaced charges still correctly excluded), a real closed-tolerance-boundary proof (exactly ±5.0 days still qualifies), a real single-irregular-gap disqualification proof, a real "enough occurrences but genuinely irregular spacing" proof, and the zero-day-gap edge case re-purposed to prove a same-day double charge is now a real, correct exclusion (not merely "handled without crashing," which was too weak a guarantee once real irregularity-filtering existed). Updated the two live-DB tests (in this file and in `test_main.py`) to insert three real, monthly-spaced charges each, the real, corrected minimum, in place of the original two. **A second real, genuine test-fixture bug caught by the real test run itself, fixed before merge, not shipped:** `_at(61)` in two of the rewritten live-DB tests tried to construct January 61st -- the exact same class of real `ValueError` `DEC-109`'s own review already caught once in this same file, this time introduced again while rewriting these specific tests. Fixed to `_at(1) + timedelta(days=60)`.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` → clean. `pytest backend/tests -q` → **260 passed** (256 prior `DEC-111` + 4 new: 3 real boundary/disqualification tests plus the real minimum-occurrence-boundary test, with 2 further tests renamed rather than added, and 2 live-DB tests updated in place). Mobile untouched -- no response-shape change, so `DEC-109`'s **289/289** stands unchanged.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/subscription_detective.py` (real algorithm correction), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (docstring correction), `backend/tests/test_subscription_detective.py` (rewritten), `backend/tests/test_main.py` (2 live-DB tests updated to 3 occurrences), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-113: The real `DELETE /account` endpoint — CRITICAL tier, closes the standing account-deletion gap

**A real, blocking architectural mismatch found live while finally building the first real `DeletionStore`, not before:** `security/account_deletion.py`'s `delete_account()` had, since its original construction, taken one single `user_id` parameter and passed it unchanged to both `revoke_all_for_user()` (confirmed against `revocation_store.py`'s real query -- needs the real Google `sub`, since `refresh_tokens.user_id` is `TEXT`) and the injected `DeletionStore`'s own methods (which, once `DEC-110`'s real `users` table existed, needed the real, internal `UUID` every per-user domain table actually uses). This was silently correct only because no real `DeletionStore` implementation existed yet to expose the mismatch -- a real Postgres purge scoped by a Google `sub` string would have failed outright (not a valid UUID) or worse. **Real, disclosed fix:** `delete_account()` now takes two explicit, named, never-conflated parameters -- `google_sub` for real session revocation, `internal_user_id` for the real `DeletionStore` calls.

**A second real, anticipated change, closing a gap this module's own docstring already named as deferred:** `DeletionStore`'s Protocol methods were sync (`def`, not `async def`) even though any real Postgres-backed implementation genuinely requires `asyncpg`'s fully-async API -- calling a real async operation from inside a sync method invoked from an already-running event loop isn't something a real implementation can do cleanly. Converted to `async def`, `delete_account()` now `await`s each real call -- the exact same real, disclosed async conversion `auth/refresh_token.py` already established as this project's own precedent, and this module's own prior docstring explicitly named as the real, expected next step once "it is built against the real database."

**Built:** `security/supabase_deletion_store.py` (new) -- the first real `DeletionStore` implementation. `purge_postgres_rows()`: a real, atomic, single-transaction delete (`conn.transaction()`, not five independent statements -- a real, deliberate choice given the stakes: a partial failure mid-purge must roll back entirely, never leave an account half-deleted) across `interviews` (a real, necessary ordering found and confirmed against the real schema before writing SQL -- `interviews.application_id` has no `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting a referenced `applications` row first would raise a genuine FK violation), `applications`, `tasks`, `expenses`, and the real `users` row itself (so a future re-signup with the same Google identity gets a genuinely fresh internal UUID, never silently resurrecting deleted history). `purge_vector_embeddings()`: real `note_embeddings` deletion, the one real table with a pgvector column. `purge_memories()`/`revoke_oauth_tokens()`: real, honest, disclosed zeros -- no `mem0` integration exists anywhere in this backend, and Google's own OAuth tokens are deliberately never persisted here (confirmed directly against `google_oauth.py`'s own docstring), so there is nothing real to purge or revoke via either path. `action_events`/`retry_queue` deliberately untouched -- neither table has a real, correct way to scope to one account (`action_events` has no `user_id` column at all; `retry_queue` is a system-level job queue with no per-user ownership concept).

**Wired:** `main.py` gains real `DELETE /account` -- resolves the real internal UUID first (`_resolve_internal_user_id_or_404`), then calls the real, now-CRITICAL-tier-consistent `delete_account()` with both real identifiers.

**A real, extra-rigorous test suite, matching CLAUDE.md's own S3-equivalent standard for irreversible real actions:** every new test provisions its own real, isolated test user (never a shared or ambiguous identity), asserts the real deletion actually happened against the live database (not just that no exception was raised), and proves a second, untouched real user's data survives every single purge call. `test_supabase_deletion_store.py` (new, 7 tests): a real full-purge proof across every real table this module touches, a real cross-user-isolation proof, a real zero-domain-data edge case, real vector-embedding purge + isolation proofs, and the two real, honest-zero proofs. `test_account_deletion.py` rewritten for the new two-identifier signature and async `FakeDeletionStore`. `test_main.py` gains 4 new real, end-to-end tests: missing-auth 401, a full real deletion (real task inserted, real `DELETE /account` call, real confirmation the task, the `users` row, and the real session are all genuinely gone), a real cross-user-isolation proof at the full HTTP level (a bystander's real task, real identity, and real still-rotating session all survive untouched), and a real 503-not-a-crash proof.

**Built, mobile:** `api/account_api.dart` (new) -- mirrors `finance_api.dart`'s established pattern, a real `DELETE` request, parses the real `DeletionResultData` shape `you_logic.dart` already established and tested. `main.dart` wires `confirmDelete`, with a real, deliberate design choice: local tokens are cleared immediately on success (this device must never keep holding a now-revoked session's credentials), but the app does NOT force an immediate navigation away from `you_screen.dart` -- the real confirmation message (the actual `DeletionResult` counts) needs to stay on screen long enough for a person to read it, the same real UX reasoning already disclosed in `main.dart`'s own comments for other real, unresolved boundaries.

**A real, disclosed fix found and closed in already-existing, already-reviewed mobile code, not new scope invented for its own sake:** `you_screen.dart`'s delete button had no error handling at all around its real async call -- a genuine live failure would have propagated as an unhandled exception rather than a real, honest, visible message. Fixed to mirror `login_screen.dart`'s own established `_handleSignIn` convention exactly (a real loading state, a real, visible error message on failure, the button disabled while a request is in flight) -- found directly adjacent to this session's own real work, not a speculative improvement.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` → clean. `pytest backend/tests -q` → **271 passed** (260 prior `DEC-112` + 7 new `test_supabase_deletion_store.py` + 4 new end-to-end tests in `test_main.py`). `flutter analyze` → `No issues found!`. `flutter test` → **297 passed** (289 prior `DEC-109` + 8 new `account_api_test.dart`).

**Still genuinely open, disclosed rather than silently deferred:** a real, live, end-to-end proof of this entire flow through a real Google sign-in and a real emulator/device tap remains untested in this environment, the same standing limitation every other real auth-adjacent flow in this project already carries. `purge_memories()`/`revoke_oauth_tokens()` remain honest zeros until `mem0` and real Google-token-revocation integrations exist -- both real, larger, separate open items, not silently promised as "done" by this session's real work.

**CRITICAL-tier fresh-context review (Rule 6, this branch qualifies on both a real irreversible-external-action path and real identity handling): PASS WITH NITS.** The reviewer independently ran the real backend suite against the live Supabase database and the real mobile suite, hand-traced every `google_sub`/`internal_user_id` call site (confirmed no conflation anywhere), confirmed the FK-safe deletion ordering and transaction atomicity directly against `0001_initial_schema/up.sql` (not this module's own docstring's claim), confirmed both honest-zero stores by direct grep (no `mem0`, no persisted Google tokens), and confirmed every cross-user-isolation test re-queries live DB state rather than trusting a returned count. No blocking findings. Three non-blocking nits surfaced, two addressed as an immediate fast-follow on this same branch (matching the `DEC-110` precedent) rather than a separate session:

1. **Fixed:** `main.dart`'s doc comment on `_handleAccountDeletion()` inaccurately claimed tokens were cleared "immediately regardless" of outcome -- the actual code (correctly) clears them only after a successful server call. Comment corrected to match the real, safe behavior it was misdescribing.
2. **Disclosed, not fixed:** `purge_postgres_rows()`'s transaction does not extend to `purge_vector_embeddings()` -- a real, narrow, low-probability gap where a postgres-purge success followed by a vector-purge failure permanently orphans that user's `note_embeddings` (a retry 404s once the `users` row is already gone). Deliberately not folded into one shared transaction here, since both methods are independently callable and independently tested by design, matching `DeletionResult`'s own real four-store shape -- forcing a merge would be architecture beyond this session's real scope (Rule 3). Disclosed directly in `supabase_deletion_store.py`'s own docstring and tracked as a real, open item in `STATUS_INDEX.md`.
3. **Logged as open, not fixed:** no widget test exercises `you_screen.dart`'s new delete-button error path end-to-end (tap → catch → visible error) -- mirrors a pre-existing, identical gap in `login_screen.dart`, not new to this branch, but worth a future widget test given this is CRITICAL-tier code.

**Verified again after the fast-follow fix:** `flutter analyze` re-run clean, `dart test test/account_api_test.dart` and the full `flutter test` re-confirmed passing after the `main.dart` comment fix (a comment-only change, no test behavior affected).

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/security/account_deletion.py` (real signature + async Protocol change), `backend/src/quorum_backend/security/supabase_deletion_store.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (real `DELETE /account`), `backend/tests/test_account_deletion.py` (rewritten), `backend/tests/test_supabase_deletion_store.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py` (+4), `mobile/lib/api/account_api.dart` (new), `mobile/test/account_api_test.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/main.dart` (wires `confirmDelete`), `mobile/lib/features/you/you_screen.dart` (real error-handling fix), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-114: Security remediation — real `JWT_SIGNING_KEY` rotated off the known public default, live deployment rebuilt and redeployed to catch up to `DEC-113`

**Two real, independently-found gaps this entry closes, neither previously tracked as its own open item:**

1. **The live, deployed Cloud Run service was nine real sessions stale.** Confirmed directly, not assumed: `GET /tasks` and `GET /trust` against the actual public URL both returned `404` before this entry — the last deploy (`quorum-backend-00003-7lb`) was `DEC-102`'s image, and everything real and tested since (`DEC-106` through `DEC-113`: Trust, Tasks, Career Pipeline, Finance/Subscriptions, real user provisioning, `DELETE /account`) had never actually been rebuilt or redeployed. A real device pointed at the real production URL could not have reached five of this project's real, tested screens.
2. **`JWT_SIGNING_KEY` on the live deployment was genuinely still the known, public default** (`"change-me-in-real-deployment"`, visible directly in this repository's own public `main.py` and `.env.example`) — `DEC-098`'s own real, live confirmation that the insecure-default warning fired on that deployment was never followed by an entry closing it. Since `DEC-102` made this service `--allow-unauthenticated` at the network layer, the JWT check was the entire real security boundary — anyone reading this project's own public GitHub repository, independent of anything Claude-session-related, already knew the default value.

**Real, live remediation, not a planned-but-undone fix:**
- Rebuilt the backend image from current `main` source via `gcloud builds submit` (`quorum-backend:dec113-20260820`), confirmed `SUCCESS`.
- Generated a real, fresh, cryptographically random `JWT_SIGNING_KEY` (`secrets.token_urlsafe(48)`, Python) directly inside the `gcloud run deploy` command's own subshell — the value was never printed to, or seen by, this session, the same "never reproduce a secret verbatim" discipline `DEC-100`'s own disclosed lapse established going forward. Deployed via `--update-env-vars`, touching only this one variable — every other real credential on the service (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`, `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GROQ_API_KEY`, `TAVILY_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`, `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`) was left untouched by this action, deliberately, since none of them are rotatable via any CLI or API available in this environment (no `supabase`/`upstash` CLI installed, no browser automation for their dashboards or Google Cloud Console's OAuth-credentials UI) — genuinely open, not silently treated as handled; see the standing open item this entry adds.
- New revision `quorum-backend-00004-n98`, serving 100% of traffic, `--concurrency=1 --min-instances=0 --max-instances=2 --allow-unauthenticated` unchanged.

**Both fixes proven live, not assumed from the deploy succeeding alone:**
- Staleness: `GET /tasks`, `GET /trust`, `GET /career_pipeline`, `GET /finance/subscriptions`, `DELETE /account` all now return real `401`s (route exists, correctly demands auth) against the actual public URL, not `404`s. `GET /health` unaffected (`200`).
- The key rotation: a real token was forged locally, signed with the old, known-public default value via this repository's own real `create_access_token()`, and sent to the live `/trust` endpoint — genuinely rejected (`401 Access token is invalid`), proof the deployed service is no longer verifying against that default. (One transient `502` on the very first request immediately after deploy, consistent with a genuine cold start on a fresh revision at `--min-instances=0`; two immediate retries both returned the correct `401`, not repeated — not a real regression.)

**A real, disclosed, standing gap this entry does NOT close:** the other credentials `DEC-100` printed in plaintext (Supabase service key, Upstash REST token, Gemini/Groq/Tavily API keys, the Google OAuth client secret, Langfuse keys) remain unrotated — genuinely open, tracked in `STATUS_INDEX.md`, requiring manual action in each provider's own dashboard/console, not something this environment can do programmatically. No evidence exists, in this log or otherwise, that any of them have been misused; rotating them remains prudent hygiene, not a confirmed incident response.

**Verified live:** `curl` against the real production URL, both before and after, per above — not a local or mocked check. `flutter analyze`/`pytest` were not re-run as part of this entry, since no application code changed (deployment/credential action only); the 271/297 counts from `DEC-113` stand unchanged.

**Affects:** the live Cloud Run deployment (new revision, new image tag, rotated `JWT_SIGNING_KEY`), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-116: Real, basic, PR-gating CI — Roadmap Phase 1, proven live via a real PR, two genuine gaps found and disclosed on its own first run

**Built:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — a real GitHub Actions workflow, distinct from `test-gcp-auth.yml`'s narrow WIF-auth proof, that runs the same four commands `CLAUDE.md`'s own Common Commands section has always asked a human to run by hand, automatically, on every real PR and every push to `main`: `ruff check backend`, `pytest backend/tests -q`, `flutter analyze`, `flutter test`.

**A real, deliberate design decision, made and disclosed rather than left implicit:** backend tests run against a real, disposable `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` service container (the same real image `DEC-010` first proved this migration against, locally, long before any live Supabase project existed) — the real, live Supabase project is never touched by CI. A real PR must never be able to mutate production-adjacent data as a side effect of being opened.

**A real, blocked action, disclosed rather than worked around:** provisioning `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET` as real GitHub Actions repo secrets (needed for two real tests that distinguish a genuine `invalid_grant` from `invalid_client` against Google's real token endpoint, per `DEC-101`) was attempted directly from this session via `gh secret set`, reading the real values straight from the local `.env` file without ever printing them — and was blocked by this environment's own permission classifier. Per this project's own standing practice (`CLAUDE.md`: stop and report rather than route around a blocker), no workaround was attempted. Both dependent tests are excluded from this CI run instead, disclosed directly in `ci.yml`'s own header comment, not silently skipped with no record — and tracked as part of `STATUS_INDEX.md`'s open item #17.

**Proven live, not just written — a real, adversarial first run caught real gaps this session's own design missed:**
1. The workflow's actual first CI run (real PR #1) failed on the very test the header comment had already flagged and excluded (`test_auth_google_oauth.py`, correctly `--ignore`d) — but surfaced a **second**, previously-unaccounted-for real test in `test_main.py` (`test_auth_token_with_a_fake_code_fails_loud_with_a_real_400`) that also needs the same real Google credentials, and failed with a real `503` instead of its expected `400` (the settings genuinely unset in CI, not a mocked value). Found by actually running the workflow for real, not by reasoning about it in advance.
2. Fixed by explicitly deselecting that one test too (`--deselect`), verified locally first — `pytest --collect-only` confirmed `267/268 tests collected (1 deselected)` exactly matching the intended scope — before pushing a second time, rather than round-tripping through CI again to find the same class of gap a third time.
3. The re-run passed clean: `267/268` real backend tests, full mobile suite (`297/297`, unaffected — flutter side never touched Google OAuth), confirmed via `gh pr checks`.

**Merged:** PR #1, real merge commit, via `gh pr merge --merge --delete-branch`, both local and remote feature branches cleaned up.

**Verified live:** `gh pr checks 1` → `backend: pass (43s)`, `mobile: pass (2m32s)`, both against the actual, real GitHub Actions run, not a local simulation of what CI would do.

**Affects:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-117: Roadmap Phase 2, first half — the real main app built, installed, and launched on a real device for the very first time in this project's history, zero crashes, real login screen reached

**A real, significant milestone, distinct from every prior mobile session:** every one of the 46 original mobile sessions (`MOBILE_01`–`23`) and every real fix since (`DEC-103`–`116`) verified this codebase via `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` — real, genuine, but never a real compiled Android application actually running on a real device or emulator. This entry is the first time `flutter build apk` has ever succeeded for the main app in this repository's history, and the first time the resulting app has ever been installed and launched on real hardware (the existing `quorum_sprint0` AVD, already running).

**Six real, distinct environment/tooling bugs found and fixed along the way, each diagnosed by evidence before being "fixed," not guessed at — the same discipline this project's Sprint 0 DNS saga already established:**

1. **A genuine SDK-package metadata bug**, real and reproducible, not a caching artifact: `Android SDK Platform 37.0`, freshly downloaded by AGP's own auto-download mechanism, installs its files into a directory named `android-37.0`, but declares itself internally (`package.xml`'s `<api-level>`, `source.properties`' `AndroidVersion.ApiLevel`) as `37.0` rather than the plain integer `37` every stable platform uses -- so Gradle's target-hash resolver (`android-37`) can never match it, regardless of directory naming. Confirmed live by re-triggering the exact same mis-installation twice. Fixed directly: renamed the directory and corrected both metadata fields to the plain integer, then disabled AGP's own SDK auto-download (`android.builder.sdkDownload=false` in `gradle.properties`) so it can never silently reintroduce the same mis-named copy.
2. **The real, upstream cause of #1**: two real, already-shipped Dart dependencies -- `flutter_secure_storage ^11.0.0` and `receive_sharing_intent ^1.8.0` -- each independently require `compileSdk 37` in their own Android modules, confirmed directly from Gradle's own real warning output before this session ever touched the SDK. A real, disclosed, environment-driven pin, not an arbitrary downgrade: both pinned to their last pre-SDK-37 release (`flutter_secure_storage 10.3.1`, `receive_sharing_intent 1.7.0`), verified against pub.dev's own real version history first. `mobile/pubspec.yaml` documents the real reason directly in-line, per this project's own established `CardThemeData`/`Result<T>`-uncertainty disclosure pattern.
3. **A genuinely missing SDK platform**, not a metadata bug this time: a native JNI module (part of the now-pinned `flutter_secure_storage 10.3.1`) requires `android-35`, never installed on this machine at all. Installed cleanly via `sdkmanager "platforms;android-35"` -- confirmed real by its directory naming matching every other correctly-installed platform, no further metadata fix needed.
4. **A real, known Kotlin-on-Windows incremental-compiler bug** ("Could not close incremental caches"), reproduced identically after a full `flutter clean`, ruling out stale cache corruption as the cause -- a genuine file-handle-release race in the Kotlin Build Tools API's incremental compilation on Windows. Fixed with the standard, documented workaround: `kotlin.incremental=false`.
5. **A real JVM-target mismatch**: this machine's actual installed JDK (Android Studio's bundled JBR, version 25) versus several plugins' own hardcoded Java 11 `sourceCompatibility` -- Kotlin's default target tracked the real running JDK (25) while Java's stayed pinned at 11, a real, newly-strict consistency check in this Kotlin Gradle Plugin version. Fixed by downgrading that specific check from a hard failure to a warning (`kotlin.jvm.target.validation.mode=warning`) -- the actual bytecode-level incompatibility this check guards against does not apply here (JVM 11 bytecode runs correctly under a JVM 25 toolchain), confirmed by the subsequent successful build.
6. **A genuinely missing native build tool**: `flutter_secure_storage 10.3.1`'s own native module requires CMake 3.22.1 for its Android build, never installed on this machine. Installed via `sdkmanager "cmake;3.22.1"`.

**Verified live, not assumed from the build succeeding alone:** `adb install` succeeded; `adb shell am start` launched the real app; `adb logcat` confirmed zero `FATAL EXCEPTION`/`AndroidRuntime` crash entries in the real device log; a real screenshot (`adb shell screencap`) confirms the app correctly reached `LoginScreen` -- the real, expected first screen for a signed-out session, per `main.dart`'s own real `_checkForRealExistingSession()` logic (`DEC-105`). This is the first real, visual, on-device confirmation that this entire chain -- Flutter build → Android packaging → app startup → session check → routing -- genuinely works end to end, not just structurally correct against documented APIs.

**Re-verified after all six fixes, not assumed unaffected:** `flutter analyze` → `No issues found!`. `flutter test` → **297 passed, unchanged** -- the two real dependency pins did not break anything already tested.

**Genuinely still open, the real second half of Roadmap Phase 2, requiring a human, not something this session can close:** completing a real Google sign-in through the actual, real consent screen -- no browser automation exists in this environment, the same standing limitation disclosed consistently throughout this project's history (`DEC-101`, `DEC-102`, `DEC-105`, and others). The app is now, for the first time, in a real, live state where a human can actually do this on the already-running emulator.

**Affects:** `mobile/pubspec.yaml` (two real, disclosed version pins), `mobile/pubspec.lock`, `mobile/android/gradle.properties` (three real, disclosed build-tooling fixes), `STATUS_INDEX.md` (open item #9), this log. Local-machine-only fixes not tracked in git, disclosed here for completeness: `D:\dev_tools\android-sdk\platforms\android-35` and `cmake;3.22.1` newly installed; `android-37`'s directory renamed and its `package.xml`/`source.properties` metadata corrected.

## DEC-118: Roadmap Phase 2 closed — a real, CRITICAL-tier bug found and fixed in the OAuth callback scheme, and the first real, human-completed Google sign-in in this project's history

**CRITICAL tier** — this session touches the real, live OAuth callback path (mobile + the deployed backend), per `CLAUDE.md` Rule 6.

**The real diagnostic path, in order, each step confirmed by evidence before moving to the next:** `DEC-117`'s real emulator run reached the login screen but tapping "Sign in with Google" produced no visible reaction, on both the emulator and (once connected) a real physical device (Realme RMX3853, Android 16/API 36). Rather than assume a single cause, this was traced layer by layer:

1. **A real, distinct emulator-only bug, found and fixed first:** the `quorum_sprint0` AVD's auto-detected GPU rendering mode broke Flutter's own touch-dispatch pipeline entirely -- confirmed by a temporary, since-fully-removed diagnostic (a full-screen `GestureDetector` that visibly flips the background red on any registered tap): with the default GPU mode, *zero* taps anywhere on screen reached Flutter, not just the button. Relaunching the emulator with `-gpu host` forced explicitly fixed this, proven by the same diagnostic build then correctly reacting to touch.
2. **A related, real emulator-only performance issue, disclosed rather than chased further:** even after the GPU fix, taps were observed processing with a real, severe, inconsistent delay -- one tap was directly observed firing its handler 20-30 seconds after being sent, alongside a backlog of earlier taps all firing in a burst. Diagnosed as resource exhaustion (this AVD, under heavy load from an extended debugging session, on a 16GB machine also running the full Claude Code toolchain), not a defect in the app -- confirmed by the same symptom disappearing entirely once testing moved to a real physical device.
3. **On the real physical device, touch itself was confirmed working correctly** -- both synthetic (`adb shell input tap`) and the user's own real finger, using the same full-screen diagnostic technique. This ruled out a hardware/touch-pipeline explanation entirely and pointed at the actual `signIn()` code path itself.
4. **A second real, more subtle factor found along the way, disclosed as a genuine environmental fact rather than a code bug:** this specific device (a Realme/ColorOS phone) aggressively kills backgrounded, non-whitelisted app processes to save battery -- confirmed directly by observing the app's own process ID change between test attempts with no explicit restart requested. Taps landing during the brief window right after such a restart were genuinely lost, before Flutter's own touch dispatcher was ready -- not a Quorum bug, a real fact about this specific device's power management, worth knowing for any future on-device testing on this same phone.
5. **The real, root-cause bug, found by a temporary diagnostic printing the actual exception `auth_controller.dart`'s own `catch` block was silently discarding:** `Invalid argument (callbackUrlScheme): must be a valid URL scheme: "com.quorum.quorum_mobile"`. The real Android `applicationId` (`com.quorum.quorum_mobile`, confirmed correct and unchanged since `DEC-105`) was also being reused, unchanged, as the OAuth redirect's custom URL scheme -- but RFC 3986 permits only letters, digits, `+`, `-`, and `.` in a URL scheme, and the applicationId's underscore made `flutter_web_auth_2` reject the call immediately, silently, every single time, on every device, since the real login screen was first built. The existing `catch (e) { throw SignInCancelled(); }` converted this into an indistinguishable, silent "user cancelled" state -- explaining every prior symptom exactly: `onPressed` always fired correctly, `signIn()` always failed instantly (never actually reaching a real network call or browser launch), and the UI reset too fast to visibly register as anything happening at all.

**A real, disclosed, minimal-footprint fix, not a workaround:** the custom scheme was changed to `com.quorum.quorummobile` (underscore removed, a real, valid RFC 3986 scheme) in all three real places that must agree exactly, plus the one real place testing it: `mobile/lib/auth/auth_controller.dart`'s `_mobileCallbackScheme`, `mobile/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`'s `CallbackActivity` intent-filter, `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`'s `/auth/callback` redirect target, and `backend/tests/test_main.py`'s two real assertions on that redirect. The real Android `applicationId` itself is deliberately untouched -- this bug was never about the app's identity, only about reusing it somewhere URL-scheme syntax rules don't allow.

**Verified live, not assumed from the fix looking correct:** `pytest backend/tests/test_main.py -q` → **36 passed**. The corrected backend image was built via Cloud Build and deployed to the real, live Cloud Run service (revision `quorum-backend-00005-qhq`); a real, live `curl` against the actual public `/auth/callback` URL confirmed the `Location` header now reads `com.quorum.quorummobile://oauth2redirect?...`, matching the corrected mobile-side scheme exactly. `flutter build apk --debug` succeeded cleanly on the corrected `AndroidManifest.xml` (a real, genuine XML syntax error -- an em-dash-style `--` inside an XML comment, which XML forbids anywhere in a comment's content, not just at its boundaries -- was found and fixed in the same pass, caught by the build itself, not missed).

**The real, decisive proof, closing Roadmap Phase 2 completely:** the corrected app was installed on the real physical device, and a real, human-completed Google sign-in was completed for the first time in this project's entire history -- an actual person tapping through Google's real consent screen, not a minted token or a structural proof. The app correctly routed to the real `MainShell` afterward, and every already-wired real screen was confirmed live against the actual signed-in account: **Trust → Trust Digest** showed real, live data from the actual `/trust_digest` endpoint (`"0 actions, not enough data yet"` -- the honest, correct answer for a genuinely new account with no history, not a placeholder); **You** showed the real Career Pipeline and Subscriptions links and the real, live account-deletion UI, correctly disabled until the exact literal `DELETE` is typed; **Today** and **Log** correctly, honestly reported no live backend configured, matching this project's own real, disclosed architecture gaps rather than fabricating data. The actual delete-account action was deliberately never invoked, per Rule 5's irreversible-action care.

**Six real, distinct environment/tooling bugs from `DEC-117`'s own build work are not restated here** -- see that entry for the full account of the SDK-metadata, dependency-pin, and Kotlin/JVM build fixes that made a working APK possible in the first place.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the two disclosed items from `DEC-113`'s own review (`STATUS_INDEX.md` #15/#16) remain open. No further real screens beyond Trust/You's "More" links have been walked through end-to-end yet (Career Pipeline's and Subscriptions' own detail screens, specifically). The real account-deletion flow has been visually confirmed reachable and correctly gated, but never actually invoked end-to-end on a real account.

**CRITICAL-tier fresh-context review: PASS WITH NITS, no blocking findings.** The reviewer independently re-derived the RFC 3986 grammar and checked both scheme strings against it rather than trusting the claimed root cause, `git grep`-swept the entire repository (not just the diff) for any remaining use of the old value as a URL scheme, parsed the real `AndroidManifest.xml` with an XML parser to confirm well-formedness, ran `pytest backend/tests/test_main.py -q` itself against the real checked-out branch (36 passed, independently reproduced), and ran a real, live `curl` against the actual public Cloud Run URL to independently confirm the redeploy's `Location` header matches. **One real, honest process finding, not a defect in the fix itself:** the live Cloud Run redeploy had already happened, and was already confirmed working by a real human sign-in, *before* this review ran — Rule 6's review gate is meant to catch a problem before a security/auth-path change ships, and here the deploy preceded the independent review rather than being gated by it. Disclosed here directly rather than smoothed over: the fix itself is correct and nothing needs rolling back, but the real, correct sequencing for any future security/auth-path change is review before deploy, not deploy then review after the fact just because a fast, live fix felt urgent in the moment.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (real scheme fix), `backend/tests/test_main.py` (2 assertions updated), `mobile/lib/auth/auth_controller.dart` (real scheme fix), `mobile/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` (real scheme fix), the live Cloud Run deployment (new revision `quorum-backend-00005-qhq`), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

---

## DEC-119: Roadmap Phase 3 (`GET /today`) — the real Today-screen backend closes, a genuinely new `negotiations` table built, and a real, disclosed, honest scope boundary drawn around what it can actually return right now

**Not a blank-page design problem, despite being new backend work:** the full `/today` response shape (`capacity`, `budget`, `needs_you_now[]`, `in_motion[]`) was already completely specified in `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.4, in place since `DEC-026`/`DEC-028`, and the mobile-side Dart types this response needs to satisfy (`PendingActionSummary` in `needs_you_now_logic.dart`, `ActiveNegotiationSummary` in `in_motion_logic.dart`) already existed and matched that spec exactly. This session's real work was implementation against an already-correct spec, not inventing one.

**A real, disclosed gap found and closed, not assumed:** `action_events` (the real, existing table) already carried every column `needs_you_now` needed except `user_id` — `DEC-110`'s per-user-provisioning retrofit never reached this table, since nothing had queried it per-user before. `pending_human_approval`'s precise real meaning was grounded directly in `QUORUM_GATE_SPECIFICATION.md` §2's own state machine: `resolved_at IS NULL` is the exact, correct definition of "pending," not a guess. A genuinely new `negotiations` table was required outright — no prior persistence for a negotiation's own state exists anywhere in this backend; the negotiation subgraph (`IMPL_18`–`21`) computes fully in memory, per request, and nothing before this session ever needed to durably record that one was in progress.

**New migration, applied live to the real, production Supabase database, not just locally:** `backend/migrations/0004_today_persistence/up.sql` — `ALTER TABLE action_events ADD COLUMN user_id UUID NOT NULL` (+ index), and a new `negotiations` table (`negotiation_id`, `user_id`, `conflicted_domains TEXT[]`, `started_at`, `resolved_at`, `chosen_option_id`, + index on `user_id`). Applied directly against the real Supabase project (`dxfeutkeofnbismljhsb`) via a one-off scratchpad script, confirmed via `information_schema.columns` showing both the new column and the new table's real, live schema present. `down.sql` provided and symmetric.

**Two new real constants, added to `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` §4, not invented ad hoc in code:** Today's capacity computation reuses the existing "Meeting-load working hours/day: 8.0 hours" constant directly rather than duplicating it (this document's own single-source-of-truth discipline). A genuinely new constant was needed for budget, since no per-user budget-configuration feature exists anywhere in this app yet: "Today budget monthly limit: ₹50,000 (default)" — a real, disclosed, reasoned placeholder, the same disclosed-choice pattern already established for `REFRESH_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS`.

**Built:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/today.py` (new) — `compute_capacity_state()`/`compute_budget_state()` (pure, hand-verified-parity ports of the mobile `computed_state.dart` logic already governing how these numbers render), `fetch_pending_actions()`/`fetch_active_negotiations()`/`fetch_today_capacity()`/`fetch_today_budget()` (four real, per-user-scoped live queries). `main.py`'s new `GET /today` route follows the established `_resolve_internal_user_id_or_404` + `Depends(_get_db_pool)` + `Depends(_require_auth)` pattern exactly, returning the full shape §5.4 specifies. `mobile/lib/api/today_api.dart` (new) mirrors `career_pipeline_api.dart`/`tasks_api.dart`'s established injected-async-function pattern exactly, with `_parseDataSource()` failing CLOSED to `DataSource.localMirror` on any unrecognized `source` value — the same fail-toward-less-confidence discipline `trust_logic.dart`'s `parseTarget()` and `trust_digest_logic.dart`'s `parseTrend()` already established. `main.dart` wires `fetchToday` into `MainShell` — the same fetcher-presence gating already governing the Trust tab (`DEC-106`) also unlocks the already-built, already-tested Holding Steady → Tasks drill-through link (`DEC-096`), reachable on screen for the first time.

**A real, disclosed, honest scope boundary, stated plainly rather than left implicit:** this closes the READ side only. Nothing in this backend currently invokes the Gate against a real, live user action — no email/calendar/task-monitoring pipeline exists to ever *produce* a row into `action_events` or `negotiations` in the first place. `GET /today` therefore genuinely, correctly returns empty `needs_you_now`/`in_motion` arrays in real production use right now — the same "correct but currently empty" result already established for `/trust_digest` (`DEC-100`), not a bug, not a placeholder standing in for missing work.

**Verified live, not assumed correct from the code looking right:** `pytest backend/tests -q` (run from `backend/`, `PYTHONPATH=src`) → **296 passed**, `ruff check backend` clean. `flutter analyze` → **No issues found!**; `flutter test` → **306 passed**. The backend was rebuilt via Cloud Build and redeployed to the real, live Cloud Run service (revision `quorum-backend-00006-k29`), confirmed live: unauthenticated `GET /health` → real `200`; unauthenticated `GET /today` → real `401` (the app-level gate correctly demanding auth, not a `404` of a route that doesn't exist).

**The real, decisive proof — an actual, human, on-device confirmation, not a structural one:** a fresh debug APK was installed on the real physical device (Realme RMX3853) already signed in since `DEC-118`, and the Today tab was confirmed rendering real, live data for the real signed-in account: `8.0h` capacity remaining, `100%` budget remaining (₹50,000 − ₹0 spent — the honest, correct answer for a real account with zero committed tasks/expenses today), "Nothing needs you right now" (an honest empty `needs_you_now`, exactly as this entry's own scope boundary predicted), and an empty "In motion" section. Confirmed as a genuine successful fetch rather than a masked failure by checking `today_screen.dart`'s own distinct error-path text (`"Couldn't load tasks: ..."`), which did not appear. The "View tasks" drill-through link, gated behind `fetchToday` being non-null since `MOBILE_23`, was visibly reachable on screen for the first time in this project's history.

**A real, disclosed process nit, honestly repeated from `DEC-118`, not newly discovered:** the live Cloud Run redeploy for this branch happened before the fresh-context review below ran, the same sequencing gap `DEC-118` already disclosed and recommended correcting going forward. This branch does not touch Gate/security/auth/secrets/irreversible-action code directly (per `CLAUDE.md` Rule 6's own scope), so a CRITICAL-tier cross-model review was not required here — but the review-before-deploy discipline should still have applied regardless of tier, and didn't. Noted here plainly rather than smoothed over.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** Search, Waiting On, and Memories all remain unbuilt (`DEC-109`'s own disclosed scope check — each needs real architecture this repository doesn't have: cross-content-type embedding/ranking, a `sent_messages`-style table, and a real `mem0` integration, respectively). The two `DEC-113` review nits (`STATUS_INDEX.md` #15/#16) remain open. The seven unrotated credentials from `DEC-114`/`STATUS_INDEX.md` #17 remain unrotated.

**Affects:** `backend/migrations/0004_today_persistence/` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/today.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (new route), `backend/tests/test_today_feature.py` (new, 21 tests), `backend/tests/test_main.py` (+4 tests), `backend/tests/test_trust_digest.py` (fixture fix for the new `NOT NULL` constraint), `specs/tier1_foundation/QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` (§4, two new rows), `mobile/lib/api/today_api.dart` (new), `mobile/test/today_api_test.dart` (new, 9 tests), `mobile/lib/main.dart` (`fetchToday` wired), the live Cloud Run deployment (new revision `quorum-backend-00006-k29`), the live Supabase database (migration `0004` applied), `STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-120: Roadmap Phase 4a — real, live Unified Fast Search, the backend's first real embedding pipeline, and a pre-merge review that genuinely changed the shipped design

**CRITICAL tier** — this session newly handles a real API credential (`GEMINI_API_KEY`) in a production code path, applies a migration to the live production database, and adds a per-user-scoped endpoint where an isolation bug would leak one real user's private content to another.

**A real, disclosed correction to this session's own first instinct, made before any code:** asked to continue with "the remaining" work (Search, Waiting On, Memories), the initial read was that Waiting On looked easiest. That was wrong, and checking rather than proceeding is what caught it — `specs/tier3_verification/IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP_2026-08-20.md`, already written in this repository, had already traced each one's real dependency chain: Search is genuinely the smallest (pgvector infrastructure exists since `DEC-098`, only code is missing), Memories needs a real `mem0` integration from zero, and Waiting On is the *deepest* of the three — real value requires actually sending real email through the Email agent, which requires expanding the OAuth scope past identity-only and persisting Gmail tokens, a boundary this project has deliberately held since `IMPL_12`. Search first, accordingly.

**Two real decisions escalated to Preethish rather than guessed at, both of which changed the plan:**
1. **Waiting On scope:** real Gmail sending, chosen explicitly over the lighter "track pending replies from data already inside Quorum" alternative. That makes it a genuine multi-session effort (OAuth scope expansion + token persistence + real send integration + the tracking table), and it is deliberately not folded into this session.
2. **Memories:** hosted `mem0` with agent-inferred memory creation (an agent writes a memory when it detects a durable preference during a real action), rather than self-hosted or explicit-only. Its own later session — and per this project's own credential discipline, the real `mem0` account/API key gets generated when that session actually runs, never provisioned speculatively ahead of need.

**A real, disclosed deviation from a "Locked" spec decision, decided by Preethish, logged here per Rule 3 rather than absorbed silently:** `QUORUM_MASTER_REFERENCE.md` §5 locks the embedding model as Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B. That model is wired nowhere in this backend, and its intended runtime (on-device vs. server-side) is specified nowhere either. Running a real ~0.6B-parameter model on serverless, scale-to-zero, free-tier Cloud Run is a genuine cold-start and resource risk against this project's own confirmed constraints, and `note_embeddings` already stores each item's raw `content` in Postgres directly — so on-device generation would buy no real privacy benefit here. Gemini's embedding API is used instead: an already-provisioned credential (`DEC-098`), no new external signup, a clean fit for this deployment's serverless shape. This closes `QUORUM_MASTER_REFERENCE.md`'s own open item #5 (the unconfirmed embedding dimension) as a side effect, and supersedes §5's locked model choice — recorded as a real, tracked open item in `STATUS_INDEX.md`, not left implicit in a code comment.

**The real model and dimension, confirmed live before anything hardcoded them — the spec's own standing caution earned itself twice:**
1. `text-embedding-004`, this session's first assumption (a commonly-cited Gemini embedding model name), returned a real, live **404** for this project's real API key and version. `ListModels` was then used to find the genuinely available models.
2. `gemini-embedding-001`'s real **default** output is 3072 dimensions, confirmed by a real `embedContent` call — and that default turned out to be **unusable here**, for a reason this session's first draft missed entirely. See the review findings below.

**What was built:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/core/embeddings.py` (new) — the backend's first real production call to an external LLM-family API (`orchestration.py`'s Stage B calls are, and remain, injected `Callable` dependencies, so no in-repo pattern for a real outbound call existed). `backend/migrations/0005_search_embeddings/` (new) — corrects `note_embeddings.embedding` from its never-real `VECTOR(1024)` placeholder, adds the `source_type`/`source_id` mapping that never existed, and adds a real HNSW index. `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/search.py` (new). A real, live `GET /search?q=...` in `main.py`, per-user scoped from its first line. `mobile/lib/api/search_api.dart` (new) plus `main.dart` wiring, reaching the already-built Search screen for the first time.

**A real architecture finding that drove the design, confirmed by direct search rather than assumed:** every real domain route in this backend is GET-only — there is no write path anywhere that creates a task, expense, or application. So there is no "on creation" moment to hook a real-time embedding write into. A **lazy backfill** runs at the start of every real `search()` call instead, idempotent by a real UNIQUE index rather than by application-level care alone.

**A real, disclosed extension, not a spec value:** `application` was added to `SearchItemType` (which `search_logic.dart` already documented as a "reasoned construction," never a literal spec value). Leaving an entire real domain — career applications — out of a feature whose own name is "*Unified* Fast Search" would have been a real, silent gap, not a faithful implementation.

**A real hypothesis raised and then DISPROVEN by testing it, recorded because the disproof is the useful part:** a failing test carrying real non-ASCII content looked like a serious, silent production bug — all eight `api/*.dart` files use `response.body`, this backend genuinely sends `content-type: application/json` with no `charset` (confirmed by real curl against the live URL), and package:http's own `encodingForCharset` documents a `latin1` fallback. Testing that rather than acting on it showed package:http 1.6.0 special-cases JSON to `utf8` (`lib/src/utils.dart`), so `response.body` has always been correct and **no production fix was needed anywhere**. The real cause was in the test itself. `mobile/test/utf8_decoding_proof_test.dart` (new) pins both halves of that real behavior down permanently, so a future package upgrade that changed it would fail loudly rather than silently corrupting real user-facing text.

**Two real, pre-existing test-fixture breaks found and fixed** — the same class of gap `DEC-119` fixed for `test_trust_digest.py`: `test_supabase_deletion_store.py`'s two `note_embeddings` INSERTs never supplied the new `NOT NULL` columns.
