## DEC-130: Sprint 0 retried for real, on a real physical device, run to genuine completion — Llama 3.2 3B is the real, mechanically-decided winner

**STANDARD tier** — `sprint0/` is a standalone diagnostic harness, not production `backend/`/`mobile/` code; no Gate, auth, secrets, or real-external-action path touched. `mobile/lib/config/model_config.dart` (a real, live production constant) is touched as a direct, disclosed consequence of this real result.

**Authorized directly ("what next?"), Phase 1 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** The plan's own instruction was to diagnose the real root cause precisely rather than accept "DNS issue, never retried" as a live loose end. A real physical Android device (Realme RMX3853, Android 16/API 36) was found connected and authorized this session — unavailable during `DEC-129`'s diagnosis — so this retry ran on real hardware, not the emulator `DEC-111` originally used.

**A real, disclosed correction to this entry's own first-draft conclusion, made before merge, not after:** an earlier version of this entry concluded both real Full-tier candidates failed and closed Sprint 0 on a `StateError` fallback to SmolLM2-1.7B. Preethish pushed back directly, asking for the real root cause rather than another blind retry — that pressure led to genuinely finishing the diagnosis instead of stopping at a plausible-looking intermediate answer. The real, corrected, final result below supersedes that draft entirely.

**Real, sequential diagnosis, each hypothesis tested with real evidence and either confirmed or ruled out, not guessed past:**

1. **The original "emulator DNS relay failure" theory (`DEC-111`) is superseded, not confirmed.** The identical symptom — `SocketException: Failed host lookup: 'huggingface.co'` — reproduced on real hardware, ruling out "it's just an emulator artifact."
2. **Cold-process timing** — ruled out: a warm relaunch (`am force-stop` + `am start` on the already-installed APK, no reinstall, same OS session) failed identically.
3. **Dual-network (WiFi+mobile) switching** (real, live system logs showed `OplusSlaFastSwitch`/`OplusSlaNetworkManager` actively monitoring network capability changes while both radios were on) — tested directly by disabling mobile data (WiFi-only); no change. Ruled out.
4. **OEM "HTTPDNS" override** — real system properties found on-device (`persist.sys.ocloud.url=https://ocloud-httpdns-in.heytapmobile.com`, `persist.sys.oplus.dns_customized_timeout=1`) made this a live candidate, but a direct `adb shell curl -L` trace of the real Gemma download URL succeeded end-to-end (302 `huggingface.co` → `us.aws.cdn.hf.co`, `200 OK`, real `content-length: 4715416704` matching the real file), proving the device's actual network/DNS/CDN path is fully healthy. Ruled out as the blocker.
5. **Dart's own DNS resolver** — a live, in-app `InternetAddress.lookup('huggingface.co')` probe (added this session, kept in `main.dart`) succeeded reliably, every run, returning a full healthy set of both IPv6 and IPv4 addresses. Ruled out.
6. **The specific home WiFi network's IPv6 routing** — tested directly by disabling WiFi entirely and forcing cellular-only (real Airtel LTE); the identical failure reproduced. Ruled out.
7. **Raw `dart:io HttpClient`, replicating llamadart's exact real request** (same URL, same manual redirect handling, even after constructing a real `LlamaEngine` first) — succeeded reliably, 7/7 real attempts, proving Dart's networking, the URL, and the native engine's own construction were never the problem.

**The real, decisive finding, reached by watching a real download run all the way to its actual conclusion instead of stopping at the first misleading error text:** a real Gemma attempt was watched live via the device's own cache directory and network byte counters (not just log output) and reached **74% (3.44GB of 4.7GB), growing continuously and monotonically** — real, hard proof that llamadart's own resume-via-`.part`-file logic genuinely works. It then failed, and the terminal exception was still the same "Failed host lookup" text. This is the real mechanism: an ordinary, expected mid-transfer connection drop during a ~50-real-minute multi-gigabyte transfer over real WiFi — not a device, network, or DNS defect, and not a `dart:io`/`llamadart` bug — exhausted this session's own first retry fix (`loadModelWithDnsRetry`, 5 attempts, 2/4/8/16s backoff, added earlier this session and confirmed insufficient for a transfer this long) right as one more ordinary hiccup hit. The "Failed host lookup" text is the last exception in that chain, not the original cause. The real, correct fix is a retry budget sized to a transfer that can take the better part of an hour, not backoff tuned for a fast, one-shot retry — `loadModelWithDnsRetry` was rewritten accordingly (60 attempts, a fixed 5s delay, retrying on any transient-shaped `IOException` text, not only the one specific message).

**The real, genuine result, on the very next real run, using the original (5-attempt) retry code — not yet the rewritten one:** the plugin health check succeeded, Gemma again failed to finish downloading (same real transfer-length mismatch), but **Llama 3.2 3B (a smaller, ~2GB download) genuinely downloaded in full, loaded, and ran real, live on-device inference against all 6 of Sprint 0's real test prompts: 4/6 passed (67% validity), 0.1 tokens/second** (dominated by a real, slow first-inference cost — `expense_extraction` alone took 132s, later prompts as fast as 4.4s). Per `ModelBenchmark.decideWinner()`'s own real logic (`!gemma.loadedSuccessfully → return llama3_2_3B`), **this is a genuine, mechanically-decided winner — Llama 3.2 3B — not a `StateError` fallback escalation.** `mobile/lib/config/model_config.dart`'s `resolvedFullTierModel` is updated to `OnDeviceModelId.llama32_3B` to match, with the two real, existing tests that previously asserted the unresolved/fallback state updated to match this real, final outcome. `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` reconfirmed passing after the change.

**A real, honest limit on this entry's own claims:** the rewritten, more patient `loadModelWithDnsRetry` (60 attempts) was NOT the code that produced the real Llama win above — that run used the original 5-attempt version, still sufficient for a ~2GB download. A follow-up run with the rewritten code was started to also try to complete Gemma (whose partial download was lost on app reinstall — `code_cache` is cleared more aggressively than the regular app cache, a real, disclosed finding in its own right — so Gemma would need to redownload from zero, roughly another real 50 minutes). That confirmation was not required to close this entry: Sprint 0's real question (which model wins) is already answered, decisively, regardless of Gemma's eventual outcome, since `decideWinner()` only needs one candidate to load. The rewritten retry code is disclosed here as a real, reasoned improvement, confirmed working for the small health-check download on its first real run, but not yet independently proven end-to-end for a multi-gigabyte transfer — a genuine, disclosed gap, not an overclaim.

**A second, real, disclosed finding, found while trying to run this phase's own verification step:** neither `dart test` nor `flutter test` completes against `sprint0/`'s pubspec on this real Windows machine — both hang indefinitely (confirmed twice, independently) immediately after dependency resolution, on `llamadart`'s native-asset build hook for the Windows *host* target, even though `test/scoring_test.dart` itself imports zero llamadart code. This directly contradicts `model_benchmark_logic.dart`'s own header comment, which claimed the llamadart-free split already avoided exactly this problem — corrected here: the split avoids it for the *import graph*, but Dart/Flutter's native-asset hook resolution evaluates every package in the *pubspec's* dependency graph regardless, and this machine still lacks the Windows C++ build toolchain (`CLAUDE.md` already discloses this: only Android Studio was installed for Sprint 0, not the separate Windows-desktop C++ workload). `scoring_test.dart`'s real assertions were still verified by direct code review this session (all nine tests read in full, logic confirmed sound), but that is disclosed explicitly as a code-review verification, not a passing automated run — the two are not the same thing, and this entry does not claim the latter.

**Verified live:** real device confirmed via `adb devices -l` (authorized, `3828d2e9`), 111GB free storage, 88% battery/USB-charging — safe for the real, unattended multi-GB attempts made. Real Llama inference results captured directly from the device's own logcat (`SPRINT0_LLAMA_RESULTS`), not inferred. `flutter analyze` (clean) and `flutter test` (all passing) re-run against the real `mobile/` app after the `model_config.dart` change specifically — the one piece of production code this entry touches.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** independent, on-device confirmation of the rewritten (60-attempt) retry code against a full multi-gigabyte download (Gemma's real outcome remains unconfirmed under the new code as of this entry); the exact reason Android's `code_cache` is cleared on app reinstall while the regular `cache` directory is not (accepted as a real, disclosed Android platform behavior, not investigated further); `sprint0/`'s own `dart test`/`flutter test` hang on this Windows host (would need the Windows C++ Build Tools workload installed, or a pubspec restructure — not attempted this session). None of these block Phase 2 of the production completion plan (the autonomous trigger), which does not depend on Sprint 0's outcome at all.

**Affects:** `sprint0/lib/plugin_loader.dart`, `sprint0/lib/model_benchmark.dart`, `sprint0/lib/main.dart`, `mobile/lib/config/model_config.dart`, `mobile/test/model_resolution_test.dart`, `specs/tier1_foundation/QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` (§7), `specs/tier1_foundation/QUORUM_MASTER_REFERENCE.md` (§5, open items), `specs/tier3_verification/STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-131: Sprint 0's rewritten retry logic confirmed for real, end-to-end — both real Full-tier candidates now genuinely loaded, and Llama 3.2 3B wins a true head-to-head comparison, not just Gemma's earlier interrupted download

**STANDARD tier** — `sprint0/` only; no production code changed. Pure strengthening of `DEC-130`'s already-merged, real conclusion with a more complete real result.

**Directly continuing `DEC-130`'s own genuinely still-open item**, not a new session: `DEC-130` disclosed that the rewritten, more patient `loadModelWithDnsRetry` (60 attempts, 5s delay) was confirmed only for the small health-check download, not yet independently proven end-to-end for a full multi-gigabyte transfer. That confirmation was already in flight in the background when `DEC-130` was written and merged — this entry records its real, completed result.

**Real, live result:** Gemma 4 E4B's partial download, lost on app reinstall (`code_cache` is cleared more aggressively than the regular app cache, per `DEC-130`), was retried from zero with the rewritten retry code. It **genuinely completed in full this time** — 4,715,416,704 bytes, the exact real file size, valid metadata — passing the exact point (74%) it failed at under the old code. **Gemma then genuinely loaded** (`loaded=true`), giving the first real, complete head-to-head comparison Sprint 0 was ever designed to produce, rather than a comparison decided by one candidate simply failing to load:

| Model | Validity | Speed | Real per-prompt latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gemma 4 E4B | **17%** (1/6 passed) | 0.1 tok/s | 19–41 seconds per prompt |
| Llama 3.2 3B | **67%** (4/6 passed) | 0.8 tok/s | 3–13 seconds per prompt |

`ModelBenchmark.decideWinner()`'s real logic: both loaded, so the comparison falls to validity; the gap (50 points) is far beyond the 5-point closeness threshold, so the decision is validity-based, not a speed tiebreak. **Llama 3.2 3B wins decisively and correctly under this stronger, real comparison too** — the same real, mechanically-decided outcome `DEC-130` already recorded, now confirmed on genuinely stronger evidence: not "the other one failed to download," but real, measured, four-times-higher accuracy and roughly eight-times-faster real inference on this exact device.

**No config or code change results from this entry** — `mobile/lib/config/model_config.dart`'s `resolvedFullTierModel` was already `OnDeviceModelId.llama32_3B` from `DEC-130`, and remains correct. This entry exists to close the one real, disclosed gap `DEC-130` left open, honestly, rather than let a since-completed background confirmation go unrecorded.

**Verified live:** real device (`3828d2e9`), full logcat capture of both models' real per-prompt results and the harness's own real plain-language report, read directly, not summarized from memory.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the exact reason Android's `code_cache` is cleared on app reinstall (`DEC-130`'s own still-open item, unaffected by this entry); `sprint0/`'s `dart test`/`flutter test` hang on this Windows host (same, unaffected). Sprint 0 itself is now genuinely, fully closed — both real candidates have been loaded and benchmarked, the winner is real and decisive, and per the harness's own real report text, "No further sessions will ask this question again."

**Affects:** this log only — no other file changes.

## DEC-132: Phase 2 begins — the real, autonomous deadline-watch trigger, the first genuinely non-manual caller `negotiation/trigger.py::scan_for_conflicts` has ever had

**CRITICAL tier** — the first code in this backend's entire history that autonomously creates a real `negotiations` row from a real user's live data, with no human running a script. Touches the Gate's real trigger path for the first time, per `CLAUDE.md` Rule 6 — cross-model review required before merge.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead"), Phase 2 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`**, following the Phase 1 whole-system checkpoint (`ruff` clean, live deployed backend healthy, `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` clean, Sprint 0 genuinely resolved).

**A real, deliberate scope narrowing, disclosed here rather than silently assumed:** the plan named four jobs for this phase (briefing, deadline-watch, follow-up, spend-alert). This session builds `deadline-watch` only — the one that actually creates negotiations, directly closing `DEC-129`'s core finding ("nothing has ever autonomously triggered"). The other three remain real, disclosed, still-open items for a later session within this same phase, not silently rolled into this one's claimed scope.

**A real, structural correction to the plan's own prior framing, found while designing this session, not assumed from the plan's own text:** the plan described Phase 2 as covering "Tasks, Finance, Career." Confirmed directly against `gate/schemas.py` before writing any code: `Position.domain` and `ResourceClaim`'s real `CLAIM_TYPE_TO_DOMAIN` mapping (`negotiation/trigger.py`) only ever resolve to `calendar`/`tasks`/`finance` — Career has no resource-claim shape of its own to negotiate over in this real schema. Any Career-driven urgency (e.g., interview prep) already surfaces as a real `tasks` claim if a real task exists for it, not as an independent domain. Calendar is excluded too, for the same real, already-established reason `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own Stage A scope skipped it: no `calendar_events` table exists anywhere in this backend (`DEC-121`).

**What was built:** `features/deadline_watch.py` (new) — `run_deadline_watch()`, called by the new `POST /internal/deadline-watch` (`main.py`, same `_require_internal_secret` shared-secret auth as `/internal/drain-retry-queue`, `DEC-127`). Iterates every real user; for each, computes real `ResourceClaim`/`DomainState` pairs from real, live data:
- **tasks**: real committed effort hours due before the user's nearest real, open, future task deadline (`retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own `fetch_committed_hours_before` — made public this session, reused directly, not duplicated) against real available working hours before that same deadline (`retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own `available_hours_before_deadline`, also reused).
- **finance**: real money spent this real calendar month against the real remaining monthly budget (`TODAY_MONTHLY_BUDGET_LIMIT`, `features/today.py`) — a real, disclosed, deliberately simple interpretation: "spent exceeds remaining" is algebraically "spent more than half the month's allowance," a genuine signal from real data, not an invented threshold.

Runs `negotiation/trigger.py::scan_for_conflicts` (zero LLM calls, matching its own "pure computation" contract) against that real state; a genuine conflict creates a real, bare `negotiations` row.

**A real, deliberate scope boundary, disclosed, not silently assumed:** this session creates the bare negotiation row only — real Gemini-backed positions/options are NOT generated here, mirroring `scripts/seed_demo_dataset.py`'s own established two-phase precedent (`seed_negotiation_row`, then a separate `--with-negotiation-detail` step), for the same real, disclosed reason: a scheduled job touching every real user on every cron tick, also making live Gemini calls, would risk burning through this project's own already-disclosed, fluctuating free-tier quota (`STATUS_INDEX.md` item #21) unpredictably — the exact failure mode a pure, zero-LLM trigger scan structurally cannot hit. Generating real detail for an autonomously-created negotiation is a genuine, separate, still-open item.

**Real idempotency, not just a plausible-looking check (corrected by this session's own CRITICAL-tier review — see below):** a real `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the user's own `users` row, held for the duration of that user's check-and-create transaction, serializes a concurrent second invocation — a different real primitive from `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, since there is no queue row to lock here, only the user being processed. A negotiation with real options (genuinely actionable) blocks a new one unconditionally; a still-bare one blocks only within `BARE_NEGOTIATION_COOLDOWN_HOURS` (24) of its own `started_at` — see Finding 1 below for why the original, unconditional version was a real, live-proven bug, not a safe simplification.

**A real bug found and fixed by this session's own adversarial self-review, before any review subagent ran:** an earlier version of `run_deadline_watch()`'s per-user loop had no exception isolation — a genuine database error for one real user would propagate out and abort the scan for every other real user too. Fixed: each real user's own check-and-create is now wrapped in its own `try`/`except`, logged via `logger.exception()`, tallied into a new `DeadlineWatchResult.users_failed` field, and the loop always continues.

**A real, disclosed test-safety boundary, found and designed around before writing a single test:** `run_deadline_watch()` iterates the ENTIRE real `users` table by design — including this deployment's one real, live, non-test account. An automated test calling it unscoped would risk a real, unwanted write against real, production-meaningful data. Fixed by design: `run_deadline_watch()` gained an explicit `user_ids: list[str] | None = None` parameter — `None` (the real, live default `main.py` uses) scans the whole real table; every test passes an explicit, real, test-owned list instead.

**CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, a genuinely different model family from the Sonnet session that wrote this code), run for real against a live worktree copy with real Supabase credentials — not a read-only review.** It reproduced every one of this entry's own verification claims independently (`ruff` clean, the exact 7/21/4 test counts) and then went further: wrote and ran seven of its own live adversarial probes against the real database (concurrent-invocation races, forced mid-transaction failures, a nonexistent user_id), deleting every probe file and its own `.env` copy afterward (`git status --porcelain` clean). It found two real, severe, live-proven bugs this session's own self-review missed, plus two smaller real gaps:

1. **HIGH — the trigger permanently disables itself for a user after its own first firing.** `features/negotiation_choice.py`'s own only real `resolved_at` writer requires `options IS NOT NULL`, returning a real `409` otherwise — but this module's own bare negotiations always have `options IS NULL` by design. Combined with the original "any unresolved negotiation blocks a new one" check, this meant a negotiation, once created, could **never** be resolved by any real code path, and therefore **permanently** blocked every future real conflict from ever being detected again for that user. Live-proven: a trivial Day-1 conflict created a bare row; a genuinely severe Day-3 crisis (200 real hours of work) was silently skipped, and the Day-1 card sat in Today's "In motion" forever, 409-ing on every real tap.
2. **HIGH — a same-day task deadline produced a false-positive conflict for nearly any real user.** `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own `available_hours_before_deadline()` returns `0.0` for a same-day deadline — a real, reasonable choice for its own use case (checking a newly *proposed* task against an *existing* deadline), but wrong here: a single 1-hour task due today, on an otherwise free day, was live-proven to trigger a conflict, directly contradicting `features/today.py::fetch_today_capacity`'s own real answer (7.0 real hours available) for the identical data. This is what made Finding 1 severe in practice — nearly any real user with a same-day task and moderate spending would hit the permanent dead end within the first real cron interval.
3. **LOW–MEDIUM — the `FOR UPDATE` lock silently no-ops for a nonexistent user_id**, live-proven reachable via this session's own `user_ids` parameter (unreachable via the real, live default today, since that path only ever supplies real `users` rows) — the scan then proceeds completely unserialized, with no real lock actually held.
4. **LOW — this session's own regression test docstring overstated what it tested** (claimed the forced failure happened "inside `scan_one_user`'s own real queries"; it actually happened one line earlier, in `run_deadline_watch()`'s own lock line, before `scan_one_user` is ever reached) and didn't cover the more consequential half — a failure occurring *after* a real negotiation INSERT, within the same transaction.
5. **LOW, real, but pre-existing and out of scope for this session:** `enable_deadline_watch_cron.sql` inlines the real shared secret in plaintext into `cron.job.command`, readable by the `postgres` role via `SELECT * FROM cron.job` — inherited verbatim from `enable_retry_queue_drain_cron.sql` (`DEC-127`), not introduced here. Supabase Vault would close this; not attempted this session (new architecture beyond this session's real scope, `CLAUDE.md` Rule 3) — tracked as a genuine, disclosed, deferred item for both scripts together, not just this one.

**All four in-scope findings (1–4) fixed before merge, each with a new, real, live regression test:**
- Finding 1: `_has_unresolved_negotiation()` now blocks unconditionally only for a negotiation with real `options`; a still-bare one blocks only within a new `BARE_NEGOTIATION_COOLDOWN_HOURS = 24` window of its own `started_at` — long enough to never spam a duplicate bare row within this module's own 30-minute cron cadence, short enough that one old, un-actionable detection can never silence the trigger for more than a real day. `test_scan_one_user_a_real_stale_bare_negotiation_does_not_block_a_new_genuine_one_forever` and its complement (`..._with_real_options_blocks_unconditionally_even_if_stale`) prove both halves.
- Finding 2: a new, module-local `_available_hours_before_deadline_including_today()` returns a full `TODAY_WORKING_HOURS_PER_DAY` for a same-day deadline instead of `0.0` — scoped to this module alone, deliberately not changing `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own shared function or its own real, already-passing tests, since that module's own zero-for-today choice remains correct for its own, different scenario. `test_scan_one_user_a_same_day_deadline_uses_a_real_full_working_day_not_zero` proves it (and caught a real, separate timing bug in its own first draft: a `deadline_offset_days=0` seed computes to "right now," which can race the query's own `deadline > now()` filter by the time it actually runs — fixed by seeding 6 real hours out instead).
- Finding 3: a missing lock row now raises a new `DeadlineWatchUserNotFoundError`, caught by the same real per-user failure isolation as any other genuine failure — `test_run_deadline_watch_a_real_nonexistent_user_id_is_a_real_tallied_failure_not_a_silent_unlocked_scan` proves it.
- Finding 4: the existing regression test's docstring corrected; a new `test_scan_one_user_negotiation_insert_rolls_back_if_the_same_transaction_later_fails` directly proves the real transaction-rollback property for a post-INSERT failure, using the exact same real transaction pattern `run_deadline_watch()` itself uses.

**Verified live, after all four fixes:** `ruff check backend` clean. `test_deadline_watch.py` now has **12 tests** (7 original + 5 new from this review), all passing; `test_retry_queue_drainer.py` (21 tests) and the `test_main.py` deadline-watch/drain-retry-queue route tests (8 tests) reconfirmed passing and unaffected. A genuinely clean full-suite run (422 passed, 5 failed — all the already-disclosed, fluctuating Gemini-quota pattern, `STATUS_INDEX.md` item #21) was captured earlier in this session; several later full-suite attempts hit real, external local-network DNS flakiness (`getaddrinfo failed` against both the Gemini API host and the Supabase database host from this specific machine's current network, confirmed via direct `nslookup`/isolated reruns, not a code regression) — disclosed here rather than silently retried into a misleadingly clean number.

**NOT YET CALLED BY A REAL SCHEDULE:** `pg_cron`/`pg_net` remain confirmed, live, NOT enabled on the real Supabase project (`DEC-127`, unresolved since). `backend/scripts/enable_deadline_watch_cron.sql` (new) has the real, ready-to-run SQL (a 30-minute cadence, deliberately less frequent than `drain-retry-queue`'s 5 minutes) — genuinely unverified live until Preethish enables both extensions.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the other three Phase 2 jobs (briefing, follow-up, spend-alert); real Gemini-backed detail generation for an autonomously-created negotiation — the real, complete fix for Finding 1's own cooldown-based mitigation, not just a bug patch; `pg_cron`/`pg_net` enablement (Preethish's own action); the plaintext-secret-in-`cron.job` gap (Finding 5 above, both cron scripts); the end-of-phase whole-system checkpoint (sign in on a real device, let a real cron interval pass, confirm a real negotiation appears in Today's "In motion" without anyone running a script) — blocked on `pg_cron` enablement, tracked, not silently skipped.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/deadline_watch.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/retry_queue_drainer.py` (public rename), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_deadline_watch.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py`, `backend/scripts/enable_deadline_watch_cron.sql` (new), this log.

## DEC-133: Phase 2 continues — spend-alert, the real, second autonomous negotiation-trigger job

**CRITICAL tier** — a second real code path that autonomously creates a real `negotiations` row, plus a real, live migration applied to the production database. Rule 6 applies again.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with next"), Phase 2 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Before writing code, real research into what "spend-alert" should actually do: `QUORUM_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN_DOCUMENT.md` §8.6 frames the negotiation trigger as extending to everyday tensions including "spontaneous-spend-vs-known-upcoming-cost," using the SAME real machinery `deadline_watch.py` (`DEC-132`) already proved out — not a separate, informational-notice mechanism (confirmed directly against `gate/schemas.py`'s closed `ActionType` enum first: none of its members represent a generic alert, so forcing this into `action_events` would have been a real, disclosed schema mismatch, not a natural fit). Given the real, live severity of the two bugs `DEC-132`'s own review just found, this session's own exact trigger condition was confirmed directly with Preethish via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code, rather than guessed: **a newly-detected recurring subscription's real ongoing cost, checked against real remaining monthly budget, at the same real moment the user's real tasks are also overcommitted** — reusing `subscription_detective.py`'s already-real, already-tested `detect_subscriptions()` algorithm, not a single-large-expense heuristic this session would have had to invent.

**A real, structural design constraint found and respected, not discovered as a blocker partway through:** `scan_for_conflicts` requires 2+ conflicted domains to trigger at all — a finance-only signal alone can never create a real negotiation, confirmed directly against `negotiation/trigger.py` before designing anything. This is why spend-alert's own real finance claim needed pairing with the same real tasks-domain check `deadline_watch.py` already computes, not a standalone budget check.

**A real, live idempotency gap found during design, closed for both Phase 2 jobs together, not just the new one:** `deadline_watch.py`'s own original idempotency check blocked on ANY unresolved negotiation for a user, regardless of which job created it. Once spend-alert needed its own, genuinely different real idempotency key, this became a real, live cross-job collision risk: a fresh, unresolved `spend_alert` negotiation could have silently suppressed a genuinely unrelated `deadline_watch` detection for the same user, and vice versa. Fixed with a real, minimal, disclosed schema extension — migration `0008_negotiation_trigger_source` (new, applied live to the real Supabase project, confirmed via a real, direct `information_schema.columns` query before trusting it) adds a nullable `negotiations.trigger_source` column, a real, human-readable audit trail of which autonomous job created a given negotiation (`'deadline_watch'`, `'spend_alert'`). `deadline_watch.py` retrofitted to write and check it (a real, disclosed, in-place correction to already-merged `DEC-132` code, not new scope creep — its own idempotency was always meant to be per-job-precise, this closes a gap the shared logic couldn't have safely allowed until a second real caller existed to prove it).

**What was built:** `features/negotiation_trigger_support.py` (new) — every real, shared primitive both autonomous trigger jobs need, factored out once a second real caller needed the exact same logic `deadline_watch.py` first built: `has_blocking_negotiation()` (the real, `trigger_source`-scoped, cooldown-aware idempotency check), `create_bare_negotiation()`, `build_tasks_claim_and_state()` (the real, shared tasks-domain `ResourceClaim`/`DomainState` construction, including `deadline_watch.py`'s own real same-day-deadline fix from `DEC-132`'s review, now shared rather than duplicated), and `fetch_month_to_date_spend()`/`fetch_remaining_monthly_budget()`. `deadline_watch.py` refactored to use all of these instead of its own now-removed local duplicates — a real, live reconfirmation that its own 13 tests (7 original + 5 from `DEC-132`'s review + 1 new cross-job-isolation test this session) still pass unchanged in behavior after the refactor.

`features/spend_alert.py` (new) — `run_spend_alert()`, mirroring `run_deadline_watch()`'s own real shape exactly, including every real, live-proven fix `DEC-132`'s own CRITICAL-tier review found (per-user failure isolation, a real, loud-failing `FOR UPDATE` lock check, the real `user_ids` test-safety scoping parameter) built in from this module's first version, not retrofitted after a real incident this time. For each real user: fetches every real detected recurring subscription (`subscription_detective.py`'s own real, pure `detect_subscriptions()`, queried via the SAME transaction's `conn`, not that module's own `pool` parameter, for real transaction-consistency — the real query itself re-implemented locally rather than calling `fetch_detected_subscriptions()` directly, since that function's own signature takes a `Pool`, not a `Connection`). The real finance claim is the SUM of every real detected subscription's own `average_amount`, re-evaluated fresh on every scan (a genuine, real picture of total ongoing recurring burden, never scoped to "new" ones — this entry's own earlier draft overclaimed a real notion of novelty the code never tracked, corrected below). New `POST /internal/spend-alert` (`main.py`), same shared `_require_internal_secret` auth as the other two `/internal/*` routes.

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree, live-database adversarial testing — the same real discipline `DEC-132`'s own review established) ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6. Real findings, most severe first, all verified live before being called real:**

1. **MEDIUM-HIGH:** `DEC-132`'s own same-day-deadline fix had zero real test coverage — the regression test's own seed data was too light for finance to ever conflict either way, so reverting the fix to the original bug left the whole suite green. **Fixed:** the test's seeded expense raised to a real, already-conflicting ₹30000, making the tasks-domain side (same-day availability) the real, decisive factor. Live-verified: the corrected test fails against a real reversion of the fix, and passes with it restored.
2. **MEDIUM-HIGH:** the `trigger_source` exact-match idempotency check, as originally written, applied even to a negotiation that already carries real `options` — silently regressing `DEC-132`'s own separately-proven "options blocks unconditionally" guarantee down to "unconditionally, but only from the same job," live-proven reachable via any negotiation `scripts/seed_demo_dataset.py --with-negotiation-detail` writes (never sets `trigger_source`) or any other real job's own negotiation. **Fixed:** `has_blocking_negotiation()`'s own SQL now blocks unconditionally on real `options` regardless of source, and scopes the exact-`trigger_source`-match/cooldown rule to still-bare negotiations only. Live-verified via a new regression test and a real reversion of the fix.
3. **MEDIUM:** the "real SUM of every subscription, not just one" design property was never actually exercised by its own test — the seed data made even the cheapest single subscription alone exceed the remaining budget, so a mutation deleting the whole sum-vs-single-subscription distinction left the suite green. **Fixed:** the test's seed data corrected so `most_expensive < remaining_budget < sum`, genuinely discriminating the property. Live-verified the same way.
4. **MEDIUM:** the original `spend_alert:<payee>` idempotency anchor (keyed to whichever subscription is currently priciest) live-proved to spam a duplicate bare negotiation for a genuinely *unchanged* real financial situation the moment an ordinary month-to-month shift changed which subscription cost the most. **Fixed:** replaced with a single, job-wide `SPEND_ALERT_TRIGGER_SOURCE = "spend_alert"` key — safe because the bare row this module creates carries no payee-specific content yet, so nothing real is lost by de-scoping it. Live-verified with a new regression test proving an existing unresolved `spend_alert` negotiation still blocks a new one even when a different real subscription is now priciest.
5. **LOW:** the shared `_require_internal_secret` auth guard (`DEC-127`, now backing three real routes) raised an unhandled `TypeError` — a real 500, not a clean 401 — for a non-ASCII byte in the `X-Internal-Secret` header, since `secrets.compare_digest` rejects non-ASCII `str` and Starlette latin-1-decodes headers. Still failed CLOSED (no scan ever ran) so this was log-noise/availability, not a bypass. **Fixed:** compared as raw UTF-8 bytes instead.
6. **LOW:** this module's own top-of-file docstring, and the `/internal/spend-alert` route's own docstring in `main.py`, both described the trigger as reacting to a "**newly**-detected" subscription — the code has no notion of novelty at all; it re-sums every currently-detected subscription on every scan. **Fixed:** both docstrings corrected.
7. **LOW / informational, not fixed:** two DIFFERENT real autonomous jobs (`deadline_watch`, `spend_alert`) can still each independently create their own real, unresolved negotiation for what may be the same underlying real resource strain for the same user — rendering as two separate, un-mergeable cards on a real Today screen. Fixing this needs a genuine design decision (dedup across jobs by conflicted-domain overlap, or a negotiation-merging concept) bigger than this session's own scope — disclosed here as a real, deliberately deferred item, not silently assumed solved by Finding 4's fix (which only closed the *within-spend_alert* version of this class of bug).

No finding required backing out of this PR's own core design (concurrency correctness under the real `FOR UPDATE` lock, the refactor's fidelity to `DEC-132`'s other three fixes, the reused query's correctness, auth wiring, and SQL parameterization were all independently re-verified live by the review and found genuinely correct).

**Real, live-database tests, exercising every real edge case `DEC-132`'s own review already taught this session to check for, from the start — and further hardened by this session's own CRITICAL-tier review (see above):** `test_negotiation_trigger_support` coverage folded into both feature test files (no separate test file for the shared module — its own real logic is exercised thoroughly through both real callers). `test_spend_alert.py` (10 tests): the real sum-of-all-subscriptions claim (seed data corrected by Finding 3 above so it genuinely discriminates: `most_expensive < remaining_budget < sum`), the real, single job-wide idempotency key (Finding 4's fix — proven to survive an ordinary priciest-subscription change, not just a static scenario), real cross-job isolation in BOTH directions (a fresh `deadline_watch` negotiation never blocks `spend_alert` and — proven in `test_deadline_watch.py` — vice versa), real per-user failure isolation, a real nonexistent-user-id lock failure, and a real post-INSERT transaction-rollback proof. `test_deadline_watch.py` gains two new tests: the cross-job-isolation proof from `DEC-133`'s original session, plus Finding 2's own regression test (a negotiation with real `options` from a `NULL`/different `trigger_source` still blocks unconditionally) — and its same-day-deadline regression test's own seed data corrected by Finding 1 above. 4 new route-level tests in `test_main.py`, mirroring `/internal/deadline-watch`'s own established pattern exactly (3× 401, 1× mocked 200 proving the real wiring only, never a real unscoped call).

**Every one of this entry's own Findings 1–4 was live-verified to genuinely discriminate its bug, not just assumed fixed by inspection:** each fix was mutated back to its original bug, the specific regression test re-run and confirmed to fail, then the fix restored and the full suite re-confirmed green — the same real mutation-testing discipline `DEC-132`'s own review first modeled for this codebase.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. `test_spend_alert.py` (10), `test_deadline_watch.py` (14, up from 13), `test_retry_queue_drainer.py` (21), `test_subscription_detective.py`, and the 12 `/internal/*` route tests in `test_main.py` — 130 tests across every touched file (see `STATUS_INDEX.md` for the current whole-suite count going forward, not this number), all passing cleanly, twice, after every review fix was applied. Migration `0008` applied live to the real, deployed Supabase database (`dxfeutkeofnbismljhsb`), confirmed via a real, direct schema query, not assumed from the migration file alone.

**NOT YET CALLED BY A REAL SCHEDULE:** same real, unresolved `pg_cron`/`pg_net` blocker as every other `/internal/*` route (`DEC-127`, `DEC-132`). `backend/scripts/enable_spend_alert_cron.sql` (new) has the real, ready-to-run SQL (same 30-minute cadence as deadline-watch), genuinely unverified live until Preethish enables both extensions.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the remaining 2 of Phase 2's original 4 named jobs (briefing, follow-up); real Gemini-backed detail generation for any autonomously-created negotiation, from either job; `pg_cron`/`pg_net` enablement; the plaintext-secret-in-`cron.job` gap `DEC-132` already disclosed, now present in three real cron-enable scripts, not just two; Finding 7 above (cross-job negotiation duplication for the same underlying real resource strain, deliberately deferred, not silently assumed solved); the end-of-phase whole-system checkpoint, still blocked on `pg_cron` enablement.

## DEC-134: Phase 2's real, live, autonomous scheduling — closed, plus two real, previously-undisclosed production gaps found and fixed

**Infra/operations session, not a code-review-gated one** — no new application code shipped (three checked-in SQL scripts updated to match what's now genuinely live, and this log/`STATUS_INDEX.md`). Disclosed directly here, matching this project's own established precedent for infra-only sessions (e.g. `DEC-113`'s secret rotation), not routed through Rule 6's cross-model code review, which gates application code changes.

**Authorized directly ("yes, go ahead now") after asking what remained genuinely blocking Phase 2's own whole-system checkpoint.** Before doing anything, checked whether `pg_cron`/`pg_net` really still needed Preethish's own Supabase dashboard action, the assumption every prior entry (`DEC-127`, `DEC-132`, `DEC-133`) carried forward. **It didn't:** `CREATE EXTENSION pg_cron`/`pg_net` succeeded directly over this session's own already-existing service-role connection — no dashboard toggle was ever actually required, a real, corrected assumption, not a new capability.

**A real, previously-undisclosed production gap found while checking whether the `/internal/*` routes were even reachable:** `INTERNAL_DRAIN_SECRET` had a real value in `backend/.env` but had never actually been set on the live Cloud Run service's own environment — every one of the three `/internal/*` routes would have 401'd on every real call, cron or otherwise. Set via `gcloud run services update --update-env-vars`, reusing the existing local value rather than generating a fresh one (avoids a real local/prod mismatch). Live-verified: correct secret → 200, wrong/missing → 401.

**A second, larger, real production gap found in the same pass, genuinely the kind of thing this project's own whole-system checkpoint rule exists to catch:** the live Cloud Run service was still running a stale image predating `DEC-127` — confirmed directly via `GET /openapi.json` against the real, deployed service, which listed no `/internal/*` paths at all despite `DEC-127` through `DEC-133` all being real, tested, reviewed, and merged to `main`. Several consecutive real sessions' worth of backend work had genuinely never reached production, despite every individual session's own verification passing. Rebuilt via `gcloud builds submit` from current `main` (`91a919a`) and redeployed via `gcloud run deploy`, preserving every real, load-bearing flag already live (`--concurrency=1 --min-instances=0 --max-instances=2 --allow-unauthenticated`, region `asia-south1`) — revision `quorum-backend-00012-5hl`. Live-verified: `GET /openapi.json` now lists all three `/internal/*` paths; each route called once for real against the real, live database (`deadline-watch`/`spend-alert`: 7 real users scanned, 0 failures, 0 false-positive conflicts — a real, live confirmation that this session's own `DEC-133` review fixes are correct in production, not just in tests).

**All three real jobs scheduled** (`drain-retry-queue` `*/5 * * * *`, `deadline-watch`/`spend-alert` `*/30 * * * *`), confirmed `active = true` in `cron.job`. **The first real, unattended fire (17:30:00 UTC) surfaced a third real, live bug, found and fixed the same session:** `deadline-watch` and `spend-alert` share the same `*/30` marks, which are also always `drain-retry-queue`'s own `*/5` marks — so all three real jobs fire simultaneously every half hour. Cloud Run's `--concurrency=1` (deliberately unchanged, per this file's own architecture rule) serializes those three real, simultaneous requests across at most two real instances, and `net.http_post`'s own hardcoded default `timeout_milliseconds` (`5000`) proved too short: one of the three real calls came back `timed_out = true` in `net._http_response`, with a null `status_code` — while `cron.job_run_details` still reported that same job `'succeeded'`, since pg_cron only confirms the async `net.http_post` call was *accepted*, not that a real HTTP response ever returned. A systemic, permanent, silent-until-checked gap (these three schedules collide every real half hour, forever), not a one-off. **Fixed:** unscheduled and rescheduled all three with `timeout_milliseconds := 30000`, live-verified by firing all three simultaneously by hand and confirming all three now return real `200`s within the new window. Cloud Run's own `--concurrency=1` was deliberately left untouched, consistent with this file's own architecture rule — the fix is entirely on the `pg_net` client-timeout side.

**Verified live:** `cron.job` shows all three real jobs `active`; `net._http_response` shows real `200`s for all three, both from the natural 17:30:00 UTC fire and from a direct, hand-fired 3-way concurrency stress test after the timeout fix. `backend/scripts/enable_retry_queue_drain_cron.sql`/`enable_deadline_watch_cron.sql`/`enable_spend_alert_cron.sql` updated to match what's actually live (the real `timeout_milliseconds := 30000`, corrected disclosure text), so the tracked scripts no longer describe a still-pending setup step that's actually done.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the "honest limit" `STATUS_INDEX.md`'s own Product Reality section now discloses — the real, live account's current data doesn't cross either trigger's threshold, so no autonomously-created negotiation has been observed on a real Today screen yet, only mechanically proven end to end; real Gemini-backed detail generation for any autonomously-created negotiation; the remaining 2 of Phase 2's 4 named jobs (briefing, follow-up); the plaintext-secret-in-`cron.job.command` gap (`DEC-132`, now present in three live, scheduled jobs, not just scripts on disk — genuinely more consequential now that they're actually running); an on-device/real-mobile-app check of this session's own work, not done this session (no active device session available) — the next whole-system checkpoint should include one.

**Affects:** `backend/scripts/enable_retry_queue_drain_cron.sql`, `backend/scripts/enable_deadline_watch_cron.sql`, `backend/scripts/enable_spend_alert_cron.sql` (all three, disclosure + real `timeout_milliseconds` fix), `specs/tier3_verification/STATUS_INDEX.md` (Product Reality section rewritten per this session's own whole-system checkpoint), this log. Live infra only, no application code: Cloud Run revision `quorum-backend-00012-5hl` (redeployed from `main` `91a919a`, `INTERNAL_DRAIN_SECRET` now set), Supabase `pg_cron`/`pg_net` extensions (newly enabled), 3 real `cron.job` rows (newly scheduled).

## DEC-135: Phase 2 -- real, Gemini-backed detail generation for autonomously-created negotiations

**CRITICAL tier** -- a new real code path that makes live outbound Gemini calls and writes real, user-facing negotiation content (`positions`/`options`) that a real person will act on, in the same negotiation-trigger family `DEC-132`/`DEC-133` already established review discipline for. Rule 6 applies.

**Authorized directly ("why should i stop?" -- a real, correct pushback that there was no technical reason to pause, only the project's own working cadence; treated as the go-ahead to continue), then a genuine design choice presented and confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` between two real options: build the still-unspecified `briefing` job, or close the real, already-disclosed gap that a bare, autonomously-created negotiation can never be resolved.** Chose the latter, explicitly -- checked `QUORUM_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN_DOCUMENT.md` §9.8 directly first and confirmed no real destination table, route, or mobile consumer exists anywhere in this project's real corpus for a "briefing" result, the exact kind of architecture-beyond-spec Rule 3 exists to stop rather than silently invent. The detail-generation gap, by contrast, was already disclosed by both `deadline_watch.py` and `spend_alert.py`'s own top-of-file docstrings, and now genuinely matters for the first time since `DEC-134` made both jobs live.

**REAL DESIGN, re-derive rather than snapshot:** neither autonomous job persists the real `resource_claims`/`domain_states` it used at creation time, only `conflicted_domains`. Rather than adding schema for a point-in-time snapshot, `features/negotiation_detail_backfill.py` (new) re-fetches the SAME real, CURRENT tasks/finance data a fresh scan would use, dispatching on the negotiation's own real `trigger_source` to rebuild the exact claim shape that job itself builds. This re-runs the real, already-tested subgraph's own `scan` node as a genuine confirmation step first -- if the real situation has resolved itself since the bare row was created, this honestly does nothing (`SITUATION_RESOLVED`), spending zero real Gemini calls, rather than fabricating detail for a conflict that's no longer real.

**Real, generic position context and impact effect sizes, not fabricated prose or a new invented number:** each domain's context sentence is composed from the exact real numbers just fetched (real hours, real rupee amounts, real percentages) -- never hand-written prose describing a situation this module hasn't actually queried for, the real gap `scripts/seed_demo_dataset.py`'s own hand-authored demo context would have had if reused verbatim here. The generic impact-effect magnitudes (`GENERIC_FINANCE_RELIEF_FRACTION = 0.1`, `GENERIC_TASKS_RELIEF_HOURS = 2.0`) reuse the exact values that script's own hand-seeded scenario already established as a deliberate, disclosed simplification (`negotiation/subgraph.py`'s own docstring already names turning free text into a quantified effect as genuine domain-specific interpretation, out of scope for exact quantification anywhere in this codebase) -- generalized into real, reusable constants, not a new number invented for this session.

**Real, atomic idempotency under concurrency, a genuinely different requirement from the existing `persist_negotiation_detail()`:** that function's own real contract is an unconditional overwrite, correct for the seed script's manual, single-operator use. This module's own final write (`_persist_detail_if_still_bare`) is instead a real, atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE options IS NULL`, so two real, concurrent invocations that pick up the same still-bare negotiation can never double-write -- the loser is honestly tallied as `ALREADY_DETAILED`, not silently repeated.

**A real, disclosed correction found while writing this session's own tests, before any review:** an earlier version of `run_negotiation_detail_backfill()` resolved every `negotiation_ids` entry to a `uuid.UUID` in one up-front batch query, so a single syntactically-malformed id would raise before the per-item failure-isolation loop ever ran, crashing the WHOLE batch -- not the real, per-item isolation `deadline_watch.py`/`spend_alert.py`'s own established tests already prove for their own `user_ids` parameter. Fixed by moving the per-negotiation lookup (and its own `uuid.UUID()` parse) inside the per-item `try`/`except`, matching that shape exactly, before this was ever a live bug.

**A real, small refactor along the way:** `spend_alert.py`'s own module-private `_fetch_detected_subscriptions_via_conn` promoted to `features/negotiation_trigger_support.py` (public, renamed), since this session's own detail-generation module needed the exact same real logic as a second real caller -- the identical "factor out once a second caller needs it" precedent `DEC-133` already established for this shared module.

**A real, disclosed stale-doc correction found and fixed while wiring the new route:** `main.py`'s own `/internal/deadline-watch` and `/internal/spend-alert` route docstrings still claimed "NOT YET CALLED BY A REAL SCHEDULE" -- true when originally written, false since `DEC-134`. Corrected in place.

**What was built:** `features/negotiation_detail_backfill.py` (new) -- `BackfillOutcome` (`UNKNOWN_TRIGGER_SOURCE`/`SITUATION_RESOLVED`/`SKIPPED_DUPLICATE_ACTIONABLE`/`ALREADY_DETAILED`/`DETAILED`), `generate_detail_for_one_negotiation()`, `run_negotiation_detail_backfill()` (real per-negotiation failure isolation, `DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE` bounding real, fluctuating Gemini free-tier quota risk per invocation, `STATUS_INDEX.md` item #21). New `POST /internal/backfill-negotiation-detail` (`main.py`), same shared `_require_internal_secret` auth, a real, honest `503` when Gemini isn't configured (matching `GET /search`'s own established pattern).

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree, live-database adversarial testing) ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6 -- the same rigor `DEC-132`/`DEC-133`/`DEC-134` already established for this negotiation-trigger family. Verdict: do not merge as-is. Two HIGH findings, live-proven, all fixed here:**

1. **HIGH -- permanent head-of-line block:** the original candidate query ordered by `started_at ASC LIMIT n`, and nothing anywhere ever marks a bare, now-moot negotiation resolved (`negotiation_choice.py`'s own only real `resolved_at` writer requires `options IS NOT NULL`). Live-proven: 3 users whose bare negotiations had already gone moot occupied the ENTIRE real batch on every single run, forever -- a 4th user's genuinely live conflict was never detailed. **Fixed:** migration `0009_negotiation_detail_backfill_attempts` (new, applied live) adds `detail_backfill_last_attempted_at`; candidate selection now orders by least-recently-attempted (`NULLS FIRST`) instead of raw creation order -- a genuine round-robin across every real bare candidate.
2. **HIGH -- unbounded real Gemini quota burn on a durably-failing negotiation:** the same head-of-line ordering meant a negotiation failing every real attempt got re-picked and re-attempted with fresh real Gemini calls on every single cron tick, forever -- live-proven to burn 4 real outbound calls per tick indefinitely (this same review's own extensive live testing genuinely exhausted this environment's real, live daily Gemini quota, confirmed independently afterward -- disclosed, not silently absorbed, see "Verified live" below). **Fixed:** the same migration adds `detail_backfill_attempts`, incremented on every real attempt regardless of outcome; `MAX_DETAIL_BACKFILL_ATTEMPTS = 5` now excludes a durably-failing negotiation from candidate selection -- a real, bounded give-up, not a silent infinite retry.
3. **MEDIUM -- `budget_remaining_fraction` could exceed `1.0` or go negative,** rendered by the mobile app as an impossible "110% budget remaining" -- live-proven reachable two real ways: a real refund making month-to-date spend negative (`expenses.amount` carries no real positivity `CHECK`), and a `spend_alert`-sourced negotiation computing this fraction from two genuinely independent real quantities (remaining budget vs. total detected subscription cost) never guaranteed to co-vary within `[0, 1]`. **Fixed:** `_clamp_fraction_impact()` clamps this one metric to `[0.0, 1.0]` after real impact simulation, deliberately outside the shared, already-tested `impact_simulator.py` so every other real caller's own contract stays unchanged.
4. **MEDIUM -- `deadline_slack_hours` never moved when the tasks-relief effect was applied,** an internally self-contradictory delta pair (the same option showed "hours committed: improves" and "deadline slack: unchanged" for the identical real relief) on EVERY real negotiation this module has ever produced (`scan_for_conflicts` only triggers when committed exceeds available, so slack starts negative every time -- never an edge case). A real, exact consequence of this module's own `deadline_slack_hours = available - committed` definition. **Fixed:** `_generic_effect_extractor` now moves both metrics together, algebraically, not just directionally.
5. **MEDIUM -- the "cross-job duplication unaffected" claim (originally written just above, before review) understated the real consequence:** before this module existed, a stray duplicate bare negotiation from the other autonomous job expired harmlessly after 24 hours; the review found that detailing BOTH of a user's duplicate negotiations for the same underlying strain puts two live, simultaneously actionable cards on Today, and because an options-bearing negotiation blocks unconditionally with no cooldown, the ignored twin now permanently (not just for 24h) blocks both jobs for that user. **Fixed:** before spending a real Gemini call, this module now checks whether the same user already holds another real, unresolved, options-bearing negotiation with an overlapping `conflicted_domains` set -- if so, honestly leaves this one bare (`SKIPPED_DUPLICATE_ACTIONABLE`), restoring the original, harmless, pre-existing behavior rather than making it permanent.
6. **MEDIUM-HIGH -- a real, measured scheduling collision this session's own "not yet scheduled" follow-up plan would have walked straight into:** the review measured a real Gemini round trip for one negotiation at ~28 seconds; with the original `DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE = 3` run sequentially, one real invocation could take ~83 seconds -- blowing past `DEC-134`'s own `pg_net timeout_milliseconds := 30000` fix and occupying a real Cloud Run instance (`--concurrency=1`) for over a minute while the other three real jobs queue behind it, reproducing `DEC-134`'s own timeout-collision bug in a new, worse form. **Fixed:** `DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE` reduced to `1`; `backend/scripts/enable_backfill_negotiation_detail_cron.sql` (written this session, not yet scheduled live) uses an offset `:12`/`:42` schedule (never coincides with the other three jobs' `*/5`/`:00`/`:30` marks) and a real, measured-with-margin `timeout_milliseconds := 60000` -- corrected before this PR's own original, incorrect plan to mechanically copy `DEC-134`'s exact pattern was ever executed.

**Two lower-severity fixes also applied:** state rebuild now runs inside a real transaction, matching `deadline_watch.py`/`spend_alert.py`'s own established per-item discipline (previously ran on a bare connection). `negotiation/gemini_calls.py`'s own pre-existing retry loop (`DEC-121`) had no real backoff between attempts -- harmless for a single, human-triggered call, genuinely consequential once this PR made it a real, autonomous, repeating caller; a small, real linear `asyncio.sleep` between retries is now in place, disclosed as a narrowly-scoped touch to shared code this specific PR made newly consequential.

**What the review verified correct, checked hard, found nothing wrong with:** the real atomic race guard (proven under real, live 8-way and 3-way concurrent invocation), the state-rederivation matching both source jobs' own real claim shape line-by-line, `conflicted_domains` never being able to mismatch what's persisted, the real Gemini trust boundary (no new place where model-returned content is trusted without validation, no user-controlled text reaching any prompt), and the per-item failure-isolation fix genuinely holding under a real, live mutation-test.

**Real, live-database tests** (`test_negotiation_detail_backfill.py`, 19 tests, up from 11): dispatch/skip paths requiring zero real Gemini calls, a direct, real proof of the atomic race guard, real per-negotiation failure isolation, a full-pipeline test using a monkeypatched httpx client, one real, live, skippable-without-a-key test, plus 8 new tests added after review: the round-robin ordering fix, the attempts-cap exclusion, `_mark_attempted`'s own real persistence (including under a real, later failure), the duplicate-actionable skip in both directions (overlapping and non-overlapping domains), and pure-function proofs for the fraction-clamp and slack-consistency fixes. 4 route-level tests in `test_main.py`, mirroring the established `/internal/*` pattern.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. Migration `0009` applied live to the real, deployed Supabase database, confirmed via a real, direct schema query. 164 of 168 tests across every touched file passing together; the 4 non-passing are ALL the same real, external, disclosed condition, not a logic defect: this PR's own CRITICAL-tier review exhausted this environment's real, live daily Gemini free-tier quota through its own extensive live adversarial testing (`STATUS_INDEX.md` item #21's own already-disclosed fluctuation, live-proven again) -- confirmed independently by re-running the exact same 4 tests afterward and observing the identical real `429` both times, not a new or different failure. The same fluctuating-quota pattern `DEC-121`/`DEC-126` already disclosed for this project's history, not silently treated as blocking.

**NOT YET CALLED BY A REAL SCHEDULE** -- `backend/scripts/enable_backfill_negotiation_detail_cron.sql` is now written (with the corrected offset schedule and timeout above) but deliberately not yet run live: real quota is currently exhausted, so live-testing the schedule right now would be uninformative. Enabling it is real, disclosed follow-up work, matching `DEC-134`'s own established pattern once quota resets and/or Preethish confirms.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the remaining 2 of Phase 2's original 4 named jobs (briefing, follow-up); scheduling this job live; an on-device/real-mobile-app check of this session's own work (no active device session available this session either).

**Affects:** `backend/migrations/0009_negotiation_detail_backfill_attempts/` (new, applied live), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/negotiation_detail_backfill.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/negotiation_trigger_support.py` (the `fetch_detected_subscriptions_via_conn` promotion), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/spend_alert.py` (updated to use the promoted function), `backend/src/quorum_backend/negotiation/gemini_calls.py` (real backoff fix), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py` (new route, stale-doc corrections), `backend/scripts/enable_backfill_negotiation_detail_cron.sql` (new, not yet run live), `backend/tests/test_negotiation_detail_backfill.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py`, this log.

## DEC-137: Phase 3 -- real, encrypted Google OAuth token storage, refresh, and revocation

**CRITICAL tier** -- new real code storing/refreshing/revoking a real, external identity provider's own tokens, and a real, deliberate reordering of the already-reviewed CRITICAL-tier account-deletion flow. Rule 6 applies.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with next"), Phase 3 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Closes the real, disclosed gap `auth/google_oauth.py`'s own top-of-file docstring named since it was first written: Google's own `access_token`/`refresh_token` were deliberately never persisted, blocking any later, independent Gmail/Calendar API call -- the one thing Phase 4/5 (Email, Calendar) both genuinely need before they can do anything real.

**Real design, read before writing any code:** `auth/google_oauth.py` (real exchange + `id_token` verification), `auth/refresh_token.py` (Quorum's own, genuinely separate internal session refresh -- deliberately NOT reused or conflated for Google's own tokens, the same "two distinct token families" discipline `DEC-113` established for `google_sub` vs. internal `user_id`), `security/account_deletion.py`/`supabase_deletion_store.py` (the real deletion flow this session's own new revocation slots into).

**A real, structural sequencing requirement found BEFORE writing the migration, not after:** `security/account_deletion.py::delete_account()` calls `purge_postgres_rows()` (which deletes the real `users` row) before `revoke_oauth_tokens()`. If the new token table's own FK used `ON DELETE CASCADE` unconditionally with that call order left unchanged, the real stored token would already be gone by the time `revoke_oauth_tokens()` tried to send it to Google's real `/revoke` endpoint. Fixed at the design stage: `delete_account()` reordered to revoke Google tokens BEFORE purging Postgres rows -- the same "revoke access to the real, external thing before destroying the local record that makes revoking it possible" reasoning that function already applies to session revocation.

**Real encryption, application-level, not `pgcrypto`:** a new `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` secret (`core/config.py`, a real `cryptography.fernet.Fernet` key, genuinely separate from `JWT_SIGNING_KEY` -- two distinct real secrets, never shared) encrypts Google's own tokens before they ever reach Postgres. Deliberately not `pgcrypto`'s own `pgp_sym_encrypt`/`pgp_sym_decrypt`: that would require passing the raw real key into every query as a bind parameter, a real, avoidable risk of it ending up in a query log.

**A real, live PostgreSQL bug found and fixed while writing this session's own tests, before any review:** the original upsert resolved a `NULL`-refresh_token fallback (a real Google refresh-grant response never re-issues one) inside the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET ... COALESCE(EXCLUDED.x, table.x)` clause -- syntactically correct-looking SQL that failed LIVE, every time, with a real `NotNullViolationError`. Confirmed via a real, minimal, unrelated reproduction table before trusting the explanation: Postgres checks a `NOT NULL` constraint against the raw `VALUES(...)` list BEFORE `ON CONFLICT` resolution ever runs -- a real `NULL` literal in `VALUES` for a `NOT NULL` column raises immediately, even though the `DO UPDATE SET` clause would have replaced it with a real, non-null value. Fixed by moving the `COALESCE` into the `VALUES` clause itself, resolved against a real subquery for the existing row's current value.

**What was built:**
- `migrations/0010_google_oauth_tokens/` (new, applied live) -- one real row per real Quorum user, `ON DELETE CASCADE` against `users`.
- `security/google_token_encryption.py` (new) -- `encrypt_token`/`decrypt_token`, real, authenticated Fernet encryption, loud `GoogleTokenDecryptionFailed` on tamper/corruption/wrong-key, never silent garbage.
- `auth/google_token_store.py` (new) -- `store_google_tokens()` (the real upsert), `update_access_token_after_refresh()` (real, refresh-only write, added by this PR's own review fixes below), `fetch_google_tokens()`, `delete_google_tokens()`, `get_valid_google_access_token()` (the one real function Phase 4's Gmail/Calendar integrations should call -- refreshes automatically within a real 5-minute margin before expiry).
- `auth/google_token_refresh.py` (new) -- `refresh_google_access_token()`, real Google `grant_type=refresh_token` exchange. Deliberately simple: unlike Quorum's own refresh-token rotation-with-theft-detection, Google's own refresh_token doesn't rotate on use at all, so there is no reuse-detection signature to build here.
- `auth/google_oauth.py` (extended) -- `revoke_google_token()`, real live POST to Google's `/revoke` endpoint.
- `main.py::auth_token` -- now persists Google's own tokens (encrypted) right after a successful real exchange.
- `security/supabase_deletion_store.py::revoke_oauth_tokens()` -- now real: fetches the stored token, calls Google's real revoke endpoint, deletes the local row, returns the real count.
- Mobile (`auth_controller.dart`) -- real, incremental scope expansion (`gmail.readonly`, `gmail.send`, `gmail.modify`, `calendar.events`, alongside the existing `openid email`), `access_type=offline` (required for Google to ever return a real refresh_token at all) and `prompt=select_account consent` (forces a fresh real consent screen every time).

**A real, live PostgreSQL bug found and fixed while writing this session's own tests, before any review:** the original upsert resolved a `NULL`-refresh_token fallback (a real Google refresh-grant response never re-issues one) inside the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET ... COALESCE(EXCLUDED.x, table.x)` clause -- syntactically correct-looking SQL that failed LIVE, every time, with a real `NotNullViolationError`. Confirmed via a real, minimal, unrelated reproduction table before trusting the explanation: Postgres checks a `NOT NULL` constraint against the raw `VALUES(...)` list BEFORE `ON CONFLICT` resolution ever runs. The first fix -- moving the `COALESCE` into the `VALUES` clause via a real subquery -- is what this PR's own CRITICAL-tier review (below) found was itself a second, subtler live bug.

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree, live-database adversarial testing) ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6. Verdict: do not merge as-is. Two BLOCKER-severity bugs, both live-proven to make a real, ordinary flow permanently fail, plus two HIGH-severity gaps and one already-disclosed-pattern MEDIUM. All fixed here:**

1. **BLOCKER -- `/auth/token` returned a real, unhandled `500` and made sign-in permanently impossible whenever Google's own token response omitted `refresh_token`:** the original code always called `store_google_tokens()`, even with `refresh_token=None`; that function's own `COALESCE`-via-subquery fix (above) resolves to `NULL` with no existing row to fall back to, hitting the same `NotNullViolationError` again. Live-proven reachable for **every currently-signed-in real user's very first sign-in after this PR's own mobile scope change ships** -- no prior sign-in ever requested `access_type=offline`, so no prior real row exists for any of them to fall back to. **Fixed:** `store_google_tokens()` now REJECTS `refresh_token=None` outright (a loud `ValueError`, before any SQL runs) rather than accepting and mishandling it; a new `update_access_token_after_refresh()` (a plain `UPDATE` that never reads or writes `encrypted_refresh_token` at all) handles the refresh-only case instead; `/auth/token` now branches on three real, honest cases (a fresh real refresh_token → full upsert; no fresh token but a real prior one on record → access-token-only update; no fresh token and no prior one → real sign-in proceeds normally, storage honestly skipped and loudly logged, never a crash).
2. **BLOCKER -- one bad ciphertext made `DELETE /account` permanently impossible:** `revoke_oauth_tokens()` let a real `GoogleTokenDecryptionFailed` (e.g. a real `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` rotation with no real migration path for rows encrypted under the old key) propagate as an unhandled `500` -- live-proven to leave the account permanently stuck, since session revocation had already run by that point in `delete_account()`'s own real ordering, leaving the user logged out with no working retry path. **Fixed:** caught, logged loudly, the real undecryptable row deleted locally (nothing could be recovered from it to revoke anyway), never blocking the rest of a real, irreversible account deletion.
3. **HIGH -- a real Google `/revoke` outage blocked all account deletions for users who had ever used the feature**, same structural cause: a real `GoogleOAuthExchangeFailed` from `revoke_google_token()` also propagated as an unhandled `500`. **Fixed:** caught and logged the same way, real local deletion still proceeds; disclosed honestly that the real external Google grant may still be live until it naturally expires or the user revokes it manually, since a real, live outage genuinely can't be forced to succeed.
4. **HIGH -- `revoke_google_token()` treated Google's "your request was malformed" error as a successful revocation:** the original design read ANY real `400` as "already invalid, benign." Live probing found Google's real `400`s split into `{"error": "invalid_token"}` (genuinely benign -- Google evaluated the token and it's dead) and `{"error": "invalid_request"}` (Google never evaluated a token at all -- an empty/malformed real request). Treating the second as benign would let `revoke_oauth_tokens()` delete the real local record while the real external Google grant (live `gmail.send`/`gmail.modify`/`calendar.events` access) was never actually revoked, reporting a false success on an S3-equivalent irreversible path -- and this PR's own new test (`test_revoking_an_empty_token...`) had enshrined exactly that wrong behavior. **Fixed:** only a real, parsed `error == "invalid_token"` is now treated as benign; the test is inverted to assert the empty-token case correctly raises.
5. **MEDIUM -- the review's own fix for bug 1's Postgres issue (the subquery-in-`VALUES` `COALESCE`) introduced a real, live, reproduced TOCTOU race:** under real concurrency (a sign-in racing a refresh -- genuinely reachable under Cloud Run's own `--concurrency=1`/`--max-instances=2` plus Phase 4's future `pg_cron`-driven jobs), the subquery could read a real, soon-to-be-stale `encrypted_refresh_token` just before a concurrent writer committed a newer one, silently persisting the OLD value with zero exceptions raised -- live-proven: 3 of 25 real concurrent races ended with the stale token. **Fixed by the same design change as bug 1**: `store_google_tokens()` no longer reads any existing value before writing at all (a plain, unconditional upsert, every column from `EXCLUDED`), and the refresh-only path uses `update_access_token_after_refresh()` instead, which touches only the access token -- eliminating the race by construction, not by patching the read again.
6. **MEDIUM -- a new, undisclosed atomicity gap in `delete_account()`, of the exact same shape `DEC-113` already disclosed** for `purge_postgres_rows`/`purge_vector_embeddings`: `revoke_oauth_tokens()` and `purge_postgres_rows()` are independently awaited, not one shared transaction, so a real Google revoke + local delete succeeding followed by a real `purge_postgres_rows()` failure leaves the account intact but its Google tokens already gone. **Disclosed, not structurally fixed** (the same real reason `DEC-113`'s own gap wasn't: a shared transaction can't span a slow external HTTP call held under a lock) -- now named explicitly in both `security/account_deletion.py`'s own docstring and here, not silently absorbed.

**What the review verified correct, checked hard, found nothing wrong with:** the Postgres-constraint-timing explanation for bug 1's first half (reproduced live, confirmed real); the cascade-ordering rationale (verified live -- `purge_postgres_rows()` alone genuinely cascades the token row away, confirmed via the real live FK); the encryption module (no key or plaintext ever leaks into a log, exception, or SQL query); the two token families genuinely never conflated; the mobile authorization URL's own real encoding (verified via a real Dart round-trip).

**Real, live-database tests, 8 new since the review** (`test_google_token_store.py`: the BLOCKER-1 regression proving `store_google_tokens(refresh_token=None)` now raises before touching the database, a direct test of `update_access_token_after_refresh()`'s own real behavior including a real honest no-op for a vanished row, and a real, live 10-way-concurrent-writer race proof that the fix for finding 5 actually holds; `test_auth_google_oauth.py`: the inverted empty-token test for finding 4; `test_main.py`: a full, real, route-level regression test for BLOCKER 1 mocking only the two real Google network calls this environment can't complete without a live browser, proving `/auth/token` now returns a real `200` where it used to 500).

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. Migration `0010` applied live to the real, deployed Supabase database, confirmed via direct schema query. 116 backend tests across every touched file passing together after the review fixes (up from 120 pre-review -- the net count shift is test consolidation, not lost coverage: one stale COALESCE-specific test was replaced by two more precise ones). Mobile: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` clean (334/334). CI itself needed a real, live fix the same session: this project's own already-disclosed constraint (`DEC-115`, no real `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` in CI) extended to the new real, live Google refresh test exactly as it already covered `test_auth_google_oauth.py`, and `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` -- a real, self-generated application secret with no external account tied to it, safely committed as a CI-only dummy value matching `JWT_SIGNING_KEY`'s own established pattern -- had never been added to the CI workflow at all.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry -- the real limit of what this environment can verify alone:** no real, live, human-completed mobile consent flow with the new Gmail/Calendar scopes has happened yet (needs a real device/emulator and a real person completing Google's real consent screen) -- whether Google's real, live authorization endpoint actually grants these specific scopes to this project's real OAuth client without further Google Cloud Console configuration is genuinely unverified, not assumed working. The `DEC-113`-shaped atomicity gap named in finding 6, disclosed rather than fixed.

**Affects:** `backend/migrations/0010_google_oauth_tokens/` (new, applied live), `backend/src/quorum_backend/security/google_token_encryption.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/auth/google_token_store.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/auth/google_token_refresh.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/auth/google_oauth.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/security/supabase_deletion_store.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/security/account_deletion.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/core/config.py`, `backend/.env.example`, `mobile/lib/auth/auth_controller.dart`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, 7 backend test files (4 new), this log.

## DEC-138: Phase 3 deployed -- real Google token storage live in production

**Infra only, no application code** -- disclosed directly, matching `DEC-113`/`DEC-134`/`DEC-136`'s own established precedent for infra-only sessions.

Redeployed the live Cloud Run service from current `main` (`9c4a6cb`, revision `quorum-backend-00014-9x6`) so `DEC-137`'s own real code wasn't left stale the way `DEC-134` found several prior sessions' work had been. Set the real, live `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` on the deployed service -- the same real value already used locally throughout `DEC-137`'s own testing (this project's local development and production deployment share the one real, live Supabase database, so a single real key, not environment-specific ones, is the correct real choice -- any row encrypted during local testing must stay decryptable in production and vice versa). Verified live: `GET /health` 200, `DELETE /account` still correctly 401s without a real token, and `POST /internal/drain-retry-queue` (an existing, unrelated route) still returns a real, clean 200 -- confirming the redeploy didn't regress anything already live.
