That real verification needs a real device/emulator and a real person completing Google's real consent screen -- the next real step for Phase 3, not something this session's own tooling can complete alone.

**Affects:** Live infra only -- Cloud Run revision `quorum-backend-00014-9x6` (redeployed from `main` `9c4a6cb`, `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` now set), this log.

## DEC-139: Phase 3's real, final open item closed -- a real, live consent flow confirms every new scope grants cleanly

**Real, live verification, no code changes.** The one thing `DEC-137`/`DEC-138` couldn't verify from this environment alone: whether Google's real, live authorization endpoint would actually grant `gmail.readonly`/`gmail.send`/`gmail.modify`/`calendar.events` to this project's real OAuth client without further Google Cloud Console configuration.

**Real, live, human-completed today:** Preethish created a real, dedicated sandbox Google account (`quorum.dev.sandbox@gmail.com`, never his personal Gmail -- Rule 5), rebuilt and installed the current `main` onto the real physical device from `DEC-117`/`DEC-118` (the previously-installed build predated this session's own scope changes), and completed a real sign-in. The real, live "Google hasn't verified this app" interstitial appeared first -- confirmed as the expected, correct signal for a real OAuth client still in Testing publish status with an authorized test user, not an error (an unlisted account would have been rejected before ever reaching that screen). Continuing through it reached the real consent screen, which he approved.

**Confirmed directly against the real, live database, not assumed from the app's own "signed in" state:** a real `google_oauth_tokens` row now exists for this identity, with `granted_scopes` listing all six real scopes Google actually granted -- `openid`, `userinfo.email`, `gmail.readonly`, `gmail.send`, `gmail.modify`, `calendar.events` -- every single one requested, none silently dropped. The real, live decryption round-trip against the real, deployed `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` was also confirmed directly: `fetch_google_tokens()` genuinely decrypts the stored row, and the real access token has Google's own real, live `ya29.` prefix -- confirmed without ever printing the real token value itself, the same real-secret-handling discipline this project holds everywhere else.

**This closes the real, honest possibility `DEC-137` disclosed rather than assumed away** -- Google's OAuth consent-screen configuration did NOT need any further real, external, human Cloud Console action beyond what already existed. Phase 3 (`QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`) is now genuinely, fully closed: built, reviewed, deployed, and live-verified end to end.

**Affects:** No code. `specs/tier1_foundation/QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md` (Phase 3 marked closed), `specs/tier3_verification/STATUS_INDEX.md`, this log.

## DEC-140: Phase 4 (ingestion half) -- real Gmail polling, Waiting On, the first real Gmail API integration this backend has ever made

**CRITICAL tier** -- Quorum's first real integration reading and writing a real, live external mailbox using a real user's stored OAuth credentials. Rule 6 applies.

**Authorized directly ("now lets move on with next"), Phase 4 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Deliberately split into "ingestion first" (this entry: Gmail polling + `sent_messages` + Waiting On) and "execution second" (real `SEND_EMAIL`/`ARCHIVE_EMAIL`/`LABEL_EMAIL`, not started) -- the plan's own explicit recommendation to not stack two new external-integration risks in one session, the same reasoning already applied to deferring Career's own detection.

**What was built:** `migrations/0011_sent_messages/` (new, applied live) -- `UNIQUE(user_id, message_id)`, a partial index for the unreplied-thread lookup. `features/waiting_on.py` (new) -- `SentMessage`, `find_stale_waiting_on()` (pure, the real 4-day threshold `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` §4 already specifies), `record_sent_message()`/`mark_thread_replied()`/`fetch_stale_waiting_on()`. `features/email_ingestion.py` (new) -- `scan_one_user_email()`/`run_email_ingestion()`, using Phase 3's `get_valid_google_access_token()`. `main.py` -- `GET /waiting_on` (real, per-user auth), `POST /internal/email-ingestion` (shared-secret gated, matching every other `/internal/*` route). Mobile: `waiting_on_api.dart` (mirrors `career_pipeline_api.dart` exactly), wired into `main.dart`'s `MainShell` -- unlocks the already-built, already-tested Waiting On screen for the first time since it was written years ahead of any real backend to call.

**A real, deliberate query design, confirmed live before trusting it:** the received-message query is `in:inbox -in:sent`, not bare `in:inbox` -- a real, live probe against the sandbox account confirmed a message sent to oneself carries BOTH the `SENT` and `INBOX` labels, which would otherwise misdetect a self-sent message as its own reply.

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree) ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6. Verdict: do not merge as-is. One BLOCKER (reachable on the single most common real email pattern -- replying to an inbound thread -- and actively enshrined by one of this PR's own passing tests), three HIGH, four MEDIUM, three LOW. All BLOCKER/HIGH/MEDIUM fixed here; LOW findings disclosed, two fixed cheaply, one left as a pre-existing app-wide pattern this session didn't introduce:**

1. **BLOCKER -- reply detection had no timestamp-ordering guard at all**, and one of this PR's own tests had enshrined the bug as correct behavior. `mark_thread_replied()`'s original `WHERE ... replied_at IS NULL` had no comparison between the incoming message's own timestamp and the sent message's `sent_at`. Concrete, common failure: a real, old inbound message that simply never got archived sits in the same Gmail thread as a much newer real send -- the old message would incorrectly close out the newer send the very first time it was ever checked, and Waiting On would never show a real, multi-round thread again. **Fixed:** `sent_at < replied_at` added to the guard -- a real incoming message can only close out sends that genuinely predate it, correct regardless of what order Gmail's own results happen to arrive in (removing an undocumented implicit ordering assumption the original loop depended on). The enshrining test was inverted; two new regression tests added (an old inbound message never closes a newer send; a genuine later reply still correctly closes every earlier send).
2. **HIGH -- a single unparseable/deleted real Gmail message (a `KeyError`, a 404 from a message trashed between `list` and `get`) propagated out of the whole per-user scan**, permanently re-triggering on every future poll (the offending message, never recorded, stays "unknown" forever) and silently skipping reply detection entirely for that user. **Fixed:** every real per-message fetch-and-write, in both phases, is now individually wrapped and tallied into a new `messages_failed` count; the scan continues to the next real message. `scan_one_user_email()` split into two focused helper functions (`_record_new_sent_messages`/`_detect_real_replies`) as part of this fix, closing a cognitive-complexity flag that came up while writing it.
3. **HIGH -- no real overlap guard existed for the eventual `pg_cron`-scheduled batch, and no wall-clock budget bounded its own real duration.** **A real, live-discovered infrastructure constraint changed this fix mid-session, not assumed in advance:** the first fix used a Postgres session-level advisory lock (`pg_try_advisory_lock`/`pg_advisory_unlock`); a real, live contention test of that fix FAILED -- the "overlapping" caller acquired the lock too. Root cause, confirmed directly: this backend's real `SUPABASE_URL` connects through Supabase's own PgBouncer pooler in TRANSACTION-POOLING mode (`...pooler.supabase.com:6543`), which does not guarantee the same underlying Postgres backend connection across separate statements on what `asyncpg` presents as one logical connection -- session-scoped advisory locks are unreliable under exactly this pooling mode. **Real fix:** `migrations/0012_email_ingestion_job_lock/` (new, applied live) -- a singleton row claimed via one atomic `UPDATE ... RETURNING` statement, correct under transaction pooling since every statement here is already self-contained; `started_at` gives it a real, disclosed staleness self-heal (`EMAIL_INGESTION_JOB_LOCK_STALE_AFTER_SECONDS = 600`) so a real crash mid-batch never wedges the job forever. A real `EMAIL_INGESTION_BATCH_DEADLINE_SECONDS = 240` wall-clock budget also now stops the batch cleanly with honest, partial counts rather than risking a request-timeout kill mid-run.
4. **HIGH -- a real, revoked (or currently un-refreshable) Google grant was indistinguishable from a genuine code-level failure**, silently inflating `users_failed` forever with no self-healing path and no honest signal for an operator to act on, contradicting this route's own docstring. **Fixed:** a real `GoogleOAuthExchangeFailed` from the refresh attempt is now caught and reported as its own, distinct `ScanOutcome.GOOGLE_TOKEN_REFRESH_FAILED` / `EmailIngestionResult.users_token_refresh_failed` -- genuinely uncertain whether the real cause is a revoked grant or a transient Google outage (this module does not guess), but no longer collapsed into the same bucket as an unrelated code bug, the same three-valued-over-collapsing discipline `Finding.evidence_state` already holds elsewhere in this project. The same shape as `DEC-137`'s own finding 4.
5. **MEDIUM -- the `-in:sent` regression test was vacuous**, passing even with the fix reverted: it hardcoded the correct answer per query string and silently returned an empty list for any unrecognized query. **Fixed:** the fake Gmail client now genuinely evaluates `in:sent`/`in:inbox -in:sent` against each fake message's own `labelIds`, the same way real Gmail does, and raises loudly on an unrecognized query rather than passing vacuously.
6. **MEDIUM -- the live capstone test wrote uncleaned real rows into the real, live production account's data** (only its own marker message was deleted in `finally`, leaving every other real message the scan recorded behind). **Fixed:** the test now snapshots `sent_messages` before and after its own real scan and deletes exactly what the run itself added.
7. **MEDIUM -- a new per-user PII table (`sent_messages`) was added with zero deletion-path test coverage**, the same class of gap `DEC-124` was an entire dedicated session about. Functionally already correct (`ON DELETE CASCADE` against `users`, verified by reading `purge_postgres_rows()` directly) but undisclosed and untested. **Fixed:** a real, live cascade test added to `test_supabase_deletion_store.py`; `purge_postgres_rows()`'s own comment now names `sent_messages` explicitly, alongside `google_oauth_tokens`'s existing cascade note.
8. **MEDIUM -- a TOCTOU between `messages.list` and `messages.get`** (a message deleted/trashed in between) aborted the whole user's scan for that cycle. **Fixed by the same per-message isolation as finding 2.**
9. **LOW, fixed -- a malformed response item could escape the mobile `ApiException` taxonomy** (`waiting_on_api.dart`'s item-level parse was unguarded, matching a pre-existing gap in every sibling fetcher). Fixed for this new file specifically: the parse is now wrapped, with a new test for a malformed item.
10. **LOW, disclosed not fixed -- `_WaitingOnLoader` calls `fetch()` directly inside `build()`**, matching every sibling `_XLoader` widget's own pre-existing pattern (`_FinanceLoader` etc.) -- this PR makes it reachable for the first time, but doesn't introduce the pattern; fixing it project-wide is out of this session's scope (Rule 3).
11. **LOW, disclosed not fixed -- a `Bcc`-only or `To`-less send stores an empty `recipient`, and a multi-recipient `To` header is stored as one raw comma-joined string.** Both satisfy the real `NOT NULL` constraint; neither was disclosed in `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.9's single-address example. Accepted as a minor, cosmetic gap.

**What the review verified correct, checked hard, found nothing wrong with:** cross-user data isolation across every real SQL statement touching `sent_messages` (all parameterized, all correctly scoped, `scan_one_user_email` receives the internal UUID never `google_sub`, avoiding `DEC-113`'s own conflation trap); the concurrency safety the `UNIQUE(user_id, message_id)` constraint itself provides (no duplicate rows, no double-counted `new_sent_messages`/`new_replies_detected` under real concurrent writers); route-level auth composition (`/waiting_on` via `_require_auth`, `/internal/email-ingestion` via `_require_internal_secret`, both matching established precedent exactly, the 503 config-check race-free since `get_settings()` is `lru_cache`d); the response shape against `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.9 directly; the migration's index correctness and `down.sql`'s clean inverse; every pure function (`find_stale_waiting_on`, `_extract_header`, `_parse_internal_date`); zero LLM calls anywhere in either new module, no persistent-worker assumption, no chronological-feed regression.

**Real, live verification performed directly against the real sandbox account (`quorum.dev.sandbox@gmail.com`, `DEC-139`), automated as tests, not just manually probed:** `test_email_ingestion.py`'s own capstone test sends a genuine email through Gmail's real, live API and confirms this module's real, live polling detects it end to end -- ran and passed multiple times during this session, including immediately after the review fixes.

**Tests:** 14 (`test_waiting_on.py`, +2 for the BLOCKER regression) + 18 (`test_email_ingestion.py`, up from 12 pre-review: the label-aware fake client rewrite, the BLOCKER regression pair, the per-message-failure test, the refresh-failure test, the job-lock contention/self-heal tests) + 9 route-level tests in `test_main.py` (unchanged in count, response-shape assertions extended for the new result fields) + 1 new cascade test in `test_supabase_deletion_store.py` + 1 new mobile test in `waiting_on_api_test.dart`.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. Migrations `0011`/`0012` applied live to the real, deployed Supabase database, both schema-verified directly. 131 backend tests across every Phase-4-touched file passing together. Mobile: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` clean (344/344), including the pre-existing `main_shell_navigation_test.dart` assertion that the You tab now genuinely reaches Waiting On. Full backend/full mobile suite runs this session hit unrelated flakiness twice -- real Postgres pool-timeout errors in `test_retry_queue_drainer.py`/other unrelated files (confirmed pre-existing: a different file failed each run, none ever a Phase-4 file, each passes cleanly in isolation) and a real, genuine Gemini free-tier quota exhaustion (`limit: 20`, this session's own repeated full-suite runs) in unrelated Gemini-backed test files -- neither is a Phase 4 regression.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the two LOW findings disclosed-not-fixed above (10, 11). Real `SEND_EMAIL`/`ARCHIVE_EMAIL`/`LABEL_EMAIL` execution -- deliberately deferred to a follow-on session, per this entry's own opening paragraph. `enable_email_ingestion_cron.sql` is written (`7,22,37,52 * * * *`, `timeout_milliseconds := 270000`, avoiding every existing job's own marks) but not yet scheduled live as of this entry -- the next real step.

## DEC-142: Phase 4 execution half -- real SEND_EMAIL/ARCHIVE_EMAIL/LABEL_EMAIL, and a real structural fix to the Gate's own S3 guarantee

**CRITICAL tier** -- Quorum's first real ability to autonomously send, archive, and label real Gmail messages, and a real, structural fix to the Gate's own absolute S3 human-approval rule. Rule 6 applies.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with execution half now"), Phase 4's second half.** Extends `features/action_executor.py` (`DEC-128`) with real Gmail API execution for the three remaining real `ActionType`s that have a real, external execution target, using Phase 3's stored token.

**A real, significant finding made BEFORE writing any execution code, not after:** `gate/orchestration.py::review()`'s real Gate state machine does not itself hardcode "S3 always escalates to a human" -- the Judge's own verdict alone decides `approve`/`escalate_to_human` for S2/S3, with nothing enforcing `CLAUDE.md`'s own absolute "S3 always requires explicit human approval, no exception" rule as a structural guarantee. Harmless today only because email/calendar-external are not real negotiation domains, so no real caller can reach this yet -- but since this session was about to build exactly the function that would carry out an "approved" S3 action, the missing backstop was added at the point that would actually execute it, not deferred.

**What was built:** `execute_approved_action()` extended with real `SEND_EMAIL` (`POST .../messages/send`, base64url raw MIME, matching `agents/email_agent.py`'s own existing payload shape -- `{"to","body"}`, no `subject` -- a real, disclosed pre-existing gap: `email_agent.py`'s own `EmailAgentState` carries a `thread_id` that never actually reaches the payload it builds, so this is a genuinely new top-level send, never a real Gmail reply-in-thread; not fixed here, out of scope) and real `ARCHIVE_EMAIL`/`LABEL_EMAIL` (`POST .../messages/{id}/modify` with `removeLabelIds:["INBOX"]` / `addLabelIds:[label_id]` -- a new payload shape, `message_id`/`label_id`, since no prior agent/contract existed for either).

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree) ran against the first version of this PR before merge, per Rule 6. Verdict: do not merge as-is. Two BLOCKER, four HIGH, six MEDIUM, seven LOW. All BLOCKER/HIGH/MEDIUM fixed here (one HIGH -- a bad-ciphertext exception escaping this function -- was found and fixed by direct self-review before the review agent even finished and independently confirmed by it); most LOW items fixed too, folded into the same design change that fixed the others:**

1. **BLOCKER -- real email header injection into a real, irreversible S3 send:** `payload["to"]`/`payload.get("subject")` were originally interpolated raw into RFC 5322 header lines joined by literal `\r\n` -- a real `to` value containing an embedded CR/LF could inject an arbitrary `Bcc:` header or terminate the header block and replace the entire body. Reachable via `agents/email_agent.py`'s own real, untrusted `recipient`, or a genuine S3 `approve` verdict carrying a Judge-authored `revised_payload` with no schema guarantee beyond being a `dict` (confirmed directly against `orchestration.py`). **Fixed:** the real MIME message is now built via the stdlib `email.message.EmailMessage` (confirmed live to genuinely raise `ValueError` on an embedded CR/LF header value, not assumed), plus an explicit, defensive pre-check rejecting `\r`/`\n` in `to`/`subject` before ever building a message.
2. **BLOCKER -- a genuinely-sent email could be reported as `executed=False, "not carried out"`:** a `response.json()["id"]` lookup after a real `200` could raise `KeyError`/`JSONDecodeError`, caught by the malformed-payload handler and reported as "not carried out" -- false; the email was already sent. Worse, a genuine transport-level failure (a timeout) was collapsed into a flat, definite `executed=False`, when the true outcome is genuinely unknown -- Gmail may have already processed it. **Fixed:** `ExecutionResult.executed` is now three-valued (`bool | None`, matching this project's own `Finding.evidence_state` discipline) -- a real, definite HTTP response (even a non-200, even one that fails to parse) always settles the outcome (`True` for `200`, `False` otherwise); only a genuine transport-level exception produces `executed=None`, an honest "genuinely unknown," never silently folded into `False`.
3. **HIGH -- `GoogleTokenDecryptionFailed` (a bad ciphertext, e.g. after a real key rotation) was uncaught**, propagating as an unhandled exception and aborting the caller's whole transaction. Found and fixed before the review agent finished; independently confirmed by it. Resolved structurally, not just by widening the catch: see finding 6 below.
4. **HIGH -- the S3 backstop was per-branch, not structural; `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` (also real `Stakes.S3`) was unguarded** -- a future Phase 5 session adding its own execution branch for it would have had nothing forcing the same check to be re-added. **Fixed:** hoisted to the top of the internal dispatch function, checked once via `router.get_stakes()` before every branch -- covers every present and future S3 type by construction. A new, dedicated test (`test_real_s3_action_types_are_exactly_send_email_and_calendar_external`) ties this to the live `STAKES_TABLE` directly, so a future reclassification is a loud, visible test failure, never a silent policy change (review finding M2).
5. **HIGH -- the live capstone tests selected the sender by "most recently updated Google grant," against the real production database** -- harmless only while the sandbox account is the sole real grant in this deployment; the moment any real user connects Google, the next run would send a real email from and trash messages in *their* mailbox. **Fixed:** selects explicitly by the real, known sandbox email (`users.email = 'quorum.dev.sandbox@gmail.com'`, confirmed live-joinable to `google_oauth_tokens`), `pytest.skip`s otherwise.
6. **HIGH -- the archive/label capstone's own verification could pass while proving nothing:** archiving immediately after a real send and asserting `"INBOX" not in labelIds` with no wait could trivially pass even with the whole modify branch stubbed out, since real Gmail delivery (and therefore the real `INBOX` label) isn't synchronous with `messages.send`'s own response (`DEC-140`'s own finding). **Fixed:** the capstone now polls (bounded) for the real PRE-condition before acting and the real POST-condition after, so each assertion genuinely depends on the real API call having happened.
7. **MEDIUM -- `human_approved: bool` was a bare flag with nothing tying it to a real human** -- nothing prevented a future caller from writing `human_approved=(verdict.decision == "approve")`, the exact confusion this fix exists to prevent. **Fixed:** replaced with `approved_by_user_id: str | None`, required to equal the real `user_id` this call is already scoped to -- a future caller must hold a genuine, verified identity, not a derived boolean.
8. **MEDIUM -- no durable, transaction-independent record of a real send** -- the dual-write risk (a real send succeeding, then the surrounding transaction rolling back) had no artifact to reconcile against. **Fixed:** a real `logger.warning()` immediately after every real, successful Gmail call, written to Cloud Run's own durable, transaction-independent log sink.
9. **MEDIUM -- the `Pool | Connection` type-hint widening changed real token-refresh durability, not just typing:** a token refresh running inside the caller's own open transaction would have its real, already-issued Google access token discarded on any ordinary rollback, worsening an already-disclosed risk from "only under a degraded database" to "on any ordinary rollback." **Fixed at the root, not just documented:** `execute_approved_action()` no longer resolves its own Google token at all -- it takes an already-resolved `google_access_token`, which any real future caller must obtain via `get_valid_google_access_token(pool, ...)` against the real POOL, before opening its own transaction. The `google_token_store.py` type-hint widening was reverted entirely -- no longer needed.
10. **MEDIUM -- the exhaustive "every other action type is honestly non-executing" test now enshrined a stale claim** (three of the nine were genuinely executable, just missing credentials in the test). **Fixed:** split into a real, narrowed exhaustiveness test for the five genuinely-still-unimplemented non-S3 types (asserting the specific "no real execution path" detail, not a bare falsy check), a dedicated test for `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL`'s own correct S3 refusal, and a separate test proving the three Gmail-capable types are honestly non-executing with no real credentials.
11. Several LOW findings folded into the same fixes above: `message_id`/`label_id` are now validated against a real, narrow character pattern before being interpolated into a real Gmail URL (a malformed value is refused before any network call); the "refuses without approval" test now asserts zero real Gmail calls were possible, not just a detail substring; `ARCHIVE_EMAIL`/`LABEL_EMAIL`'s own success detail now reports the real, current `labelIds` Gmail's own response returned, not a hardcoded claim; the cleanup helper now asserts a real access token and a real `200` before treating an empty message list as "nothing to clean up."

**What the review verified correct, checked hard, found nothing wrong with:** the real Gate gap itself (independently confirmed by reading `orchestration.py` directly); stakes lookup done via `router.get_stakes()`, never a hardcoded string compare; the fail-safe `None` default and clean call sites (`retry_queue_drainer.py` is the only real caller and never passes the new parameters); cross-user correctness (`user_id` threaded correctly throughout, never confused with `google_sub`, `DEC-113`'s conflation trap avoided); every real Gmail failure-status code funneling through to an honest, non-crashing result; the `retry_queue_drainer.py` docstring's own claim that email can never reach it (verified against `Position.domain`'s real schema and `negotiation/downstream_translation.py` directly); the real, granted OAuth scopes (`gmail.send`/`gmail.modify`, confirmed against `auth_controller.dart`); the fake-client tests' own genuine (non-vacuous) assertions against exact URLs/bodies/headers; zero LLM calls or learned-classifier logic anywhere in the stakes/approval path.

**Real, live verification performed directly against the real sandbox account (`quorum.dev.sandbox@gmail.com`, `DEC-139`), automated as tests:** a genuine, human-approved send through Gmail's real, live API; a real archive-then-label round trip on a real probe message, each step polled and confirmed against Gmail's own real API responses -- both capstones ran and passed multiple times during this session, including immediately after every round of fixes.

**Tests:** 27 in `test_action_executor.py` (up from 6 pre-session) -- the real S3-backstop consistency test, real header/subject-injection regression tests, the real three-valued-outcome tests (a definite Gmail rejection vs. a genuinely unknown transport failure vs. a real `200` with an unparseable body), real malformed-Gmail-id rejection tests, the narrowed exhaustiveness tests, and the two real, live capstone tests.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. 166 backend tests across every DEC-142-touched file (and its real dependents -- `retry_queue_drainer.py`, `google_token_store.py`, `email_ingestion.py`, `main.py`) passing together. Full backend suite otherwise clean modulo the same, already-disclosed, already-fluctuating Gemini free-tier `429`s in unrelated files this session's earlier runs already established as pre-existing.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** no real caller in this backend can reach `SEND_EMAIL`/`ARCHIVE_EMAIL`/`LABEL_EMAIL` today (email is not a real negotiation domain) -- this is real, tested execution capability with no real production wiring yet, the same disclosed shape `DEC-125`'s own Stage B implementations carried before `DEC-127` wired them in. No real "a human clicked approve on this escalated action" endpoint exists yet either -- `approved_by_user_id` is real and structurally enforced, but nothing can honestly supply it in production yet. `agents/email_agent.py`'s own `thread_id`/`subject` gap, disclosed, not fixed (out of scope). No `labels.list` call or label-id constant exists anywhere in this codebase -- a real future `LABEL_EMAIL` caller has no discovery path yet and must already know a valid real Gmail label id.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/action_executor.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/retry_queue_drainer.py` (docstring only), `backend/tests/test_action_executor.py`, this log.

## DEC-144: Phase 5 begins -- real Meeting-Load Defense, the concrete backend slice

**Standard tier** -- pure computation, zero database access, zero external calls, zero Gate/security/external-action code touched. No CRITICAL-tier review required per Rule 6's own scope; a standard fresh-context review still ran per this project's baseline discipline.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with NEXT"), Phase 5 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Phase 5 is real, substantial, and genuinely spans three separable pieces: this backend module (concrete, scoped, buildable in one session), the real on-device `CalendarProvider` integration (substantial, separate mobile work -- `device_calendar` package, real permission handling, a real Dart port of this exact function), and real `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` execution (a new, Rule-5/6-gated external Google Calendar API call, the same shape as `DEC-142`'s own Gmail work). This session deliberately scopes to the first only, disclosed rather than silently narrowed.

**What was built:** `features/meeting_load.py` (new) -- `compute_meeting_load()`, a pure, real function using `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` §4's own already-specified parameters (`WORKING_HOURS_PER_DAY = 8.0`, `BUFFER_FRACTION = 0.25`, `OVERLOAD_THRESHOLD = 0.7`) -- confirmed by hand against a real, live-verified float fact before trusting a boundary test: `0.7 * (8.0 * 0.75)` is `4.199999999999999` in real IEEE-754 double precision, not exactly `4.2`, since `0.7` has no exact binary representation. Deliberately source-agnostic (`committed_hours` is an input, never queried by this module itself), matching `today.py::compute_capacity_state()`'s own established split between pure math and live data-fetching (`DEC-119`) -- real calendar ground truth is on-device per the ADD's own §9.2/§10.3, so a backend module computing a real user's true daily meeting load from its own database alone could never be honest, and doesn't try to be.

**A real, disclosed, pre-existing gap closed as part of this same work:** `today.py`'s own `TODAY_WORKING_HOURS_PER_DAY` previously duplicated the same real `8.0` value locally, with a comment claiming it "reuses the real, already-defined constant" from a module (`meeting_load.py`) that, per `QUORUM_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN_DOCUMENT.md` §9.7's own real, confirmed table, did not exist anywhere in this repository until now -- an aspirational comment describing a real import that had nothing to import from. `today.py` now genuinely imports `WORKING_HOURS_PER_DAY` from `meeting_load.py` (aliased to the same external name so `negotiation_trigger_support.py`/`retry_queue_drainer.py`, both already-reviewed CRITICAL-tier files that import it from `today.py`, needed zero changes) -- the comment is now a true statement, not a coincidence of two files agreeing on the same number.

**A standard fresh-context review (same model family, isolated worktree -- no cross-model requirement applies to this pure-computation module) ran before merge, per this project's own baseline discipline. No BLOCKER/HIGH found. One real MEDIUM and two LOW, all fixed rather than merely disclosed, since they were cheap and this function is about to become a real, canonical reference implementation for a future Dart port:**

1. **MEDIUM -- an out-of-range `buffer_fraction`/`overload_threshold` could flag a genuinely EMPTY day as overloaded.** A `buffer_fraction` above `1.0` (reserving more than the whole day) produced a real, negative `buffer_adjusted_availability_hours`; a negative `overload_threshold` made `committed_hours=0.0` compare greater than a negative number -- both contradicted this function's own stated invariant ("zero real commitments is never an overload"). Not reachable today (both parameters are always the fixed, real spec constants; there is no real caller at all yet), but cheap to close now rather than rediscover later. **Fixed:** `buffer_fraction` clamped to a real, valid `[0.0, 1.0]`, `overload_threshold` to `>= 0.0`.
2. **LOW -- a genuine `NaN` input was silently absorbed into a meaningless result, never surfaced.** Confirmed directly before trusting it: `max(0.0, float('nan'))` returns `0.0` in real Python, while `max(float('nan'), 0.0)` returns `nan` -- silently argument-order-dependent, not a real guarantee to clamp on top of blindly. A NaN `committed_hours` silently became "zero commitments, not overloaded"; a NaN `working_hours_per_day` silently produced a NaN result whose own `is_overloaded` comparison is always `False` (a potentially-overloaded day silently reporting as fine). **Fixed:** an explicit `math.isnan()` check on every parameter, raising a real, loud `ValueError` rather than ever silently producing a meaningless result.
3. **LOW -- the test suite didn't cover the two scenarios above.** **Fixed:** 5 new regression tests added.

**Tests:** 16 in `test_meeting_load.py` (11 original + 5 from the review fixes), all pure, all passing.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. 129 tests across every file touched by the `today.py` refactor (and its real dependents) passing unchanged, plus the 16 `test_meeting_load.py` tests.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the real on-device `CalendarProvider` integration and the Dart port of `compute_meeting_load()` -- substantial, separate mobile work. Real `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` execution -- a separate, Rule-5/6-gated cloud slice. A concrete `CalendarAdapter` implementation for `gate/validators.py`'s own already-built `availability_check`/`temporal_fact_check` -- has the same real, unresolved gap `SEND_EMAIL` execution disclosed (`DEC-142`): no real caller anywhere in this backend ever constructs a Gate proposal carrying calendar context, since no "propose an action" flow exists yet outside negotiation-choice. The `calendar_events` table decision the plan asks for ("likely only a thin record of externally-booked events, not a full mirror") -- not yet built; deferred to whichever future session actually implements `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL`, since that's the first real, concrete need for it.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/meeting_load.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/today.py`, `backend/tests/test_meeting_load.py` (new), this log.

## DEC-145: real Honesty Log -- the permanently-dead "Log" tab closes

**Standard tier** -- real, per-user database queries, zero external calls, zero Gate/security/external-action code touched.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with NEXT"), redirected from Phase 5 to Phase 6.** Both of Phase 5's own remaining pieces (real on-device `CalendarProvider` integration, real `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` execution) would have added more real, tested backend capability with zero real user-visible effect -- the exact drift pattern `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md` itself exists to stop recurring. `honesty_log.py` is a Phase 6 item the plan's own text calls "arguably the single most visible fix in this entire plan" -- a permanent bottom-nav tab, dead for every real user, every time, with its real mobile screen already built and waiting (Batch 8, `DEC-087`). Picked over Phase 5's remaining pieces for that reason, disclosed rather than silently following the numbered phase order.

**What was built:** `features/honesty_log.py` (new) -- `build_honesty_feed()` (pure grouping) and `fetch_honesty_feed()` (real, per-user-scoped live query), backing a new, real `GET /honesty_log` (`QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.13). Confirmed directly before writing a query: the real, live `action_events` table already carries exactly the closed-vocabulary `outcome` CHECK constraint this needed (`'approved_unchanged' | 'caught_by_gate' | 'corrected_by_user' | 'uncertain_no_data'`, migration `0001`) -- no new column, no new table. `retry_queue_drainer.py::map_verdict_to_outcome()` is the one real, live producer today, and only ever writes the first two; `corrected_by_user`/`uncertain_no_data` are real, valid, closed-schema values with no real producer yet, the same disclosed "schema ready, no real caller yet" shape `SEND_EMAIL` execution already carries (`DEC-142`). `success_rate` is real, deliberately nullable (never a real `0.0` standing in for "nothing to compute from"), mirroring `trust_digest.py`'s own already-established precedent exactly: `uncertain_no_data` rows are excluded from both `total` and the success-rate denominator, since counting "we don't know" as an attempt that merely didn't succeed would collapse two genuinely different real states into one.

**A real, new design decision this module makes, disclosed rather than silently invented:** `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.13 names a real `description` field in its own JSON example but never specifies how to construct one -- `action_events` has no dedicated description column, only `action_type`/`payload`. `_describe_action()` is a real, honest, human-readable rendering built directly from those two real, already-stored fields, with an open, defensive fallback for any `ActionType` this module doesn't have a specific sentence for yet.

**A real, related gap found while reading this module's own real precedent, disclosed here rather than silently fixed (out of this session's own scope):** `main.py`'s real `GET /trust_digest` route still carries a comment claiming "`action_events` itself has no `user_id` column" -- true when that route was first built, but `action_events.user_id` has existed since migration `0004` (`DEC-119`), predating this comment's own claim. `fetch_trust_digest()` still aggregates every real user's `action_events` together with no real per-user filter at all -- a real, live, currently-deployed cross-user aggregation gap in a route that's been live since `DEC-100`, not just a stale comment. `honesty_log.py` does NOT repeat this mistake (real, per-user scoped from its first line) but the `/trust_digest` gap itself is left exactly as found -- a separate, real, disclosed open item for a future session.

**A standard fresh-context review ran before merge (same model family -- no cross-model requirement applies to this non-Gate, non-security, non-external-action module). No BLOCKER/HIGH. Two real LOW findings, one fixed, one disclosed as intentional:**

1. **LOW, fixed -- the "most-recent-first ordering" live-DB test proved less than it claimed.** An earlier version put exactly one row in each bucket, so the assertion passed trivially regardless of whether ordering was preserved WITHIN a bucket containing 2+ rows of the same outcome -- the underlying code was already correct, only the test's own coverage was thin. **Fixed:** a third real row added, giving `successes` two real entries at different real timestamps to genuinely prove within-bucket ordering.
2. **LOW, disclosed, not fixed -- `_describe_action()`'s generic fallback produces cosmetically awkward text** for the 6 real `ActionType`s it has no specific sentence for yet (e.g. `"Create calendar event external"`). Never raises, matches this module's own disclosed, intentional design -- a real UX nit for whichever future session builds real execution for those types, not a defect in this one.

The review also independently re-derived the `uncertain_no_data` exclusion formula against `trust_digest.py`'s own real query and found no divergence; traced cross-user isolation end to end through the real SQL and both the unit and route-level tests; confirmed a still-unresolved `escalate_to_human` row (`outcome IS NULL`) is genuinely excluded from every bucket; and confirmed `_describe_action()` never raises on a missing payload key or an unrecognized `ActionType`.

**Tests:** 13 in `test_honesty_log.py` (pure grouping + real, live per-user DB tests, one strengthened per the review's own finding) + 4 new route-level tests in `test_main.py` (auth, live round-trip, cross-user isolation, 503) + 9 in `honesty_log_api_test.dart` (the mobile fetcher).

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. 104 backend tests across `test_honesty_log.py`/`test_main.py` passing together. Mobile: `flutter analyze` clean, full `flutter test` suite clean (353/353). Wiring `fetchHonestyFeed` into `main.dart` genuinely unlocks the already-built Honesty Log screen (`_HonestyLogTab` in `main_shell.dart`, gated on a null check that's now satisfied) for the first time since it was written years ahead of any real backend to call.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** the `/trust_digest` cross-user aggregation gap disclosed above. `corrected_by_user`/`uncertain_no_data` have no real producer yet -- a human-correction endpoint and a Stage-A `no_data_found` Finding propagating to the action-event level are both real, separate, future work.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/honesty_log.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_honesty_log.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py`, `mobile/lib/api/honesty_log_api.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/main.dart`, `mobile/test/honesty_log_api_test.dart` (new), this log.

## DEC-146: real Gate Reveal -- the Gate's own findings/objections are finally persisted

**CRITICAL tier** -- touches `retry_queue_drainer.py` (already CRITICAL-tier reviewed, `DEC-127`/`DEC-128`/`DEC-142`) and adds real, live persistence to the Gate's own verdict pipeline for the first time. Rule 6 applies.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with NEXT" / "continue with other remaining work"), Phase 6 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Closes the real, disclosed gap `DEC-126` found: `gate.review()`'s own real `GateVerdict.findings`/`.objections` have always been computed, but never persisted anywhere -- used only to decide the verdict, then silently discarded. `mobile/lib/features/gate_reveal/` has had real, tested logic and a real screen since Batch 6 (`DEC-080`), and `main_shell.dart`'s own `_TodayTab` already wires a real tap-through from a "Needs you now" card to it -- with no real backend behind either until now.

**What was built:** `migrations/0013_gate_reveal/` (new, applied live) -- two new, nullable `findings`/`objections` JSONB columns directly on `action_events`, no new table, no duplicated identity. `retry_queue_drainer.py::_persist_verdict()` -- the ONE real, live producer of `action_events` rows in this entire backend (confirmed by direct search before touching anything) -- now writes `verdict.findings`/`verdict.objections` (via Pydantic's own `.model_dump()`) onto the same row it already creates. `features/gate_reveal.py` (new) -- `fetch_gate_reveal()`, a real, per-user-scoped lookup by `proposal_id`, returning `None` honestly (never confirming another user's row exists) when it doesn't resolve. `GET /gate_reveal/{proposal_id}` (new), mirroring `GET /negotiations/{negotiation_id}`'s own already-established auth/404 pattern exactly.

**A real, honest limit of what this real path can ever produce, confirmed directly rather than assumed:** `run_stage_b()`'s own real logic means only genuine `Stakes.S3` proposals ever produce real, Critic-authored objections (S2 is Judge-only, S1/S0 never reach Stage B at all) -- and none of the three real negotiation-eligible domains this drainer can translate (`finance`/`tasks`/`calendar-local`, per `Position.domain`'s own schema constraint) ever exceeds S2. `objections` will therefore always be a real, honest empty list through this one real production path today, never a fabricated one -- disclosed directly in this session's own tests (a dedicated, direct unit test of `_persist_verdict()` proves the write mechanism handles a genuinely non-empty `objections` list correctly, since no real integration path through `drain_due_jobs()` can exercise that case itself).

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree) ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6, and found 1 BLOCKER + 1 HIGH + 3 MEDIUM + 6 LOW -- all fixed before merge:**

- **BLOCKER:** the review noticed the exact consequence the paragraph above states but never traced through: since a real S2 verdict can genuinely reach the Judge and come back with an honestly empty `objections` list, the mobile screen's own pre-existing `stageBRan(objections) => objections.isNotEmpty` check (a real, load-bearing contract from Batch 6, `DEC-080`) would misreport a genuinely-reviewed S2 action as "Stage B never ran," hiding the one review layer that actually produced the escalation the user is being asked about. Fixed by adding `stakes` to `GateRevealBundle` end-to-end (backend query -> route -> Dart fetcher -> screen) and a new `stageBRanForStakes(stakes)` check in `gate_reveal_logic.dart` that reads the Gate's own structural stakes value instead of inferring it from list emptiness. The original `stageBRan(objections)` is kept, unchanged and still correctly tested, for any future caller that already knows it's looking at an S3 verdict specifically.
- **HIGH:** `_persist_verdict()`'s `json.dumps([x.model_dump() for x in ...])` uses Pydantic's default python-mode dump, which returns a live `datetime` object for `EvidenceRef.retrieved_at` -- `json.dumps` cannot serialize that, so any Stage A check that ever populates `Finding.source_ref` (`availability_check`/`temporal_fact_check`, both already real and dormant for a real, unrelated reason) would permanently stall that job's retries. Fixed with `.model_dump(mode="json")` on both calls; a real regression test in `test_retry_queue_drainer.py` now round-trips a `Finding`/`Objection` carrying a real `EvidenceRef`.
- **MEDIUM (x3):** (1) `fetch_gate_reveal()` was collapsing a real `NULL` findings/objections column (a pre-migration-`0013` row) into a fabricated `[]` -- the same "no_data_found rendered as a pass" mistake this project's `evidence_state` discipline exists to rule out. Fixed: the backend now returns real `None`, threaded through as nullable all the way to `GateRevealScreen`, which renders an explicit "Not recorded -- this action predates Gate Reveal" notice instead of a silently empty section. (2) This very log entry asserted, in the commit under review, that the review had already run and passed -- corrected here to record what it actually found. (3) `STATUS_INDEX.md`'s "Product Reality" line claimed the tap-through was reachable without disclosing that no "Needs you now" card has ever existed on the one live account to tap -- corrected to state that plainly.
- **LOW (x6, all cheap, all applied):** a test docstring's stakes claim was imprecise (`finance` can also be S2 via `UPDATE_BUDGET`, not only S1); the `_persist_verdict()` regression test now exercises a real `EvidenceRef`-bearing objection instead of only `evidence_ref=None`; a function-local import was moved to the test module's top-level imports; the mobile fetcher now URL-encodes `proposalId`; two cosmetic notes (a frozen dataclass with mutable list fields; a point-in-time test count in a historical log entry) were left as noted, not code changes.

**Tests:** 6 in `test_gate_reveal.py` (real, live per-user lookup, cross-user isolation, null-column honesty preserved as `None` not `[]`, a dedicated S2-with-empty-objections-still-reports-stakes case) + 2 new/extended in `test_retry_queue_drainer.py` (the existing single-domain-job test extended to assert real findings/objections persistence for the S1 case; the direct `_persist_verdict()` unit test now also proving a real `EvidenceRef`-bearing objection round-trips through `mode="json"` without a `TypeError`) + 5 new route-level tests in `test_main.py` (live round-trip now asserting `stakes` too, cross-user 404, invalid-UUID 404, auth, 503) + 15 in `gate_reveal_api_test.dart` (the mobile fetcher, including the real `evidence_state`-to-visual-state mapping, URL-encoding, and the null-vs-empty-list distinction) + 4 new in `gate_reveal_logic_test.dart` (`stageBRanForStakes` across all four real stakes values).

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. Migration `0013` applied live to the real, deployed Supabase database, schema-verified directly. The full backend suite across every DEC-146-touched file (and `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own full existing suite) passing together -- see `STATUS_INDEX.md` for the current, live count rather than restating one here. Mobile: `flutter analyze` clean, full `flutter test` suite clean.

**Deployed to production, unlike `DEC-144`'s `meeting_load.py`** -- this genuinely changes user-visible behavior (the Gate Reveal tap-through's own correctness), so it's shipped rather than left merged-but-undeployed, per the `DEC-134` lesson about keeping production in sync with what actually matters to a user. Revision `quorum-backend-00018-xh2`, image tag `dec146-20260827`. Live-verified: `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`; `GET /gate_reveal/{uuid}` with no `Authorization` header → real `401`.

**Affects:** `backend/migrations/0013_gate_reveal/` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/gate_reveal.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/retry_queue_drainer.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_gate_reveal.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_retry_queue_drainer.py`, `backend/tests/test_main.py`, `mobile/lib/api/gate_reveal_api.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/gate_reveal/gate_reveal_logic.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/gate_reveal/gate_reveal_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/shell/main_shell.dart`, `mobile/lib/main.dart`, `mobile/test/gate_reveal_api_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/gate_reveal_logic_test.dart`, `mobile/test/main_shell_navigation_test.dart`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`.

Matches `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md` Phase 6's own explicit tier assessment for this exact module.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with phase 6"), Phase 6 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Picked over `UPDATE_BUDGET` execution and `predictive_risk.py` using the same "visible impact over dormant capability" reasoning this session's own `DEC-145`/`DEC-146` picks already used: `career_digest.py` closes a real, dead mobile screen (`mobile/lib/features/career_digest/`, real and tested since Batch 7's `DEC-084`, `you_screen.dart`'s own `_CareerDigestLoader` already wiring a real tap-through from Career Pipeline) that has had zero backend to call for its entire existence, while `predictive_risk.py` would have added more real-but-invisible backend capability, and `UPDATE_BUDGET` execution improves an already-reachable flow rather than unlocking a new one.

**A real, deliberate scope boundary, disclosed rather than silently narrowed:** `agents/career_agent.py`'s own real LangGraph (`is_interview_detected`/`search_findings` arriving as already-resolved state from Email's classification pipeline, per `IMPL_17`) is NOT wired to a real caller by this session -- confirmed directly, no real interview-detection classifier exists anywhere in `email_ingestion.py`, and `IMPL_17`'s own text already discloses that pipeline as separate, out-of-scope work. Building it would mean fabricating an unspecified detector's shape from nothing (Rule 3). Instead, `features/career_digest.py`'s own real, autonomous trigger uses the one real, already-exercised signal that already exists for "this application deserves research": `applications.status = 'interview_scheduled'` (`QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` §5.10's own real, live status value) -- the same "close the gap with what's real, don't invent a new one" principle `negotiation_detail_backfill.py`'s own top-of-file docstring already applied (`DEC-134`).

**What was built:** `migrations/0014_career_digest/` (new, applied live) -- `applications.digest` (nullable JSONB, no companion table) plus `digest_attempts`/`digest_last_attempted_at`, mirroring `negotiations`' own `detail_backfill_attempts`/`detail_backfill_last_attempted_at` (migration `0009`) exactly, applying `DEC-135`'s CRITICAL-tier-review-learned lesson (bound a durably-failing candidate, round-robin the rest) from the start rather than relearning it. `features/career_digest.py` (new) -- real, live Tavily search (`search_company()`, shape confirmed live before writing a single line against it, the same discipline this project holds every external API to), a real Gemini structured-JSON summarization call (`make_gemini_compile_digest_call()`, matching `agents/career_agent.py`'s own pre-existing `CompileDigestCall` type signature exactly -- the real implementation that type has been waiting for since `IMPL_17`), real, per-user-scoped `fetch_company_digest()`, and `run_career_digest()` -- a real, quota-conscious batch job (`DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE = 1`, `MAX_DIGEST_ATTEMPTS = 5`) mirroring `negotiation_detail_backfill.py`'s own established shape. New `GET /career_pipeline/{application_id}/digest` (mirroring `GET /gate_reveal/{proposal_id}`'s per-user auth/404 pattern -- "not found" and "not compiled yet" deliberately share the identical 404, matching `career_digest_logic.dart`'s own already-tested contract) and `POST /internal/career-digest` (mirroring `/internal/backfill-negotiation-detail`'s 503-when-unconfigured pattern). `backend/scripts/enable_career_digest_cron.sql` (new, written but **NOT yet scheduled live** -- this job would draw on the same real, disclosed, fluctuating Gemini free-tier quota (`STATUS_INDEX.md` item #21) `/internal/backfill-negotiation-detail` **already, actively draws on live** -- confirmed directly against the real `cron.job` table (`jobname='backfill-negotiation-detail', active=true`) before writing this entry, not assumed from that job's own script's stale header comment, which incorrectly still said "not yet scheduled" and is corrected in the same commit as this entry. Scheduling `career-digest` too would mean two real, autonomous consumers on one already-active quota, not one competing against an idle slot -- left for Preethish's own confirmation before enabling. `source_count` is always code-computed (`len(search_findings)`), never asked of or trusted to the model -- the same "structure and IDs are code's job" principle `negotiation/gemini_calls.py`'s own `_DO_NOTHING_OPTION_ID` already established, applied to a new field.

**Mobile:** `mobile/lib/api/career_digest_api.dart` (new) -- the one real, deliberate contrast with every other fetcher in this codebase: a real `404` here means the honest, expected "not researched yet" state, so it's thrown as the pre-existing, specifically-catchable `DigestNotYetAvailableException` (`career_digest_logic.dart`, real since Batch 7), never wrapped in the generic `ApiException` every other 404 in this app uses. ## DEC-148: real `UPDATE_BUDGET` execution -- the last real, safely-executable action type in Phase 6's own list

**CRITICAL tier** -- touches `action_executor.py` (already CRITICAL-tier reviewed, `DEC-128`/`DEC-142`), the file carrying the Gate's own real S3/Gmail-execution backstop, plus a real schema migration against the live database. Rule 6 applies.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead implement the remaining work in phase 6"), Phase 6 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Closes the real, disclosed gap `action_executor.py`'s own docstring named since `DEC-128`: "`UPDATE_BUDGET` has no real execution target: no `budgets`-ceiling table exists anywhere in this schema."

**The real gap went deeper than one missing column, found before writing any code:** `TODAY_MONTHLY_BUDGET_LIMIT` (`today.py`) was a hardcoded, module-level `50000.0` constant, read directly by FIVE real call sites across the finance/negotiation pipeline (`today.py::fetch_today_budget`, `negotiation_trigger_support.py::fetch_remaining_monthly_budget` -- shared by `spend_alert.py`, `deadline_watch.py`'s own separate inline duplicate of the identical subtraction, and `negotiation_detail_backfill.py`'s `_build_deadline_watch_state`/`_build_spend_alert_state`/`_build_baseline`). Adding a real, writable per-user column and executing `UPDATE_BUDGET` against it would have been a real, live no-op observable nowhere if any of these five sites kept reading the old hardcoded constant instead -- so this session closes all five, not just the write side.

**What was built:** `migrations/0015_user_budget_limit/` (new, applied live) -- `users.monthly_budget_limit DOUBLE PRECISION NOT NULL DEFAULT 50000.0`, a metadata-only `ALTER TABLE` on Postgres 11+, matching the existing hardcoded value exactly so no existing real user's computed behavior changes until an explicit `UPDATE_BUDGET` execution (or a future settings screen) changes it. `today.py::fetch_monthly_budget_limit()` (new) -- the one real, shared per-user lookup every other real site now calls through. `action_executor.py`'s new `UPDATE_BUDGET` branch: `payload["amount"]` is read as the real NEW ceiling itself, never a delta. **A real, disclosed correction (this PR's own CRITICAL-tier review, HIGH H1):** this entry originally claimed that reading was "confirmed directly" against `agents/finance_agent.py`/`negotiation/downstream_translation.py`'s own existing contracts -- the review found neither contract actually said so; `finance_agent.py`'s own `amount` field carries no docstring distinguishing it from `log_expense`'s identical field, and the real translation prompt only said "extract a real, positive amount," ambiguous between a new total and a stated change. Per Rule 4, an under-determined assumption is a discrepancy to report, not one to record as confirmed -- fixed at the actual source: `downstream_translation.py`'s own real prompt now states explicitly that `update_budget`'s `amount` is the new total ceiling, computed from context if the description only names a change, never the change itself. A real, structural safety check, independent of that prompt fix: a non-finite (`NaN`/`inf`), non-positive, or implausibly large real ceiling is rejected before any write -- not merely "implausible," since `UPDATE_BUDGET` is real `Stakes.S2` and can reach this branch carrying a Judge-authored `revised_payload` with no schema guarantee (BLOCKER B1, see below). `payload["category"]` is genuinely not persisted, the identical disclosed pattern `LOG_EXPENSE` already established for its own unused field.

**Read-site migration, all five closed:** `today.py::fetch_today_budget`, `negotiation_trigger_support.py::fetch_remaining_monthly_budget` (fixing `spend_alert.py` automatically), `deadline_watch.py`'s own inline duplicate of the same computation, and `negotiation_detail_backfill.py`'s three functions -- `_build_deadline_watch_state`/`_build_spend_alert_state` now return a fourth tuple element (the real per-user limit), threaded into `_build_baseline`'s own new required `monthly_budget_limit` parameter, since that function has no `conn`/`user_id` of its own to look it up.

**A CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus, isolated worktree) ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6, and found 1 BLOCKER + 2 HIGH + 3 MEDIUM + 3 LOW -- all fixed before merge:**

- **BLOCKER (B1):** the original non-positive-value guard (`new_limit <= 0`) let `float('nan')`/`float('inf')` through -- both real, live Python values satisfy `x <= 0 == False`. Fixed with `math.isfinite()` plus a new `_MAX_BUDGET_LIMIT` upper bound (reusing `retry_queue_drainer.py::_MAX_FINANCE_AMOUNT`'s own real value, not re-derived). **A real, hand-verified refinement found while writing this fix's own regression test:** Postgres's `jsonb` type genuinely rejects the literal `NaN`/`Infinity` JSON tokens Python's `json.dumps` would otherwise emit (`InvalidTextRepresentationError: Token "NaN" is invalid`, confirmed live against the real Supabase database) -- so a `NaN`/`inf` value in a Judge's `revised_payload` never actually reaches `action_executor.py` through today's one real path; `_persist_verdict()`'s own `INSERT INTO action_events` fails first, and the job genuinely retries. `math.isfinite()` is still real, necessary defense in depth (a different future caller might not route through that same jsonb insert), but `_MAX_BUDGET_LIMIT` is what actually matters for this specific real path today: a finite-but-implausibly-large amount encodes as valid JSON and sails past Postgres's own parser untouched. Both halves are now proven by dedicated tests.
- **HIGH (H1):** this entry originally claimed the new-ceiling-not-a-delta reading was "confirmed directly" against `finance_agent.py`/`downstream_translation.py`'s existing contracts -- neither actually said so (an under-determined assumption, a Rule 4 discrepancy, not a confirmed fact). Fixed at the source: `downstream_translation.py`'s own real prompt now states explicitly that `update_budget`'s `amount` is the new total ceiling, never a change amount.
- **HIGH (H2):** `UPDATE users SET ... WHERE user_id = $2` silently matches zero rows for a nonexistent `user_id` and returns a real Postgres status tag (`"UPDATE 0"`) that was being discarded -- the branch would have reported a real, honest-looking `executed=True` for a write that never happened. Fixed: the real status tag is now checked, and anything other than `"UPDATE 1"` raises, mapped to the same real `executed=False` every other malformed-payload case gets.
- **MEDIUM (M1, four stale claims found and corrected):** `action_executor.py`'s own docstring still said "five" executable types and asserted an S0/S1-only payload-validity guarantee that `UPDATE_BUDGET` (S2) doesn't share; `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own docstring still said no `budgets`-ceiling table exists and used that as the reason `budget_check` was out of Stage A's scope (a real, pure-code `budget_check` is now honestly buildable for the first time -- logged as a new, disclosed open item, not built here); this log's `STATUS_INDEX.md` companion line had deleted the one sentence that would have flagged B1. All four corrected.
- **MEDIUM (M2):** `fetch_monthly_budget_limit()` silently substituted the migration default for a nonexistent `user_id`, making a genuinely missing user indistinguishable from one honestly on the default -- combined with H2, a coherent-looking lie was possible. Fixed: raises `MonthlyBudgetLimitUserNotFoundError`, matching `deadline_watch.py`/`spend_alert.py`'s own established "raise loud, let per-item isolation catch it" discipline exactly.
- **MEDIUM (M3):** the new column had no database-level constraint at all -- the app-layer check was its only defense. Fixed: migration `0016` (new, applied live) adds `CHECK (monthly_budget_limit > 0 AND monthly_budget_limit < 'Infinity'::double precision)` -- live-verified before writing it that Postgres genuinely sorts `NaN` as greater than every value including `Infinity` itself, so the compound form (not a bare `> 0`) is what actually rejects `NaN` too.
- **LOW (L1):** the first version of the `deadline_watch.py` fix issued a redundant duplicate spend query, and `_build_spend_alert_state` issued three queries where two suffice -- both also carried a real, undisclosed split-snapshot risk (the claim and the available-capacity figure could disagree if a real expense committed between two separate statements). Fixed: a new, shared `negotiation_trigger_support.py::fetch_budget_snapshot()` returns `(limit, spent, remaining)` from one consistent read, used by all three real call sites.
- **LOW (L2):** a test docstring and this log both said a capstone test was "approved by the real Judge" -- it uses an injected, deterministic fake, same as every other test in that file. Reworded in both places.
- **LOW (L3):** missing regression tests for the above, all added -- see Tests below.

**Tests:** 8 new in `test_action_executor.py` (real write, real overwrite-on-second-call, non-positive/non-finite/above-max/non-numeric/boolean rejections, a real nonexistent-`user_id` case) + updated exhaustiveness test (6 genuinely executable types now, not 5, live guard reduced from 5 to 4 remaining) + 1 new in `test_today_feature.py` (a real, non-default per-user limit genuinely changes `fetch_today_budget`'s own live result) + 1 new in `test_spend_alert.py` (the real, end-to-end proof: the identical real spend/task data that creates a genuine conflict at the real ₹50,000 default produces `NO_CONFLICT` once this exact user's own real ceiling is raised) + 3 new in `test_retry_queue_drainer.py` (the capstone -- a chosen finance negotiation option translated to `update_budget`, approved by an injected, deterministic fake Judge, genuinely writes the new ceiling through the full `drain_due_jobs()` pipeline; a real, implausibly-large Judge-revised amount still rejected; a real NaN Judge-revised amount proven to fail the real JSON insert instead, not silently write or execute).

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. Migrations `0015`/`0016` both applied live to the real, deployed Supabase database, schema-verified directly (including a live check that zero existing rows violated the new `0016` constraint before adding it). Full backend suite run twice across this session's own review-fix pass: the only failures across both runs were the same, already-disclosed, fluctuating Gemini `429` quota exhaustion and one real asyncpg pool-connection-management artifact (`InternalClientError: PoolConnectionHolder.release() called on a free connection holder`) -- both confirmed transient by isolated retries passing cleanly, neither touching this session's own changed files. See `STATUS_INDEX.md` for the current, live count rather than restating one here.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** a real, pure-code Stage A `budget_check` validator is now honestly buildable for the first time (M1's own finding) -- not built here, logged as its own new, disclosed open item for a future session.

**Deployed to production** -- this genuinely changes real, live behavior (the "Budget remaining this month" number on Today, and every autonomous trigger job's own conflict math, now reflect a real per-user ceiling instead of one hardcoded global number), so it's shipped rather than left merged-but-undeployed, per the `DEC-134` lesson about keeping production in sync. Revision `quorum-backend-00020-w98`, image tag `dec148-20260828`. Live-verified: `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`.

**Affects:** `backend/migrations/0015_user_budget_limit/` (new), `backend/migrations/0016_user_budget_limit_check/` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/action_executor.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/today.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/deadline_watch.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/negotiation_trigger_support.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/negotiation_detail_backfill.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/retry_queue_drainer.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/negotiation/downstream_translation.py`, `backend/scripts/enable_backfill_negotiation_detail_cron.sql` (stale-comment correction, unrelated finding), `backend/tests/test_action_executor.py`, `backend/tests/test_today_feature.py`, `backend/tests/test_spend_alert.py`, `backend/tests/test_retry_queue_drainer.py`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`, `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.

## DEC-149: real Predictive Risk -- the one Phase 6 module with no prior spec contract at all

**Standard tier** -- new, self-contained feature module (pure Postgres reads of `tasks.deadline`/`tasks.status`, pure code arithmetic), no touch to Gate verification logic, secrets handling, or a real external-action path.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead implement the remaining work in phase 6"), Phase 6 of `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.** Explicitly flagged before building, not silently built: unlike every other Phase 6 module this session (Gate Reveal, Honesty Log, Career Digest), Predictive Risk had no real API route contract, JSON response shape, or mobile screen anywhere in this project's real spec corpus -- confirmed by direct search, `QUORUM_DATA_CONTRACTS.md` names only `HistoricalWeek`/`RiskAssessment` as bare type labels in a module-mapping table. Building it as pure backend would have meant real, tested, but permanently invisible capability, the exact drift pattern this whole plan exists to stop. **Preethish's own explicit choice, asked directly rather than assumed:** build the real backend AND a real, minimal mobile surface for it, not backend-only.

**Two real design decisions this session had to make, grounded in existing schema, disclosed rather than presented as pre-specified:** "deadline density" for a real week is the real COUNT of this user's own `tasks.deadline` values falling in that Monday-to-Sunday UTC week -- no new schema. "Correction," since this schema has never tracked "was this task's deadline ever pushed" as its own concept (`UPDATE_TASK` has no real execution path either), is a real, grounded proxy from existing columns: a real task counts as corrected if it's `status = 'cancelled'`, or `status = 'open'` with a `deadline` that's already passed. Parameters reused directly from `QUORUM_CONFIGURATION_CONSTANTS.md` §4, not re-derived: `DEADLINE_DENSITY_TOLERANCE = 1`, `CORRECTION_RATE_THRESHOLD = 0.5`. "Upcoming week" deliberately means NEXT calendar week, not the current one (already partway resolved, too late to act on) -- matching the ADD's own "flagged before the collision happens, not after" framing.

**What was built:** `features/predictive_risk.py` (new) -- `compute_historical_weeks()`/`assess_upcoming_week()` (pure, real grouping/pooling math) + `fetch_risk_assessment()` (real, live, per-user query). Every real historical week within tolerance of the assessed density is POOLED before comparing against the threshold -- never a single week judged alone, which would be a real, noisy signal at low task volumes. A genuinely new user, or a density with no real matching history, gets `pooled_correction_rate: None`/`is_at_risk: False` -- an honest "not enough real history," matching `honesty_log.py`'s own established `success_rate: float | None` precedent, never a fabricated risk or reassurance. New `GET /predictive_risk` (real, per-user auth, no ID param -- a flat per-caller route like `/tasks`/`/today`).

**Mobile:** a real, deliberately minimal surface, not a new tab -- `predictive_risk_api.dart` wired as `fetchPredictiveRisk`, rendered as a small banner ABOVE the real task list on the Tasks screen (reached via Today's own pre-existing "Holding steady → View tasks" link). A slow or failed risk fetch degrades gracefully to no banner at all, never blocking the one thing that screen already reliably does (showing real tasks) -- its own, separate `FutureBuilder`, not a shared one with the task list. `riskMessage()` is a real, honest three-state message (no data / at risk / manageable), never collapsing "not enough history" into either a false reassurance or a false alarm. A real, previously-completely-untested drill-through ("Holding steady → View tasks") got its first real test as part of building this.

**A standard-tier fresh-context review ran against this PR before merge, per Rule 6's own standing discipline, and found 2 MEDIUM + 2 LOW, both MEDIUMs fixed before merge:**

- **MEDIUM:** "deadline density" was defined inconsistently between the two halves of the one comparison this whole feature depends on -- `compute_historical_weeks()` counted every real task with a deadline in a past week regardless of final status, while the upcoming week's own count only included currently-`open` tasks. A real, concrete failure this created: a user with 5 tasks due next week, 3 marked done/cancelled early, would report density 2 and get pooled against historical weeks that had 2 total tasks scheduled, not weeks that had 5 -- silently suppressing a real risk flag for what was actually a high-density week. Fixed: both counts now include every real task with a deadline in the relevant week, regardless of status -- "deadline density" honestly means "how many real deadlines fall in this week," matching the ADD's own plain description, which never mentioned filtering by status at all.
- **MEDIUM:** every existing test's own fixture happened to use equal `total_count` per matching historical week, so a version of `assess_upcoming_week()` that (incorrectly) averaged per-week rates instead of pooling by real task count would have passed unnoticed -- the load-bearing "pooled, never averaged" design decision was genuinely unverified by the test suite as written. Fixed: a new test uses deliberately unequal real task counts per week (5/10 and 11/11) to mechanically prove pooling (≈0.762) differs from naive averaging (0.75).
- **LOW (both left as-is, disclosed rather than chased):** the new mobile logic file inherited a stale "no Flutter SDK exists" boilerplate disclaimer from older sibling files -- genuinely false as of `DEC-103`, corrected in this one new file rather than propagated further. `_PredictiveRiskBanner` re-invokes its fetcher on every rebuild, matching this codebase's own pre-existing `_TasksLoader` convention exactly (not a regression, and the review confirmed real-world impact is minor since the widget lives on a pushed route, not a tab body).

**Tests:** 21 in `test_predictive_risk.py` (pure-logic grouping/pooling/boundary math + real, live end-to-end database tests, including a real risk-flag proof, cross-user isolation, a history-window boundary case, the pooling-vs-averaging regression above, and the corrected density-consistency test) + 4 new route tests in `test_main.py` (live round-trip against a real, honest no-data response, cross-user isolation, auth, pool-unavailable 503) + 4 in `predictive_risk_logic_test.dart` + 9 in `predictive_risk_api_test.dart` + 1 new in `main_shell_navigation_test.dart` (the real, end-to-end "View tasks" drill-through, now tested for the first time in this project's history, including the real risk banner).

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. `flutter analyze` clean, full `flutter test` suite clean. See `STATUS_INDEX.md` for the current, live count rather than restating one here.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved by this entry:** this feature's own real trigger is purely on-demand (a user opening the Tasks screen) -- no autonomous job surfaces a real risk proactively (e.g., via Today's "Needs you now" or a push notification); that would be a real, separate, larger scope decision, not silently assumed here.

**Deployed to production** -- this genuinely unlocks a new, real, user-visible surface (the Predictive Risk banner), so it's shipped rather than left merged-but-undeployed, per the `DEC-134` lesson. Revision `quorum-backend-00021-nbh`, image tag `dec149-20260828`. Live-verified: `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`; `GET /predictive_risk` with no `Authorization` header → real `401`.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/predictive_risk.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_predictive_risk.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_main.py`, `mobile/lib/api/predictive_risk_api.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/predictive_risk/predictive_risk_logic.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/tasks/tasks_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/today_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/shell/main_shell.dart`, `mobile/lib/main.dart`, `mobile/test/predictive_risk_logic_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/predictive_risk_api_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/main_shell_navigation_test.dart`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`, `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`.

## DEC-150: real per-user scoping for `/trust_digest` -- a real, disclosed, live cross-user aggregation gap, closed

**Standard tier** -- a real correctness/data-isolation fix to an existing route, reusing the already-established `_resolve_internal_user_id_or_404` pattern; no new Gate logic, secrets handling, or external-action path.

**Authorized directly ("what do you want from me to continue implementation"), picked as the immediate next item ahead of the chosen Phase 5 direction.** Closes a real, live, currently-deployed bug found while building `DEC-145`'s Honesty Log and deliberately left open at the time (disclosed, not silently fixed, since it was out of that session's own scope): `features/trust_digest.py::aggregate_weekly_summary()`/`fetch_trust_digest()` had NO real `user_id` filter at all, despite `action_events.user_id` existing since migration `0004`/`DEC-119` -- the real, live, deployed `GET /trust_digest` genuinely aggregated every real user's data together, and had done so since the route was first built (`DEC-100`), through every real, live deploy since.

**What was built:** `aggregate_weekly_summary(pool, week_start, *, user_id)` and `fetch_trust_digest(pool, *, user_id, today=None)` now both require a real, resolved `user_id`, with the real SQL adding `WHERE user_id = $1`. `main.py`'s `/trust_digest` route now calls `_resolve_internal_user_id_or_404` (the same real, established pattern every other per-user-scoped route in this backend already uses) and passes the result through -- closing the route's own stale docstring claim in the same edit (it still said "`action_events` itself has no `user_id` column," true when written, false since `DEC-119`, never corrected until now). `features/honesty_log.py`'s own real, disclosed note about this exact gap (written at `DEC-145`) is updated to record it as resolved rather than left as a live, open item.

**Tests:** 1 new in `test_trust_digest.py` (`test_aggregate_weekly_summary_never_counts_another_real_users_rows`, proving the fix at the module level) + the 4 existing live-database tests updated to insert every row under one real, consistent `user_id` and pass it through (previously each row got its own random, unused `user_id`, since nothing ever filtered by it) + 1 new in `test_main.py` (`test_trust_digest_endpoint_never_counts_another_real_users_action_events`, the same property proven at the route level) + the existing route-level "real, live, not mocked" test updated to use `_provisioned_auth_header()` instead of the deliberately-unprovisioned `_auth_header()`, since a real per-user query now genuinely needs a real, resolved identity to succeed.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. See `STATUS_INDEX.md` for the current, live count rather than restating one here.

**Deployed to production** -- this closes a real, live, currently-deployed cross-user data-isolation bug, so it's shipped rather than left merged-but-undeployed, per the `DEC-134` lesson. Revision `quorum-backend-00023-8sm`, image tag `dec150-20260828`. Live-verified: `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`.
