## DEC-151: real `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` execution -- Phase 5's real, narrow cloud slice

**CRITICAL tier** -- touches `action_executor.py`, a real S3 external-irreversible-action path, per `CLAUDE.md` Rule 6, the same tier `DEC-142`'s original Gmail execution work carried.

**Direction confirmed directly by Preethish** (`AskUserQuestion`, "Phase 5: real Calendar (Recommended)") as the next real implementation focus after Phase 6 closed out. Scoped deliberately narrow, matching `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`'s own Phase 5 language: Calendar's real ground truth belongs on-device (the already-built-but-dormant `mobile/lib/features/calendar_sync.dart`), so this session builds only the one piece that genuinely needs a server-side Google API call -- inviting a real external attendee -- and deliberately defers the on-device `device_calendar` wiring and the server-side `CalendarAdapter` bridge question until a real device is reconnected (both real, open, disclosed items, not abandoned).

**Real API shapes confirmed live before writing any code** (Rule: never assume a spec's API shape, verify it), against the real sandbox account (`quorum.dev.sandbox@gmail.com`): `POST https://www.googleapis.com/calendar/v3/calendars/primary/events` with `{"summary", "start": {"dateTime"}, "end": {"dateTime"}, "attendees": [{"email"}]}` returns a real `200 {"id", "htmlLink", "status": "confirmed", "attendees": [...]}`; `DELETE .../events/{event_id}` returns a real `204`. A real test event was created (sandbox account as its own invitee, never a real uninvolved third party, per Rule 5) and then really deleted, confirming the full create-verify-delete cycle works before any implementation code existed.

**A real, necessary schema gap found and closed first:** `agents/calendar_agent.py::build_event_proposal()` never carried a real external attendee email at all -- `has_external_invitee` was a bare boolean with nothing real to invite. `invitee_email` is now a real, required parameter whenever `has_external_invitee=True`, raising `ValueError` loud (never fabricated, never guessed) when missing -- the same "never invent what wasn't genuinely provided" discipline `negotiation/downstream_translation.py` already applies to this exact domain.

**What was built:** a new `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` branch in `action_executor.py`, mirroring `_real_gmail_post`'s established three-valued-outcome pattern (`_real_google_calendar_post`: a transport failure is genuinely `None`/unknown, a non-200 is a definite `False`, a real `200` is a definite `True` plus a durable `logger.warning()`). Payload fields (`title`/`invitee_email`/`start`/`end`) are checked for real, sane types and parseable ISO datetimes before any network call -- the same defensive posture `UPDATE_BUDGET` established at `DEC-148` for a Judge-revised S3/S2 payload that may not match the pre-Gate-validated shape. No header- or path-injection guard was added for these fields (unlike `SEND_EMAIL`'s MIME headers or the Gmail message/label ids in a URL path) because none of them are ever used anywhere but a JSON request body here -- Google's own real API is the correct source of truth for whether they're WELL-FORMED, though (see the review findings below) that is a narrower guarantee than the original version of this entry implied. The existing, structural S3 human-approval backstop (`DEC-142`) already covered this action type with zero new code, exactly as designed.

**A real, disclosed caller gap, matching `SEND_EMAIL`'s own precedent exactly:** `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s calendar domain translation always sets `has_external_invitee=False` (a negotiation option's free text never names a real external attendee's email, and guessing one would be fabrication), so no real code path in this backend can produce a `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL` proposal today. Real execution capability now exists and is live-verified; no real caller exists yet. Disclosed, not hidden -- the same honest gap this project already lived with for `SEND_EMAIL` between `DEC-142` and today.

**CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus), no BLOCKER, 1 HIGH + 4 MEDIUM found and fixed before merge:**
- **H1, the review's most consequential finding:** Google's real Calendar API genuinely does not email a real event's real attendees unless `sendUpdates` is explicitly set on the request -- the original version of this branch omitted it, meaning it would have silently BOOKED an event while never actually INVITING anyone, the opposite of this session's own stated purpose. Fixed: `?sendUpdates=all` added to the real POST URL, live-verified against the real sandbox account (a real invite email query param confirmed present on the real outgoing request).
- **M2:** no `end > start` check existed in this branch, despite `retry_queue_drainer.py`'s own sibling pre-Gate validator already enforcing it for this exact domain -- a Judge-revised `revised_payload` could bypass that upstream check entirely, the identical shape as `DEC-148`'s own BLOCKER finding for `UPDATE_BUDGET`. Fixed with a real, independent check plus a new regression test.
- **M3:** a real test bug in the live capstone -- `start.replace(hour=(start.hour + 1) % 24)` never advances the real calendar date, so a test run starting in the 23:00 UTC hour would have produced an `end` genuinely BEFORE `start`, exactly the shape M2's new check now correctly refuses. Fixed with a real `timedelta(hours=1)`.
- **M1:** the function's own safety-property docstring miscounted its branch groupings after this session's edit (claimed "three of seven" then "remaining three" while double-counting one type), and separately implied the S3 human-approval backstop answers the payload-TRUST question for `SEND_EMAIL`, which it does not -- that backstop is about human APPROVAL of the decision to act, never about the PROVENANCE of the payload itself. Both real guarantees are needed and neither substitutes for the other. Docstring corrected to state the real grouping precisely (four payload-trusted branches: `CREATE_TASK`/`LOG_EXPENSE`/`ARCHIVE_EMAIL`/`LABEL_EMAIL`; three payload-untrusted: `UPDATE_BUDGET`/`SEND_EMAIL`/`CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL`) and to state the backstop's real scope honestly.
- **M4:** the original docstring's "no injection vector" claim was true but incomplete -- it implied `invitee_email`/`title` need no scrutiny at all, when in fact a Judge-authored `revised_payload` could carry a syntactically valid but attacker-chosen recipient and an attacker-influenced subject line, which this branch would genuinely send as a real Google Calendar invitation from the real user's own account. What actually holds the line against that is the S3 human-approval backstop, not a format check -- the docstring now discloses this explicitly rather than implying the field's free-form nature is itself safe.
- **3 LOW, also fixed:** an unbounded offending `start`/`end` string could amplify unbounded into a real `ValueError` message (now truncated via a new `_truncate_for_error()` helper); the live capstone parsed `real_event_id` only after two assertions, so a real event could leak into the sandbox calendar if either failed (now parsed immediately after the call, before any assertion); a real, timezone-NAIVE ISO string parsed without error but produced a genuinely ambiguous real Google Calendar event with no `timeZone` sibling (now rejected -- `agents/calendar_agent.py`'s own real payload always originates from a tz-aware datetime, so a naive one reaching this branch is never legitimate).
- 2 new regression tests added (`end` before `start`; a timezone-naive datetime), the fake-client success test now asserts the real `sendUpdates=all` query param, and the live capstone test re-ran clean against the real sandbox account after every fix (85/85 across the three affected test files, zero skips on the live capstone).

**Tests:** `test_calendar_agent.py` -- one existing test updated for the new required parameter, one new fail-loud-without-email test, one new graph-invocation-with-a-real-invitee test (10 total, all passing). `test_action_executor.py` -- 11 new fake-client unit tests (9 original plus the 2 review-driven regression tests above) plus one real, live capstone test (`test_execute_approved_action_create_calendar_event_external_a_real_genuine_booking_via_google_calendar`) that creates a real event via `execute_approved_action`, verifies it with a real follow-up `GET` against Google's live API, and really deletes it as cleanup -- confirmed to run (not skip) against the real sandbox account, all passing.

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean. Full backend suite (pre-review-fixes): 684 passed, 7 failed + 5 errors, all confirmed pre-existing Gemini-429-quota flakiness and its cascading pool-exhaustion fallout, none touching this session's files. See `STATUS_INDEX.md` for the current, live count rather than restating one here.

**Deployed to production** -- real, live-verified execution capability, even without a real caller yet (the same "ship the real capability, disclose the caller gap honestly" pattern `SEND_EMAIL` already established at `DEC-142`), so it's shipped rather than left merged-but-undeployed, per the `DEC-134` lesson. Revision `quorum-backend-00024-mqm`, image tag `dec151-20260828`. Live-verified: `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/agents/calendar_agent.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/action_executor.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/retry_queue_drainer.py`, `backend/tests/test_calendar_agent.py`, `backend/tests/test_action_executor.py`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`.

## DEC-152: real on-device Calendar sync wiring -- Phase 5's larger, deferred piece, closed for the mobile-side half

**Standard tier** -- entirely mobile, on-device, zero backend/Gate/secrets/external-action touch. Confirmed directly before starting: `retry_queue_drainer.py` (the one real Stage A caller in this backend) never invokes `availability_check`/`temporal_fact_check`, so no real caller needs a `CalendarAdapter` implementation today either -- see the explicit non-build decision below.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead") after a whole-system checkpoint reconnected the real device.** Closes the larger, deferred half of Phase 5 that DEC-151 (the narrow cloud slice, `CREATE_CALENDAR_EVENT_EXTERNAL`) explicitly left open: `mobile/lib/features/calendar_sync.dart` was real and tested since Batch 5 but had never been referenced anywhere else in this app -- a genuinely dead module, confirmed by grep before this session began.

**Three real, necessary gaps found and closed before any of this could work on a real device, none of them assumed -- each verified directly:**
1. `CalendarSync.syncNearTermEvents()` never called `hasPermissions()`/`requestPermissions()` at all before `retrieveCalendars()` -- confirmed against the real `device_calendar-4.3.3` package source. Fixed, with a real, easy-to-get-wrong subtlety also caught and guarded against: `Result<bool>.isSuccess` is genuinely `true` even when `data` (the real permission boolean) is `false` -- `isSuccess` only means "the platform call itself succeeded," never "permission was granted." A naive `if (result.isSuccess)` check would have silently treated a denied permission as granted.
2. `AndroidManifest.xml` declared neither `READ_CALENDAR` nor `WRITE_CALENDAR` -- confirmed directly against the real, installed manifest. Without both, `requestPermissions()` cannot succeed on a real device regardless of the Dart-side fix above (confirmed against the plugin's own real Kotlin source: both permissions are requested together in one native call). Added both, plus a real `NSCalendarsUsageDescription` to `ios/Runner/Info.plist` for correctness -- genuinely untested on a real iOS device, since none exists in this project's environment.
3. `QuorumDatabase()` (the real, production Drift constructor, distinct from `.forTesting()`) had never been instantiated anywhere in this app at all, confirmed by direct search -- `CalendarMirror` and its sibling mirror tables have existed since Batch 5 with no real caller ever opening the database. Now owned by `main.dart`'s own `_QuorumAppState`, closed in `dispose()`, matching the existing `_httpClient` resource-ownership pattern exactly.

**A real, disclosed non-build decision, not silent scope-narrowing:** the server-side `gate/validators.py::CalendarAdapter` Protocol (`find_event`/`list_events_in_range`, needed by `availability_check`/`temporal_fact_check`) stays genuinely unimplemented. Building a real bridge would mean pushing some or all of a user's real on-device calendar contents to the cloud -- a genuinely new category of data leaving the device, and a real privacy decision, not a mechanical wiring task. Since no real caller needs it today (confirmed above) and it wasn't part of what this session was asked to build, it stays a logged, disclosed open item per `CLAUDE.md` Rule 3, not spec-invented architecture. If ever built, the production completion plan's own narrower option (a thin, real record of only Quorum-created external events, not a full mirror) is the right starting point, not a full calendar upload.

**What was built:** a new `CalendarSyncResult.permissionGranted` field (a genuine third real outcome -- "we were never allowed to look" must never be confused with "genuinely zero real events," the same discipline the Gate's own `Finding.evidence_state` already holds itself to, applied here to a mobile-side outcome instead). A new `features/calendar/calendar_logic.dart` (pure, zero-Flutter-dependency display logic -- `sortByStartTime`, `formatEventDayLabel`, `formatEventTimeRange`, hand-rolled with no `intl` dependency, matching this codebase's own established convention) and `calendar_screen.dart` (a real, minimal screen, honest empty/permission-denied states, the first fully local, no-network screen in this app's history). Wired through `you_screen.dart`'s "More" section (a new `_CalendarLoader`, running a real sync THEN a real mirror read, so a transient sync failure never blanks out events a prior successful sync already stored) -> `main_shell.dart` -> `main.dart`, where the real `QuorumDatabase`/`CalendarSync` instances are finally constructed.

**A real, disclosed process note:** the first Android manifest edit this session failed the real Gradle build (`ManifestMerger2$MergeFailureException`) -- an XML comment containing a literal `--` inside its body, which XML's own grammar forbids (only the opening `<!--`/closing `-->` may contain it). Caught immediately by the real build, not shipped; fixed by rewriting the comment without double hyphens. Worth remembering for any future manifest comment in this codebase, where `--` is otherwise this project's own standard prose convention.

**Tests:** `calendar_sync_test.dart` -- 3 new tests for the real permission-handling logic, using a real, hand-built `_FakeCalendarPlugin` (extends `DeviceCalendarPlugin`, calling its own `@visibleForTesting` `.private()` constructor -- no mocking library exists anywhere in this project, matching the backend's own `_FakePostClient` convention). New `calendar_logic_test.dart` -- 10 tests, including a real, hand-verified weekday check (independently computed in Python before finalizing the test, matching `calendar_sync_test.dart`'s own established discipline for its range-filter test). `main_shell_navigation_test.dart` extended with a new You -> Calendar drill-through test. `flutter analyze`: clean. `flutter test`: 409/409 passing.

**A real, disclosed process correction found by this session's own changes:** `calendar_sync_test.dart` was previously a genuine zero-Flutter-dependency file, run via plain `dart test` (named as such in `pubspec.yaml`'s own dev_dependencies comment). It no longer is -- `device_calendar` (needed for this session's own `_FakeCalendarPlugin`) transitively imports `package:flutter/material.dart`, which the standalone Dart SDK genuinely cannot compile (confirmed live: `dart test` fails to even load the file). `flutter test` is the real, confirmed-working command for it now; both the file's own header and `pubspec.yaml`'s comment are corrected in the same session rather than left silently wrong.

**NOT completed the same session; genuinely witnessed the next real session, once the device reconnected:** the real, on-device verification (granting calendar permission for the first time on the real physical device, confirming a real synced event actually renders) did not happen the same session -- the device (reconnected earlier that session for the whole-system checkpoint) genuinely disconnected again before that specific build could be installed, confirmed directly (`adb devices` returned empty after a full `adb kill-server`/`start-server` cycle, not a daemon glitch). A fresh debug APK was built and left ready.

**RESOLVED the next real session, `2026-08-29`:** the device reconnected, a fresh debug APK was rebuilt from the merged `main` (including the `Info.plist` review fix) and installed. Tapping the You tab's new "Calendar" entry for the first time triggered a genuine, real Android system permission dialog ("Allow quorum_mobile to access your calendar?") -- direct, live proof that both real fixes actually work together on a real device: the manifest declaration (without it, Android would never show this dialog at all) and the Dart-side `requestPermissions()` call. Tapping "Allow" (real bounds resolved via `uiautomator dump`, not guessed) triggered a real sync and a real read-back, rendering six real, genuine events from the device's own calendar provider ("Janmashtami"/"Janmashtami (Smarta)", real Hindu festival calendar entries synced from this device's real calendar accounts) -- `formatEventTimeRange` and `formatEventDayLabel` both confirmed correct against real, non-fixture data (`Fri, Sep 4` independently re-verified against Python's own `datetime.date(2026, 9, 4).strftime('%A')`, genuinely a Friday). This is the first real, on-device confirmation of this entire feature, not just a passing test suite -- the concrete gap this entry's own prior paragraph disclosed is now closed.

**Standard-tier fresh-context review: no BLOCKER/HIGH, 1 MEDIUM + 1 LOW found, MEDIUM fixed before merge.** The reviewer independently ran `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` in an isolated worktree and confirmed both claims above directly, not by trusting the commit message. **MEDIUM, genuinely funny in hindsight:** the exact same XML-comment-forbids-`--` bug this entry's own "real, disclosed process note" above describes finding and fixing in `AndroidManifest.xml` was reintroduced one file later, in the new `Info.plist` comment -- unnoticed because iOS has no real build environment in this project to have caught it the way the real Android Gradle build did. Fixed the same way (rewritten without a double hyphen), verified this time with a real, independent XML parse (`python3 -c "import xml.dom.minidom as m; m.parse(...)"`) rather than trusting eyes alone, and both files re-checked for any other stray `--` inside a comment body. **LOW, not fixed, matches existing file-wide convention exactly:** `_CalendarLoader.build()` calls its own combined sync+fetch future inline in `FutureBuilder`'s `future:` argument, which would re-run on any rebuild -- identical to every other loader already in `you_screen.dart` (`_FinanceLoader`, `_CareerDigestLoader`, etc.), so left as-is rather than fixing this one call site inconsistently with its siblings.

**Affects:** `mobile/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`, `mobile/ios/Runner/Info.plist`, `mobile/lib/features/calendar_sync.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/calendar/calendar_logic.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/calendar/calendar_screen.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/you/you_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/shell/main_shell.dart`, `mobile/lib/main.dart`, `mobile/pubspec.yaml`, `mobile/test/calendar_sync_test.dart`, `mobile/test/calendar_logic_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/main_shell_navigation_test.dart`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`.

## DEC-153: real Quick-capture -- the first real write path beyond negotiation-choice and account deletion

**CRITICAL tier** -- the first synchronous, user-facing path in this backend where genuinely untrusted, freshly-typed free text is sent to an LLM and that LLM's own output feeds directly into a real Gate review and a real database write, all within one real HTTP request. Structurally the same class of risk `DEC-127`/`128` (the retry-queue drainer's own first real Gate-invoking and execution-invoking paths) were reviewed at this tier for, even though every individual piece reused here had already been reviewed once.

**Authorized directly, via two successive `AskUserQuestion` forks after Phase 5 closed out:** Phase 7 (Quick-capture) over Meeting-Load Defense, then Tasks-domain-first over closing Email's own gap or building a full multi-domain classifier immediately.

**A real, disclosed scope narrowing from the production plan's own text, found by direct verification before writing a line of code:** the plan describes Quick-capture as feeding "the real domain agents' own existing natural-language construction paths (tasks_agent's NL creation, email_agent's draft-from-intent)." Checked directly against every one of the 5 agent files rather than trusted: `tasks_agent.py`/`finance_agent.py`/`calendar_agent.py` have zero LLM calls and zero NL extraction of any kind -- each takes already-structured fields. Only `email_agent.py` has a real free-text-in path (`draft_reply_node`), and it still needs a real `recipient` supplied separately (never extracted from the text), plus it has zero real callers anywhere in this backend today. "Feed free text into an existing NL path" was therefore real for at most one domain, and even that one needed new work. Tasks was chosen instead (the simplest real schema, no recipient/external party to resolve) -- this session builds the one real, new NL-extraction call that domain genuinely lacked, rather than silently assuming a path existed that didn't.

**A second real, disclosed scope narrowing, also decided before writing code:** the plan's own second bullet says to route this "through Sprint 0's on-device model... falling back to cloud otherwise." Sprint 0's own real, measured result (`DEC-130`/`131`) is 67% validity for the winning on-device candidate -- not yet strong enough to be the primary path for something that writes real data. Cloud (Gemini) extraction is used exclusively; on-device extraction/routing is a real, disclosed, deferred follow-on, not silently dropped. A third scope decision: a direct in-app text-entry floating action button, not an extension of the OS share-sheet (`share_intent_handler.dart`) -- simpler to build and verify this session; the share-intent extension remains a real, separate future entry point.

**What was built, backend:** `features/quick_capture.py` (new) -- a real, honestly-framed Gemini extraction prompt (explicit prompt-injection framing: the user's free text is DATA describing what they want, never an instruction to follow), reusing the exact same `{title, estimated_hours, deadline_iso}` schema shape `negotiation/downstream_translation.py::_TASKS_SCHEMA` already defines, but with a genuinely new prompt (that module's own prompt describes "a user chose a negotiation option," a real, factually false description of this session's own context). `capture_task_from_text()` orchestrates extract -> propose -> Gate -> persist/execute on one connection. Three real, already-tested pieces of `retry_queue_drainer.py` were made public and reused directly rather than re-derived: `validate_and_build_task_proposal()` (the real `_MAX_ESTIMATED_HOURS`/positivity bound check), `build_stage_a_checks_for_domain()` (renamed from `_build_stage_a_checks`, the real `provenance_check`/`deadline_conflict_check` construction), and `persist_gate_verdict()` (renamed from `_persist_verdict`, the real `action_events` write plus execution, including its own real `.model_dump(mode="json")` fix for a datetime-serialization bug a naive reimplementation here could easily reintroduce). `PrefetchedCommittedHoursAdapter` also made public for the same reason. A new `POST /quick_capture` route, real per-user auth, a real `503` when the extraction provider isn't configured (matching `GET /search`'s own established convention) and a real `502` (never a fabricated task) when a real extraction attempt genuinely fails.

**What was built, mobile:** `features/quick_capture/quick_capture_logic.dart` + `quick_capture_screen.dart` (new) -- a real, minimal text-entry screen matching `you_screen.dart`'s own `_SearchHost` structural convention exactly, showing the real outcome by reusing `gate_reveal_logic.dart`'s own `FindingSummary`/`EvidenceVisualState` and `gate_reveal_screen.dart`'s own `FindingRow` widget (both made public this session for this reuse) -- the same trusted Gate-transparency rendering a "Needs you now" tap-through already shows, applied here to a user's own freshly-typed action. `api/quick_capture_api.dart` (new). A new floating action button on `MainShell`'s own shared top-level `Scaffold`, visible across all four tabs (not just Today), gated behind the same optional-fetcher-hides-UI convention every other real feature in this shell already uses.

**Tests:** `test_quick_capture.py` (new, 8 tests) -- real, live-database integration tests including a genuine deadline-conflict Stage-A hard-fail (a real, already-committed task's hours plus a new claim genuinely exceeding real available capacity), a structural proof `critic_call`/`judge_call` are never invoked for `Stakes.S1` (both fakes raise `AssertionError` if called), and three malformed-extraction error paths, plus a real, live capstone extraction test against the real Gemini API (ran, not skipped). `test_main.py` +4 (401, blank-text 422, provider-not-configured 503, and a real, live, end-to-end capstone -- a real task genuinely created from real free text through the real HTTP route, confirmed via a direct `tasks` table read, then cleaned up; ran, not skipped). `quick_capture_logic_test.dart`/`quick_capture_api_test.dart` (new, mobile), `main_shell_navigation_test.dart` +2 (the real FAB→create flow; the FAB's presence confirmed identical from a non-Today tab).

**Verified live:** `ruff check backend` clean, full backend suite 709/709 passing (a fully clean run, including every previously-flaky live-Gemini test). `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 426/426 passing.

**CRITICAL-tier cross-model review (Opus): 1 BLOCKER + 2 HIGH + 5 MEDIUM + 6 LOW found, all fixed before merge.** The reviewer independently ran `ruff`/the full backend suite/`flutter analyze`/`flutter test`, and independently live-probed the real pipeline with adversarial inputs against a real database connection -- not just reading the diff.
- **B1 (BLOCKER):** `validate_and_build_task_proposal()` was missing the exact `math.isfinite()` check its finance sibling already carries (the real, disclosed `DEC-148` BLOCKER) -- a real `NaN`/`Infinity` `estimated_hours` passed both `<= 0` and `> _MAX_ESTIMATED_HOURS` (both real, live `False` for `NaN`/`inf`) and reached a real `tasks.estimated_hours NUMERIC(4,1)` write, live-confirmed to genuinely ACCEPT a stored `NaN` -- which would have permanently poisoned every future `SUM(estimated_hours)` capacity read for that user. Fixed: mirrors the finance validator exactly.
- **H1:** `title` had zero real validation -- live-confirmed 3 of 7 adversarial payloads (a non-string title, a `None` title) produced an uncaught `asyncpg` error and a real HTTP `500`, violating this module's own documented `502` contract; a real, unbounded 50,000-character title was live-confirmed to write successfully. Fixed: real type/non-empty/length (`_MAX_TASK_TITLE_LENGTH = 500`) checks added alongside the existing numeric bounds, plus a real, defense-in-depth `except asyncpg.PostgresError` in the route itself.
- **H2:** `build_extraction_prompt()` omitted the current-UTC-time anchor its own sibling (`negotiation/downstream_translation.py`) already carries -- live-confirmed Gemini genuinely, silently returns `deadline_iso: null` for an explicit "due next Friday" with nothing to resolve it against, which meant `deadline_conflict_check` silently never ran for the most natural real deadline phrasing there is. Fixed: the real time anchor added, matching the sibling exactly; the free text also moved behind an explicit boundary marker, with every instruction preceding it.
- **M1:** raw upstream Gemini error bodies (confirmed to include real quota/billing text) were reaching the end user verbatim through a real `502` response. Fixed: logged server-side only (`logger.warning`), `QuickCaptureError`'s own message is now a clean, generic fact.
- **M2:** the real Gemini extraction call ran INSIDE the route's `pool.acquire()`/`conn.transaction()` block, holding a real pooled connection idle-in-transaction for up to ~60s for a call that touches no database. Fixed: `capture_task_from_extracted_args()` split out from `capture_task_from_text()`; the real route now extracts BEFORE ever acquiring a connection.
- **M3:** no rate limiting exists anywhere in this backend, and one shared, free-tier-capped `GEMINI_API_KEY` backs this route plus `/search`/the Judge/negotiation translation/Career Digest -- live-confirmed during this very review to exhaust the real daily quota and cause real, unrelated tests to fail as collateral. Genuinely new infrastructure, out of this session's scope (Rule 3) -- logged explicitly in `quick_capture.py`'s own top-of-file docstring as a real, disclosed, future-session open item.
- **M4:** the mobile `Create` button had no in-flight guard -- a real rapid double-tap could fire two real, billed extraction calls and create two real `tasks` rows from one intent. Fixed: `_isSubmitting` disables both the button and the text field while a real request is in flight.
- **M5:** `test_quick_capture.py`'s own `user_id` fixture never cleaned up its real `users` row, unlike its established sibling fixtures -- live-confirmed to have already left 24 real, orphaned rows in the real Supabase database from this session's own test runs. Fixed, and the 24 real orphaned rows deleted by hand as part of this fix.
- **L1-L6:** a mangled test name from the earlier `sed` rename (`testpersist_gate_verdict...` missing an underscore) restored; two stale docstring references to the now-renamed `_persist_verdict` corrected; a real, fixed retry-backoff delay added to `_call_gemini_json`; the prompt-injection test strengthened from a tautological literal-containment check into a genuine ordering proof (every instruction precedes a real boundary marker, the free text follows it); a mobile-side JSON-parsing gap wrapped in the same try/catch every other malformed-response case already uses; this section itself added, since the original entry had none.

Verified again after fixes: `ruff check backend` clean; the 41 deterministic tests covering this session's own files all pass; the two live-Gemini-dependent tests hit the real, disclosed, shared daily quota (already exhausted by the review's own live probing) and were not re-verified live this pass, consistent with this project's own established, disclosed Gemini-quota-flakiness pattern; `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 426/426. Every one of B1's adversarial cases (`NaN`, `Infinity`, a dict title, a `None` title, a 50,000-character title, a whitespace-only title) was hand-re-verified directly against `validate_and_build_task_proposal()` after the fix -- all six now correctly rejected, the genuinely valid case still accepted.

**Deployed to production** -- this genuinely unlocks a new, real, user-visible write path (the Quick-capture floating action button), so it's shipped rather than left merged-but-undeployed, per the `DEC-134` lesson. Revision `quorum-backend-00025-stt`, image tag `dec153-20260829`. Live-verified: `GET /health` → `200 {"status":"ok"}`; unauthenticated `POST /quick_capture` → real `401`.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/quick_capture.py` (new), `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/retry_queue_drainer.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/action_executor.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/gate_reveal.py`, `backend/src/quorum_backend/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_quick_capture.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_retry_queue_drainer.py`, `backend/tests/test_main.py`, `mobile/lib/features/quick_capture/quick_capture_logic.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/quick_capture/quick_capture_screen.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/api/quick_capture_api.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/gate_reveal/gate_reveal_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/shell/main_shell.dart`, `mobile/lib/main.dart`, `mobile/test/quick_capture_logic_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/quick_capture_api_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/main_shell_navigation_test.dart`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`.

## DEC-154: real Meeting-Load Defense -- the genuine multi-day projection, ported to Dart where it always belonged

**Standard tier** -- entirely mobile, pure logic plus one existing screen's own UI addition, plus a docstring-only backend correction. Zero Gate/security/auth/secrets/external-action touch.

**Authorized directly ("go ahead with next") after DEC-153 closed.** Chosen over extending Quick-capture to more domains -- that would have reopened the "single domain first vs. full multi-domain classifier" fork Preethish already explicitly decided in DEC-153's own session, which this session deliberately did not reopen without checking first.

**A real, disclosed correction to this session's own initial framing, found by reading `meeting_load.py`'s existing docstring directly rather than assuming what "the fuller multi-day capacity projection" meant:** the file already explicitly says a genuine multi-day version needs real calendar ground truth, which this architecture puts ON-DEVICE ONLY, and that this exact function was "meant to be ported to Dart... so the real, live computation runs where the real committed-hours figure is actually available." A backend-side multi-day feature was never actually buildable honestly -- `predictive_risk.py`'s own real, different choice (task-deadline density, not meeting load) already worked around the identical gap for a related but distinct concept. This session's real value only became possible because `DEC-152` (this same broader work) built the real on-device `CalendarMirror` sync that never existed before.

**What was built:** `mobile/lib/features/meeting_load/meeting_load_logic.dart` (new) -- a direct, hand-verified port of the real Python `compute_meeting_load()` (identical real parameters: `workingHoursPerDay=8.0`, `bufferFraction=0.25`, `overloadThreshold=0.7`; identical real defensive clamping; a real, hand-verified account of where Dart's own NaN-comparison semantics for `math.max`/`math.min`/`.clamp()` genuinely differ from Python's, confirmed live in a real Dart script before trusting it, matching the Python file's own precedent of hand-verifying exactly this). `computeWeeklyMeetingLoad()` (new) is the real, genuine multi-day projection this was always for -- sums real event durations per real calendar day from real, already-synced `CalendarMirror` data, over a real 7-day window, with zero backend involvement at all. A new, quiet `_MeetingLoadBanner` on `calendar_screen.dart` (an honest "Next 7 days look manageable" or a specific, real list of overloaded days), matching `_PredictiveRiskBanner`'s own established restraint on the Tasks screen -- but synchronous, since the real data is already in hand, not a second async fetch.

**A real, disclosed correction found and fixed along the way, unrelated to this session's own new code:** `calendar_logic.dart`'s own docstring (written during `DEC-152`) claimed "`dart test` genuinely works for this file's own tests," reasoning only that `drift` itself has no Flutter import -- a real, confidently-stated claim that was never actually run before being written down, and is wrong: `db/database.dart` (imported for `CalendarMirrorData`) itself pulls in real Flutter plugin packages (`sqlite3_flutter_libs`/`path_provider`), confirmed live to fail loading under the standalone Dart SDK with the identical error class `calendar_sync_test.dart`'s own, already-correct header already documents. Both files' comments corrected; `meeting_load_logic.dart`/its own test file state the real, verified fact directly rather than repeating the same unverified claim.

**Tests:** `meeting_load_logic_test.dart` (new) -- 16 single-day tests directly mirroring `test_meeting_load.py`'s own real, hand-verified cases one-for-one (a real parity proof between the Python reference and this Dart port, including the exact `4.199999999999999`-not-`4.2` floating-point boundary fact), plus 5 new multi-day tests (same-day summing, cross-day isolation, outside-the-window exclusion, a custom `lookAheadDays`). `main_shell_navigation_test.dart` +2 (a real, light week reassures; a real, 6-hour single-day commitment genuinely warns) -- both computed relative to the real `DateTime.now()` at test-run time, never a fixed date that could drift into the past.

**Verified live:** `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing. `ruff check backend` clean (docstring-only change).

**Genuinely NOT witnessed this session, disclosed rather than skipped:** the real device was disconnected the entire session -- this feature has not yet been seen rendering against a real, synced calendar on a real physical device. The next real on-device pass should check it.

**Standard-tier fresh-context review: no BLOCKER/HIGH, 1 MEDIUM + 2 LOW found, all fixed.** The reviewer independently built and ran the branch in an isolated copy (not the shared checkout), reproducing `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` (449/449) and the `dart test`-fails-to-load claim directly, and independently ran a Dart script to re-verify this session's own NaN/`.clamp()` behavioral claims rather than trusting the code comments.
- **MEDIUM:** `computeWeeklyMeetingLoad()`'s `today.add(Duration(days: offset))` is not DST-safe -- Dart's own SDK documentation confirms a local-time `.add()` can shift across a real DST transition, landing on the wrong real calendar day, which `isSameCalendarDay` would then either double-count or silently drop. Fixed: real calendar-field construction (`DateTime(year, month, day + offset)`) instead, which Dart's own constructor normalizes correctly. The identical, pre-existing gap in `calendar_logic.dart::formatEventDayLabel()`'s own `tomorrow` calculation (predating this session) was fixed the same way while already in the area -- real, deployment-target India/IST observes no DST, so neither was ever reachable in practice, but both are now correct regardless.
- **LOW:** this entry's own STATUS_INDEX line overclaimed "closing item #8's last real gap" -- item #8 still genuinely lists `style_reply.py`/`mem0`/`computed_state.py` as absent, unaffected by this session. Reworded to claim only the specific sub-gap this session actually closed.
- **LOW, a real process note, not a code defect:** this is the third consecutive session (`DEC-152`/`153`/`154`) appending to `STATUS_INDEX.md`'s "Product Reality" section since the last full rewrite-checkpoint (`DEC-151`) -- `CLAUDE.md`'s own whole-system-checkpoint rule calls for one "at minimum every 2-3 sessions." One is now due before further sessions stack more bullets onto that section.

Verified again after fixes: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing.

**Affects:** `backend/src/quorum_backend/features/meeting_load.py`, `mobile/lib/features/meeting_load/meeting_load_logic.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/calendar/calendar_logic.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/calendar/calendar_screen.dart`, `mobile/test/meeting_load_logic_test.dart` (new), `mobile/test/calendar_logic_test.dart`, `mobile/test/main_shell_navigation_test.dart`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`.

## DEC-155: Phase 8 Session 1 -- real design system foundation

**Standard tier** -- entirely mobile, pure theme/visual work. Zero Gate/security/auth/secrets/external-action touch.

**Authorized directly, via an approved plan.** "Go ahead and finish remaining, then continue implementing next two phases" (Phase 8, real visual design; Phase 9, production hardening) read, correctly, as a batch instruction -- which sits in direct, real tension with `CLAUDE.md`'s own binding cadence rule ("one session, explicit approval, then the next -- not a batch run through multiple sessions unsupervised"). Per this project's own decision protocol ("when my recommendation and Preethish's stated preference genuinely conflict: implement my recommendation, with the reasoning explained plainly"), a full session-by-session breakdown for both phases was drafted via plan mode and explicitly approved before any implementation began, rather than silently batch-running either phase or silently ignoring the instruction. This entry is Session 1 of that approved breakdown.

**Real, confirmed starting point:** `quorum_theme.dart` was a single neutral seed color feeding `ColorScheme.fromSeed`, Flutter's own stock Material 3 type scale, and no formal spacing system -- `google_fonts` was not yet a dependency (confirmed directly against `pubspec.yaml` before adding it).

**What was built:** Added `google_fonts` (`^6.2.1`, resolved `6.3.3`) -- no bundled font assets, this package's own real, documented distribution mechanism. `quorum_theme.dart` gained a real, explicit `TextTheme` (deliberate sizes/weights/line-heights for every role including `display*`, not Flutter's stock scale), rendered in IBM Plex Sans via `GoogleFonts.ibmPlexSansTextTheme()` -- a real typeface choice matching the file's own existing "instrument-grade clarity" language (ADD §12.1), deliberately distinct from this category's common Inter/Space Grotesk default. A real `tertiary` accent (teal-700, `0xFF0F766E`) was added to the existing `ColorScheme.fromSeed(...)` call, confirmed directly against the installed Flutter SDK source to be a real, accepted override. `QuorumTextStyles.metric()` (new) uses IBM Plex Mono with tabular figures for the app's prominent numeric readouts (capacity hours, budget/Trust percentages) -- not yet applied to any screen, by this session's own explicit scope boundary (later Phase 8 sessions apply it). New `theme/spacing.dart` -- `QuorumSpacing`, a real 4pt-based scale (`xs=4` ... `xxl=48`), also not retrofit onto existing screens this session.

**Verified live:** `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing (no regression). One flagged uncertainty (`ThemeData.cardTheme` expecting `CardThemeData` vs. `CardTheme` across recent Flutter versions, disclosed in the file's own header comment since this environment has no real compiler to pre-confirm it) resolved clean by this session's own real `flutter analyze` run -- no rename needed.

**Standard-tier fresh-context review: 1 MEDIUM-HIGH + 1 MEDIUM + 1 LOW found, all fixed before merge.**
- **MEDIUM-HIGH:** the new `tertiary` accent collided with an existing, real semantic color use -- `needs_you_now_zone.dart` already used `colorScheme.tertiary` (previously an auto-derived harmonized tone) as its S2 medium-stakes icon color, paired with `Icons.error_outline` per the project's own "color + icon shape, never color alone" discipline. Hardcoding `tertiary` to a fixed teal reserved for "interactive emphasis / primary call-to-action" would have collided that meaning with the pre-existing S2 severity signal in the same color. Fixed: `needs_you_now_zone.dart`'s S2 case now uses `QuorumStatusColors.needsAttention` (amber) directly -- reusing the real, already-established semantic status color rather than either the newly-repurposed `tertiary` or inventing a third parallel color system. Semantically apt besides: "S2, needs your attention" is exactly what that status color already means.
- **MEDIUM:** the new type scale omitted `displayLarge/Medium/Small` entirely, silently contradicting its own doc comment that `ibmPlexSansTextTheme()` "applies IBM Plex Sans on top of these exact sizes, preserving them" -- traced directly against the installed `google_fonts` 6.3.3 source: a role left out of the base `TextTheme` resolves to a bare, unsized `TextStyle`, not Material 3's own stock display defaults. No current screen reads `textTheme.display*` (confirmed via a full grep), so nothing broke today, but it was a real, latent trap for the next screen that does. Fixed: real Material 3 baseline sizes/weights (57/64, 45/52, 36/44) added explicitly, kept rather than invented since no screen yet gives a real reason to deviate from them for display text.
- **LOW, process:** this entry itself was initially missing from the same commit as the code -- flagged by the reviewer per this project's own disclosed-log discipline, fixed by adding it here before the session is called complete.

Verified again after fixes: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing.

**Genuinely NOT witnessed this session, disclosed rather than skipped:** the real device was disconnected throughout (checked via `adb devices -l`) -- the new type scale, accent color, and metric text style have not yet been seen rendering on a real physical screen. Same disclosed gap as `DEC-153`/`154`; the next real on-device pass should check all three together.

**Affects:** `mobile/pubspec.yaml`, `mobile/pubspec.lock`, `mobile/lib/theme/quorum_theme.dart`, `mobile/lib/theme/spacing.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/today/needs_you_now_zone.dart`, this log, `STATUS_INDEX.md`.

## DEC-156: Phase 8 Session 2 -- Today + Trust screens, real componentry

**Standard tier** -- entirely mobile, pure UI componentry using `DEC-155`'s own new tokens. Zero Gate/security/auth/secrets/external-action touch.

**Authorized directly, via the same approved Phase 8/9 session breakdown `DEC-155` operates under** -- a brief check-in ("Session 1 done, moving to Session 2") rather than a new prompt, matching that plan's own stated cadence.

**What was built:** a new, shared `QuorumIconBadge` (`quorum_theme.dart`) -- a colored icon inside a soft-tinted circular container, replacing bare leading `Icon`s in three places (`NeedsYouNowZone`'s stakes icon, `InMotionZone`'s sync icon, Trust's "View weekly trend"/Missed-scenario icons) with one shared, considered piece. Deliberately preserves every existing color+icon-shape pairing exactly -- this only adds a container around the same real signal a screen already computes, never changes what it means. `HoldingSteadyZone`'s two numbers and Trust's headline catch-rate now use `QuorumTextStyles.metric()` (IBM Plex Mono, tabular figures) instead of an ad hoc `TextStyle(fontSize: 36, ...)` literal -- Trust's own "No data yet" fallback (zero self-test scenarios) deliberately keeps the normal headline style instead, since that case is a sentence, not a number. `QuorumSpacing` tokens replace the literal `16`/`24`/`12`/`8`/`4` values across `today_screen.dart` and all three Today zone files. Trust's own "View weekly trend" link and each Missed scenario are now wrapped in a `Card`, matching the card-based list pattern every other real list in this app already uses, rather than a bare `ListTile` floating in the `ListView`. Missed scenarios use `QuorumStatusColors.critical` for their icon badge -- a real, confirmed self-test failure, not an ambiguity, so `critical` (not `needsAttention`) is the correct existing status color to reuse.

**A real, disclosed scope decision made and then reverted within this same session:** `quorum_theme.dart`'s `cardTheme` was briefly changed to `margin: EdgeInsets.zero` (so a screen's own explicit `QuorumSpacing` gap would be the only source of rhythm between stacked cards), then reverted before committing once a direct grep found seven other real screens (Calendar, Tasks, Finance, Career Pipeline, Honesty Log, Waiting On, Negotiation) stacking `Card`s with no separator of their own, relying entirely on Card's own implicit default margin for spacing -- all seven are outside this session's own scope (several are later Phase 8 sessions' own job). Zeroing that margin now would have silently collided those screens' cards together, a real regression on unreviewed code introduced by a session that was supposed to touch only Today and Trust. Today's own zones instead add an explicit `QuorumSpacing.sm` gap on top of the existing default margin. A later session may zero it globally once every `Card`-using screen has its own explicit gap, not before.

**Verified live:** `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing (no regression; no test in this repository asserts on `Card`/`ListTile` widget types or hardcoded font sizes for either screen, confirmed by grep before relying on that).

**Genuinely NOT witnessed this session, disclosed rather than skipped:** the real device remained disconnected (checked via `adb devices -l`) -- the plan's own stated verification for this session ("a real on-device screenshot... confirm real capacity/budget numbers and the real Trust percentage still render correctly under the new visual system") could not be performed. This is now the fourth consecutive session (`DEC-153` through `DEC-156`) with a real, disclosed on-device verification gap stacking up -- worth closing at the next real device reconnection before it becomes a fifth.

**Affects:** `mobile/lib/theme/quorum_theme.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/today_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/today/needs_you_now_zone.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/today/in_motion_zone.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/today/holding_steady_zone.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/trust/trust_screen.dart`, this log.

## DEC-157: Phase 8 Session 3 -- Tasks + Finance + Career Pipeline screens, real componentry

**Standard tier** -- entirely mobile, pure UI componentry building on `DEC-155`/`156`'s tokens. Zero Gate/security/auth/secrets/external-action touch.

**Authorized directly, via the same approved Phase 8/9 session breakdown, brief check-in cadence ("Session 2 done, moving to Session 3").**

**What was built:** `QuorumSpacing` tokens replace literal `16`/`8` values across all three screens. A new `QuorumTextStyles.metricSmall()` (`quorum_theme.dart`) -- the same real IBM Plex Mono + tabular-figures treatment as `metric()`, sized to `titleMedium` instead of `headlineMedium`, since a full headline-sized number would overwhelm a single row in a dense list the way it doesn't for a standalone Today/Trust readout. Every list row across all three screens now uses `ListView.separated`/an explicit `SizedBox` gap plus a `QuorumIconBadge` leading icon, matching Session 2's established pattern.

**Tasks:** each row's icon SHAPE now signals `TaskStatus` (a real, closed, database-enforced set -- confirmed safe to switch on exhaustively, unlike Career Pipeline's genuinely open `applications.status`): `done` gets `QuorumStatusColors.verified` as a distinct color (a real, positive completed outcome); `open`/`cancelled` share a neutral tone, distinguished only by icon shape, since neither is inherently good or bad. The status `Chip` is now tinted to match. **A real, disclosed fix found while applying the new status-color system properly, not a pure reskin:** `_PredictiveRiskBanner` previously collapsed three real, distinct states -- genuinely no historical data yet, a real predicted busy week, a real all-clear -- into only two visual treatments, with "not enough data" rendering identically to "all clear." This is exactly the `no_data_found`-collapsed-into-a-pass mistake `CLAUDE.md`'s own `evidence_state` discipline exists to prevent elsewhere in this project; there was no reason this screen should be the standing exception. `riskMessage()`'s own three-way logic is untouched -- only which icon/color the UI selects around the same real message changed. The Card's own background no longer flips to `errorContainer` on risk -- status is now signaled through the badge alone, matching every other real screen's own "status via icon+badge, card stays neutral" pattern rather than being the one exception.

**Standard-tier fresh-context review: 2 MEDIUM found (both on the same banner), fixed before merge.** The reviewer independently re-ran `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` in an isolated worktree (449/449 confirmed) and independently cross-checked `QuorumStatusColors`' real, established usage convention across every other file that uses it, rather than trusting this session's own inline justification.
- **MEDIUM:** the banner's initial mapping used `QuorumStatusColors.uncertain` for "a real predicted busy week" -- backwards from this codebase's own real, established convention. Confirmed directly: `gate_reveal_screen.dart`'s `EvidenceVisualState.negative` and `negotiation_screen.dart`'s `MetricVisualDirection.worsens` both already map to `critical` for a real negative/failure outcome; `uncertain` is reserved for a genuinely NEUTRAL, unchanged state (`MetricVisualDirection.unchanged`), not a real warning. The initial mapping meant this banner's single most actionable state (a real, quantified predicted overload) read as LESS alarming than "we don't know yet" -- backwards from what a predictive-risk warning should communicate. Fixed: `isAtRisk` now maps to `critical`, matching the codebase's own two other real precedents for exactly this kind of outcome.
- **MEDIUM, compounding the first:** removing the Card-level `errorContainer` background (in favor of the icon badge alone) was flagged as a real, disclosed reduction in visual weight for the banner's most severe state, on top of the wrong color choice. The `critical` fix substantially addresses this in practice (a strong, established red reads clearly even at the badge's own small scale) -- the badge-only presentation itself is kept, deliberately, to match every other real screen's own "status via icon+badge, card stays neutral" pattern rather than reintroducing the one screen that flips its whole card background, which was never how any other status signal in this app is shown.

Verified again after fixes: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing.

**Finance:** a neutral `Icons.autorenew` badge on every row (no per-subscription status exists to signal); the trailing amount uses `QuorumTextStyles.metricSmall()` -- tabular figures keep a column of real currency amounts visually aligned, the exact numeric-readout case that style was built for.

**Career Pipeline:** a neutral `Icons.business_center` badge on every row, deliberately identical regardless of status -- `applications.status` is a genuinely open vocabulary (confirmed directly, again, against `career_pipeline_logic.dart`'s own header: only two of four `knownStatusOrder` values are real anywhere in this codebase today), so inventing a color per status now would mean guessing a meaning for values that don't exist yet -- exactly the unrequested architecture `CLAUDE.md` Rule 3 exists to prevent. Status sections also gained an explicit bottom gap between them, previously absent.

**Verified live:** `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing (no regression; confirmed no test in this repository asserts on `Chip`/`Card`/`ListTile` widget types, icon identity, or color values for any of the three screens before relying on that -- only text content, which is unchanged).

**Genuinely NOT witnessed this session, disclosed rather than skipped:** the real device remained disconnected (checked via `adb devices -l`) -- this is now the fifth consecutive session (`DEC-153` through `DEC-157`) with a real, disclosed on-device verification gap. Worth closing before Phase 8 closes out.

**Affects:** `mobile/lib/theme/quorum_theme.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/tasks/tasks_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/finance/finance_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/career/career_pipeline_screen.dart`, this log.

## DEC-158: Phase 8 Session 4 -- Calendar + Quick-capture + Search + Honesty Log + You screens, plus this app's first real motion

**Standard tier** -- entirely mobile, pure UI componentry + animation, building on `DEC-155`-`157`'s tokens. Zero Gate/security/auth/secrets/external-action touch (motion wraps existing, already-reviewed logic; no new business logic added).

**Authorized directly, via the same approved Phase 8/9 session breakdown, brief check-in cadence ("Session 3 done, moving to Session 4").**

**What was built, componentry:** `QuorumSpacing`/`QuorumIconBadge` applied to Calendar, Quick-capture, Search, Honesty Log, and You (its main list plus the Search host). Two real, disclosed inconsistencies found and fixed along the way, not just reskinned:
- Quick-capture's result banner used a raw `Colors.green` literal for a successful capture and no color at all otherwise -- the one real status signal in this app never wired to `QuorumStatusColors`. Now `verified`/`needsAttention` (a non-executed Gate decision here is an honest, informational outcome, not a confirmed failure -- matching `describeQuickCaptureOutcome()`'s own existing uniform treatment of all three non-executed decisions).
- Calendar's Meeting-Load banner flipped its whole Card background to `errorContainer` when overloaded -- the same pattern `DEC-157`'s own review already corrected on the Tasks screen's Predictive Risk banner. Now a `QuorumIconBadge` in `QuorumStatusColors.critical`, card stays neutral, consistent with every other real status signal in this app.

**A real, deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight:** Waiting On, Memory Transparency, and Career Digest (all real, You-tab drill-throughs) are untouched this session -- confirmed directly against both this session's own plan and the master `QUORUM_PRODUCTION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`'s own original Phase 8 text, neither of which ever names these three screens across any of Phase 8's five sessions. They still render correctly (the global theme -- fonts, colors, `cardTheme` -- already applies everywhere automatically), just without the bespoke icon-badge/spacing-token componentry pass every other screen received.

**Honesty Log's own real, deliberate exception to this app's established color pattern:** every row's icon differs by SHAPE only (`approved_unchanged`/`caught_by_gate`/`corrected_by_user`/unrecognized), never by color -- every badge uses the identical neutral tone, section to section. This is a stricter reading of "identical prominence" than most other screens (which use `QuorumStatusColors`' real severity colors freely), deliberately: this screen's own header already made an explicit, reasoned case against a `TabBar` splitting successes from failures, specifically because even a visually-symmetric split reads as unequal prominence; a differential status color (green for success, red for a catch) would reintroduce the identical asymmetry via a different mechanism. Shape alone conveys the real distinction without it.

**What was built, real motion -- this app's first of any kind (confirmed by a full grep across `mobile/lib` before this session: no `AnimatedContainer`/`AnimatedOpacity`/`AnimationController`/`TweenAnimationBuilder` existed anywhere before it).** New `theme/motion.dart` (`QuorumMotion`) -- two real, deliberately short durations (`reveal` 350ms, `transition` 250ms) and a `resolve()` helper that honors a real, live `MediaQuery.disableAnimations` reduced-motion request with `Duration.zero`, since Flutter's own implicit animation widgets don't honor that setting on their own. The plan's own three explicitly named real motion targets, all built:
1. **The Gate verdict reveal.** `gate_reveal_screen.dart`'s own header previously claimed the Stage A -> Stage B "staged reveal" was "literally implemented, not just described" -- checked directly and found only half true: Stage B's PRESENCE was real and conditional, but its APPEARANCE was never staged in TIME, rendering in the same frame as Stage A whenever it existed. This session adds the real, timed piece: Stage B, when it ran, now enters a deliberate `QuorumMotion.reveal` beat after Stage A, fading and sliding into place via `TweenAnimationBuilder` rather than snapping in.
2. **A negotiation choice being submitted.** `main_shell.dart`'s `_NegotiationLoaderState` now wraps its choosing-vs-accepted body in a real `AnimatedSwitcher`, cross-fading into the real, honest "Choice accepted" terminal state instead of an instant swap.
3. **Today's capacity/budget numbers updating.** `holding_steady_zone.dart`'s `_ComputedNumberRow` now wraps its metric `Text` in an `AnimatedSwitcher` keyed by the value text itself -- a real change (e.g. "8.0h" -> "7.5h") cross-fades; a rebuild carrying the identical value never re-triggers the transition, since the key is unchanged.

**A real, disclosed correction to `holding_steady_zone.dart`'s own header, found while already in the file for the motion work:** it still hardcoded "36px, weight 600" describing the metric numerals -- accurate only before `DEC-156` replaced that literal `TextStyle` with `QuorumTextStyles.metric()`. Restated generally rather than re-pinning a size this file no longer controls.

**A real bug this session's own `flutter test` run caught, not a hypothetical:** the Gate Reveal motion's first implementation called `QuorumMotion.resolve()` (which reads `MediaQuery.of(context)`) from `initState()` -- a real, live `FlutterError`, since Flutter's own element lifecycle forbids establishing a new inherited-widget dependency before the first `didChangeDependencies()` call. Confirmed live: the exact real test exercising this screen (`main_shell_navigation_test.dart`'s "tapping a Needs You Now action opens the real Gate reveal") failed with this real stack trace on the first run. Fixed by moving the one-time reveal-scheduling logic into `didChangeDependencies()` (guarded by a `_scheduledReveal` flag, since Flutter can call that method more than once) -- the standard, correct pattern for this exact case, not a workaround.

**Verified live:** `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 449/449 passing (including the real fix above, and confirming `pumpAndSettle()` correctly waits through the new `Future.delayed`-based reveal timers and animations without hanging or timing out).

**Standard-tier fresh-context review: 2 LOW found (1 fixed, 1 left as a defensible nit), no BLOCKER/HIGH/MEDIUM.** The reviewer independently ran `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` (449/449 confirmed), isolated and re-ran the exact test the commit message named as fixing the real `MediaQuery`-in-`initState()` bug (confirmed it genuinely exercises the Stage B path, not trivially passing on an S0/S1 action), and confirmed via `git grep` on `main` that no `Animated*`/`AnimationController`/`TweenAnimationBuilder` existed anywhere in this app before this session -- the "first real motion" claim is accurate, not asserted without checking.
- **LOW, fixed:** no test asserted the actual TIMED staging of Stage B -- that it's genuinely absent immediately after Stage A appears and genuinely present only once `QuorumMotion.reveal` has elapsed. A logic inversion (Stage B showing immediately, or never showing at all) would still have passed every existing test. Fixed: a new test taps into the real Gate reveal, pumps twice (deliberately not `pumpAndSettle()`, stopping well short of the real 350ms delay) to assert Stage B's genuine, real absence, then advances exactly `QuorumMotion.reveal` and asserts its genuine, real appearance.
- **LOW, left as-is, a defensible nit:** Quick-capture's `false -> QuorumStatusColors.needsAttention` mapping is a plausible but debatable color choice against that color's own strict doc comment ("genuine ambiguity, no_data_found") -- `uncertain` ("real but not urgent") arguably fits this case's own stated rationale slightly better. Not fixed: the reviewer's own check found this exact `Icons.info_outline` + `needsAttention` pairing already used, unchanged, for `gate_reveal_screen.dart`'s pre-existing "Not recorded" placeholder -- consistent with real, established practice already in this codebase, not a new inconsistency this session introduced.

Verified again after the fix: `flutter analyze` clean, `flutter test` 450/450 passing.

**Genuinely NOT witnessed this session, disclosed rather than skipped:** the real device remained disconnected (checked via `adb devices -l`) -- this is now the SIXTH consecutive session (`DEC-153` through `DEC-158`) with a real, disclosed on-device verification gap, and the first to include real, timed animation behavior that a passing test suite can confirm doesn't hang or crash but cannot confirm actually looks and feels right. This should be the top priority for the next real device reconnection, ahead of starting Session 5.

**Affects:** `mobile/lib/theme/motion.dart` (new), `mobile/lib/features/gate_reveal/gate_reveal_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/negotiation/negotiation_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/shell/main_shell.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/today/holding_steady_zone.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/calendar/calendar_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/quick_capture/quick_capture_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/honesty_log/honesty_log_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/search/search_screen.dart`, `mobile/lib/features/you/you_screen.dart`, this log.
